### Promulgate

The Environment and Natural Resources Code, which the National Assembly adopted on May 30, 2023, during its 9th session of the 6th legislature, and which the Senate reviewed and fully finalized in its form and legal aspects on June 13, 2023, during its 10th plenary session of the 4th legislature, and which has the following full content:

# Environment and Natural Resources Code

## Book 1
## General Provisions

### Chapter 1
### Objective and Scope

#### Article 1 .- Objective

This Code defines all rules to be respected and practiced with the objective of modernizing, harmonizing, and improving the management of environmental protection, conservation, and restoration of natural resources, biodiversity, and ecosystem functions for sustainable living and development in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 2 .- Scope

This Code has the scope of application over:

A- All activities related to the environment, natural resources, and heritage within the Kingdom of Cambodia.

B- All activities of persons operating both inside and outside the Kingdom of Cambodia that cause impact or influence on the environment, natural resources, and heritage of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 3 .- Glossary

Key terms used in this Code are defined in the glossary, which is an annex to this Code.

### Chapter 2
### Implementation of Roles and Duties of Relevant Ministries and Institutions in the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector and the Preparation of Draft Policies Related to the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector

#### Section 1
#### Implementation of Roles and Duties of Relevant Ministries and Institutions in the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector

#### Article 4 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in Charge of the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector

The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector has the competence to implement roles and duties in accordance with the provisions of this Code, laws, and legal instruments in each sector within its competence.

#### Article 5 .- Roles and Duties of Other Ministries and Institutions in Charge of Sectors Related to Natural Resources

Other ministries and institutions in charge of sectors related to natural resources have the authority to implement roles and duties in accordance with this Code, laws, and legal instruments in each sector within their competence.

#### Article 6 .- Roles and Duties of Sub-National Administrations

1- Sub-national administrations have the competence to implement roles and duties in accordance with this Code, laws, and existing legal instruments.

2- The Royal Government, the ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector, and other relevant ministries and institutions may transfer or delegate functions performed in their respective sectors to sub-national administrations.

#### Section 2
#### Preparation of Draft Policies and Action Plans Related to the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector

#### Article 7 .- Preparation of Draft Policies and Action Plans Related to the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector of the Ministry in Charge of the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector

The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector shall prepare draft policies and action plans related to the environmental and natural resources sector to increase the effectiveness of environmental protection and the management and conservation of natural resources, in accordance with the policies of the Kingdom of Cambodia and international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

#### Article 8 .- Preparation of Draft Policies and Action Plans Related to the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector of Other Ministries and Institutions

Other ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations may prepare draft policies and action plans related to the environmental and natural resources sector in their respective sectors within their competence to increase the effectiveness of environmental protection and the management and conservation of natural resources, in accordance with the policies of the Kingdom of Cambodia and international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

### Chapter 3
### General Principles

#### Article 9 .- Principles of Sustainable Development

All economic and social development activities must meet the development needs and environmental needs of current and future generations with equity and sustainability, in which environmental protection and management and conservation of natural resources are necessary parts of the development process and cannot be separated from each other.

#### Article 10 .- Principle of Avoidance of Damage to the Environment and Natural Resources

Persons shall not perform any activity that causes or may cause damage to the environment and natural resources. In cases where it is unavoidable, that person shall take appropriate measures for environmental and natural resources protection and shall prevent or reduce that damage to the minimum level.

#### Article 11 .- Principle of Public Participation

Persons who may be directly or indirectly affected by any decision related to the environment and natural resources have the right to participate in providing timely comments after receiving information through the participation process from relevant parties with transparency and accountability before that decision is made.

#### Article 12 .- Principle of Access to Environmental and Natural Resources Information

1- Persons have the right to receive information related to the environment and natural resources.

2- All information regarding environmental protection and natural resources management shall be widely disseminated to the public to create more opportunities for public participation in planning and decision-making that pose impacts on society and the environment.

3- The right to receive information related to the environment and natural resources shall be in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 13 .- Principle of Responsibility of the Causer (Polluter Pays)

Persons who have caused damage to the environment shall be responsible for the costs of repair and for measures to prevent, avoid, and reduce environmental damage.

#### Article 14 .- User Pays Principle

Persons who use natural resources shall pay the costs for the use or the impacts arising from the use of those natural resources.

#### Article 15 .- Precautionary Principle

In cases where there are concerns that there is serious damage to the environment that cannot be restored or repaired, the lack of scientific certainty may not be used as a reason for delaying the implementation of measures to prevent damage.

#### Article 16 .- Principle of Mitigation or Prevention of Environmental Damage

Various measures for mitigation or prevention of environmental damage shall be taken before the damage occurs. These measures must be taken in advance by requiring environmental and social impact assessments rather than recovering or repairing the damage.

#### Article 17 .- Evidence-Based Decision-Making Principle

Information with scientific, technological, and best available innovative characteristics shall be used as the basis for transparent decision-making related to the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 18 .- Public Interest Principle

The public interest must prevail over the interests of individuals or private legal entities in the decision-making process related to the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 19 .- Principle of Environmental Integration

Environmental protection and sustainable development shall be integrated into development planning and decision-making in both the initial and implementation stages of policies and laws.

## Book 2
## Environmental Management and Sustainable Mechanisms

### Content 1
### Disaster Risk Reduction and Management

#### Chapter 1
#### General Provisions

#### Article 20 .- Objective

This Content aims to manage, prevent, reduce disasters, and restore damages and/or impacts in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 21 .- Scope

This Content has the scope of application on the management, prevention, reduction of disasters, and restoration of damages and/or impacts in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Chapter 2
#### Responsible Institutions

#### Article 22 .- Responsible Ministry and Institution

The ministry or institution in charge of the disaster sector is responsible for disaster management in accordance with the provisions of this Code and relevant legal instruments.

#### Article 23 .- Roles and Duties of the Responsible Ministry and Institution

The ministry or institution in charge of the disaster sector has the following roles and duties:

A- Prepare draft policies, strategic plans, action plans, various legal instruments, and technical guidelines for disaster management.

B- Coordinate with ministries, institutions, and relevant parties to seek funds to support disaster restoration and management.

C- Provide comments and proposals to the Royal Government on declaring policies and various measures regarding disaster preparedness and management to respond to emergencies and ensure the safety of the citizens.

D- Submit proposals to the Royal Government regarding the preparation of contingency funds, materials, and human resources for preparedness and emergency response interventions, rescue, and restoration of all damages and/or impacts before, during, and after disasters.

E- Disseminate disaster management work to the citizens and train human resources to increase the effectiveness of disaster management capacity.

#### Article 24 .- Cooperation on Disaster Management

The ministry or institution in charge of the disaster sector shall cooperate with the ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector, relevant ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations to:

A- Prepare disaster management plans and disaster reduction plans to reduce the impacts of natural or man-made disasters.

B- Prepare for prevention and reduction before disasters occur, emergency response during disasters, and restoration of damages and/or impacts after disasters.

C- Assess the causes of vulnerability caused by disasters.

D- Strengthen the capacity of relevant institutions and sub-national administrations to manage, prevent, reduce disasters, and restore damages and/or impacts.

E- Consult and collect inputs from citizens in various regions, competent ministries and institutions, sub-national administrations, and relevant parties to create disaster resilience.

F- Promote public awareness, organize training, education, and encourage participation in managing, preventing, reducing disasters, and restoring damages and/or impacts.

### Content 2
### Biodiversity Management for Sustainable Development

#### Chapter 1
#### General Provisions

#### Article 25 .- Objective

This Content aims to manage and conserve biodiversity and the sustainable use of ecosystem services.

#### Article 26 .- Scope

This Content has the scope of application on the management and conservation of biodiversity, the use of ecosystem services, and safety land management for sustainable development in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### Chapter 2
### Responsible Institutions

#### Article 27 .- Responsible Ministry and Institution

The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector is responsible for the management and conservation of biodiversity, the sustainable use of ecosystem services, and safety land management.

#### Article 28 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in Charge of the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector

The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector has the following roles and duties:

A- Implement international instruments related to the environment, biodiversity, and safety land to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

B- Coordinate the preparation of national reports, assess the status of biodiversity and the use of ecosystem services, and provide recommendations to strengthen the management and conservation of biodiversity and the use of ecosystem services for sustainable development.

C- Lead international negotiations to represent the position of the Kingdom of Cambodia on biodiversity to international bodies.

D- Prepare draft policies, legal instruments, strategic plans, action plans, and project programs for creating botanical gardens, arboretums, seed banks, and biodiversity conservation sites outside natural habitats to promote conservation, research, technology exchange, and educational dissemination to increase public knowledge.

E- Prepare draft policies, legal instruments, strategic plans, action plans, measures, conservation and development programs, management programs, research programs, collection and identification, species restoration, and assessment of functions, roles, and values of biodiversity and ecosystems for sustainable conservation and development.

F- Provide comments, audit, monitor, and implement policies, legal instruments, strategic plans, and action plans for the research of biodiversity and ecosystems effectively to promote and increase education, training, dissemination, and technology exchange.

G- Prepare legal instruments and procedures for collecting and compiling biodiversity and ecosystem data for the preparation of national biodiversity status reports and monitoring those statuses.

H- Provide comments and audit procedures, methodologies, and documents for research and verify research results before publishing them as national dissemination documents, as well as coordinate and lead national biodiversity research forums to strengthen cooperation among researchers and publishers.

I- Provide support, promote innovation, collect and compile data, research, educate, disseminate, and train relevant ministries and institutions on biodiversity and ecosystem management for sustainable development.

J- Establish sustainable financing mechanisms for biodiversity and ecosystem conservation to ensure that the functions, roles, and values of biodiversity and ecosystem services are recognized and integrated into negotiations with relevant parties and the government as priorities across sectors and the private sector.

K- Coordinate the mobilization of biodiversity and ecosystem financing effectively and efficiently by cooperating with the ministry in charge of economics and finance and national and international partners.

L- Coordinate technical work with ministries, institutions, and relevant parties to integrate innovation, promote restoration, monitor, audit, and effectively implement the management of biodiversity and ecosystems for sustainable development.

M- Coordinate with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations to integrate innovation, monitor, and audit the effectiveness of policy implementation, strategic plans, action plans, and legal instruments related to biodiversity and ecosystem management.

N- Lead and coordinate the preparation of national biodiversity targets and indicators based on the global biodiversity strategic plan, sustainable development goals, national policy framework, and strategy reflecting national status and priorities and available resources.

O- Collect, produce, update, manage, and make available to the public information on biodiversity and ecosystems by cooperating with ministries, institutions, and relevant parties.

#### Article 29 .- Cooperation with Relevant Ministries and Institutions

1- The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector shall cooperate and coordinate with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations as necessary when implementing its roles and duties.

2- The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector may enter into agreements on the production and sharing of data with competent ministries, institutions, and other legal entities to create, use, and share data and information on biodiversity and the sustainable use of ecosystem services.

#### Article 30 .- Strategic Plans

Relevant ministries and institutions, in cooperation with the ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector, shall include and integrate biodiversity conservation and the sustainable use of ecosystem services and the fair and equitable sharing of benefits from the use of genetic resources into their strategic plans and shall declare and implement necessary guidelines and mechanisms to ensure the sustainability of biodiversity and ecosystem services.

#### Article 31 .- Implementation of International Instruments Related to Biodiversity

The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector shall cooperate with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations in preparing draft policies, strategic plans, action plans, legal instruments, and various programs to implement the United Nations Convention on Biological Diversity, the Cartagena Protocol on Biosafety, the Nagoya Protocol on Access and Benefit-sharing (ABS), and other international instruments related to biodiversity and ecosystem services to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

### Content 3
### Biosafety

#### Chapter 1
#### General Provisions

#### Article 32 .- Objective

This Content aims to ensure the effectiveness of the conservation and sustainable use of biodiversity by paying attention to the risks from the use and release of living modified organisms (LMOs) into the environment and the impacts on human health through the implementation of the precautionary principle of biosafety, raising awareness of modern biotechnology, and preventing negative impacts on biodiversity and natural resources conservation.

#### Article 33 .- Scope

1- This Content has the scope of application on all operational activities related to living modified organisms, such as import, export, transit, contained use, introduction into the environment, direct use as food or feed, and processing in various other forms.

2- Activities or operations as stated in paragraph 1 do not include the use of living modified organisms for pharmaceuticals.

#### Chapter 2
#### Responsible Institutions

#### Article 34 .- Responsible Ministry and Institution

The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector has the competence to audit all activities and/or operations related to living modified organisms.

#### Article 35 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in Charge of the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector

The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector is the national focal point in charge of the biosafety management sector, with the following roles and duties:

A- Prepare draft policies, strategic plans, action plans, technical guidelines, and legal instruments for biosafety management.

B- Review and decide on requests for the import and export of living modified organisms.

C- Act as the national focal point for primary contact between the Royal Government of the Kingdom of Cambodia and the Secretariat of the Convention on Biological Diversity.

D- Responsible for timely notification to other countries and international organizations and relevant biosafety clearing-houses in case of any incidents in the Kingdom of Cambodia caused by any unintentional transboundary movement of living modified organisms.

E- Notify and disseminate timely information through coordination mechanisms and biosafety information sharing.

#### Article 36 .- Cooperation with Ministries and Institutions

The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector, in cooperation with competent ministries and sub-national administrations, shall:

A- Audit all activities and/or operations related to biosafety.

B- Review and provide technical comments on requests for risk assessment of activities and/or operations related to living modified organisms.

#### Article 37 .- Advance Risk Assessment

1- The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector shall ensure that there is an appropriate risk assessment in advance for all activities and/or operations related to living modified organisms as specified in this Code.

2- The proponent has the obligation to conduct a risk assessment of their activities and/or operations related to living modified organisms. All costs for implementing this obligation shall be the sole responsibility of the proponent.

3- The ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector shall review and decide on the proposal after receiving all relevant documents.

### Chapter 3
### Living Modified Organisms

#### Section 1
#### Import and Export of Living Modified Organisms

#### Article 38 .- Procedures Before Importing Living Modified Organisms

1- Persons who intend to import living modified organisms into the Kingdom of Cambodia shall provide written notification and receive advance agreement from the ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector before requesting permission from competent ministries and sub-national administrations.

2- Procedures for requesting and requirements for notification of the request for the first-time import permission of living modified organisms shall be determined by a Prakas of the Minister of the Ministry in Charge of the Environmental and Natural Resources Sector after consultation with competent ministries and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 39 .- Import for Contained Use

Permission for the first-time import of living modified organisms into the Kingdom of Cambodia for contained use shall require a risk assessment and advance agreement from the ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector after consultation with ministries and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 40 .- Import for Intentional Introduction into the Environment

The import of living modified organisms into the Kingdom of Cambodia for intentional introduction into the environment shall require a risk assessment and advance agreement from the ministry in charge of the environmental and natural resources sector.

Following the consultation with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, before applying for import authorization from the competent ministries and institutions.

#### Article 41 .- Import for Direct Use as Food, Feed, or for Processing

1- The first import of living modified organisms into the Kingdom of Cambodia for direct use as food, feed, or for processing shall be subject to a risk assessment and prior approval from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, before applying for import authorization from the competent ministries and institutions.

2- Any person intending to apply for import authorization for living modified organisms as per Paragraph 1 above shall provide the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources with fixed written certificates from the competent ministries and institutions of the exporting country, certifying the accuracy of information related to the living modified organisms to be imported.

#### Article 42 .- Decision-Making Procedure for Import

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall respond in writing, specifying basic approval or disapproval to the applicant within a maximum of 65 (sixty-five) working days from the date of receiving the application.

2- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources may attach conditions to the approval, requesting additional information or a release order for the proposal, after receiving the results of the risk assessment and other required documents.

3- Within a maximum of 180 (one hundred and eighty) working days after receiving the application, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall notify the applicant in writing and the biosafety information clearing-house network of its decision.

4- When the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has approved the living modified organisms for direct use as food, feed, or for processing, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall notify all members of the Cartagena Protocol through the Biosafety Clearing-House within 10 (ten) days.

5- The principle approval letter from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall be attached to the import authorization for living modified organisms.

6- The procedures, requirements, and conditions for decision-making shall be determined by a Prakas of the Minister of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, following consultation with relevant ministries, institutions, or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 43 .- Procedure Prior to Export of Living Modified Organisms

1- Any person intending to export living modified organisms from the Kingdom of Cambodia shall notify the competent institutions of the importing country in writing before applying for an export permit from the competent ministries and institutions of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

2- The competent ministries and institutions may issue an export permit for living modified organisms from the Kingdom of Cambodia after receiving written approval from the competent institutions of the importing country. Approval letter

from the importing party shall be accompanied by an export permit and other documents related to packaging and handling for export.

#### Article 44 .- Export Certification

Any exporter of living modified organisms from the Kingdom of Cambodia shall have a written certification from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources on the accuracy of all information provided related to the living modified organisms to be exported. This certification shall be attached to the export permit and/or other documents related to packaging and handling for export.

#### Article 45 .- Preparation of Documents

1- Unless otherwise provided by separate legal provisions, any person intending to import or export living modified organisms from the Kingdom of Cambodia for any purpose shall provide accompanying documents clearly specifying the identity of those living modified organisms, that they are indeed living modified organisms, and specifying the requirements for safety in packaging, handling, transport, use, and storage.

2- These documents shall be attached to the goods during transboundary movement and transport to the entry point.

3- The import or export of living modified organisms from the Kingdom of Cambodia shall be subject to the following conditions:

a- For living modified organisms for direct use as food, feed, or for processing, it must be clearly specified that these goods are living modified organisms and are not intended for intentional introduction into the environment.

b- For living modified organisms for intentional introduction into the environment, the identity, relevant characteristics, safety requirements for packaging, handling, transport, use, and storage must be clearly specified, and the contact point for further information must also be specified, along with a statement that the transboundary movement of the living modified organisms is in accordance with the requirements of the Cartagena Protocol for exporters.

c- For living modified organisms for contained use, it must be clearly specified that these goods are living modified organisms and the safety requirements for packaging, handling, transport, use, and storage must be clearly specified, and the contact point for further information must also be specified, including the name and address of the person and institution to whom the living modified organisms are to be delivered.

### SECTION 2: LIABILITY AND REDRESS

#### Article 46 .- Obligations to be fulfilled by persons causing impacts or damage

1- Any person who has conducted operations related to living modified organisms and who has caused impacts or damage to the environment, society, economy, biodiversity, human health, or property from their operations shall fulfill the following obligations:

a- Immediately notify the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources of the environmental, social, and economic impacts or damage.

b- Conduct a preliminary assessment of the environmental, social, and economic impacts or damage.

c- Take various appropriate response measures.

2- The obligations as provided for in the above paragraph shall be fulfilled in accordance with the instructions from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 47 .- Self-action by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

1- After receiving information about the environmental, social, and economic impacts or damage as provided for in Article 46 (Obligations to be fulfilled by persons causing impacts or damage) of this Code, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources may take necessary measures to prevent or reduce those impacts or damage, in case the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources finds that the initial response measures of the person as provided for in Paragraph 1 of Article 46 (Obligations to be fulfilled by persons causing impacts or damage) of this Code are not appropriate.

2- All costs related to the above necessary measures shall be the entire responsibility of the person who conducted operations related to living modified organisms and who caused environmental, social, and economic impacts or damage.

#### Article 48 .- Assessment of Environmental, Social, and Economic Impacts or Damage

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall conduct an assessment of the environmental, social, and economic impacts or damage caused by the person as provided for in Paragraph 1 of Article 46 (Obligations to be fulfilled by persons causing impacts or damage) of this Code.

#### Article 49 .- Liability for Environmental, Social, and Economic Impacts or Damage

Any person who has conducted operations related to living modified organisms that caused impacts or damage to the environment, society, economy, biodiversity, human health, or property from their operations shall be fully liable for those impacts or damage.

#### Article 50 .- Identification of Liable Persons

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall lead and cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions to identify the liable persons as provided for in Article 49 (Liability for Environmental, Social, and Economic Impacts or Damage) of this Code.

#### Article 51 .- Cooperation in Managing Impacts or Damage in Case of Force Majeure

In case of force majeure leading to environmental, social, and economic impacts or damage arising from living modified organisms, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and relevant parties shall cooperate to take all measures to manage, prevent, and reduce those impacts or damage.

#### Article 52 .- Provision of Information about Environmental Damage

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall provide public notification about potential negative impacts in the future resulting from living modified organisms, but only if this notification does not cause public disorder and social insecurity.

## PART 4: ACCESS AND BENEFIT-SHARING FROM THE USE OF GENETIC RESOURCES

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 53 .- Purpose

The purpose of this Part is to ensure the sustainability and conservation of biodiversity through the fair and equitable sharing of benefits arising from the access and use of genetic resources, derivatives of genetic resources, or associated traditional knowledge.

#### Article 54 .- Scope

This Part applies to the access to genetic resources, derivatives of genetic resources, and associated traditional knowledge originating within the Kingdom of Cambodia or acquired by the Kingdom of Cambodia or by any person under its jurisdiction for the purpose of using genetic resources and associated traditional knowledge.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 55 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the competence to manage the access and the fair and equitable sharing of benefits arising from the use of genetic resources in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 56 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources is the national focal point with the following roles and duties:

a- To develop and implement policies, frameworks, strategic plans, action plans, and programs related to the access and benefit-sharing from the use of genetic resources to ensure the conservation and sustainable use of genetic resources.

b- To develop and implement mechanisms, forms, procedures, and necessary measures related to the access and benefit-sharing from the use of genetic resources.

c- To lead and cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions to develop and implement legal documents related to the fair and equitable access and benefit-sharing from the use of genetic resources.

d- To promote capacity building, research, technology transfer, and knowledge sharing with the public related to the access and benefit-sharing from the use of genetic resources.

2- The mechanisms, forms, and procedures for the access and benefit-sharing from the use of genetic resources, and legal penalties for related offenses, shall be determined by a separate Sub-decree.

## PART 5: SUSTAINABLE USE OF ECOSYSTEM SERVICES

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 57 .- Purpose

The purpose of this Part is to ensure the use of ecosystem services to support the sustainable conservation of biodiversity and sustainable development in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 58 .- Scope

This Part applies to the use of ecosystem services both inside and outside natural protected areas in a sustainable manner in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 59 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall be responsible for managing the sustainable use of ecosystem services.

#### Article 60 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the following roles and duties:

a- To develop and implement policies, frameworks, strategic plans, and programs related to the use of ecosystem services.

b- To develop principles, legal documents, mechanisms, forms, procedures, and requirements related to the use of ecosystem services in cooperation with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations.

c- To coordinate, collect, distribute, and manage the implementation process for the use of ecosystem services, including negotiating contracts and resolving related disputes.

#### Article 61 .- Basic Principles for the Use of Ecosystem Services

Basic principles for the use of ecosystem services include:

a- Ensuring public participation in the protection and conservation of biodiversity and sustainable development.

b- Ensuring the sustainable use of ecosystem services.

#### Article 62 .- Types of Ecosystem Services

Types of ecosystem services include, among others:

a- Provisioning services: Food for humans and animals, fresh water, medicines, and raw materials.

b- Regulating services: Including various benefits obtained from ecosystem regulation processes, such as fresh air production and participation in natural nutrient cycles.

c- Cultural services: Referring to various non-material benefits obtained from ecosystems, among others: abundance of ideas, concepts, recreation, and aesthetics.

d- Supporting services: Necessary for humans, plants, and animals, among others: the process of photosynthesis and nutrient cycling.

#### Article 63 .- Use of Ecosystem Services

1- Any person using ecosystem services shall pay a service fee, in case this obligation is required by a Prakas of the Minister of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

2- The service fee mentioned in Paragraph 1 shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prakas between the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry of Economy and Finance.

3- The conditions and procedures for paying ecosystem service fees shall be determined by a Prakas of the Minister of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

## PART 6: CLIMATE CHANGE

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 64 .- Purpose

This Part aims to strengthen and improve adaptation and resilience to climate change and the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions by:

a- Assessing vulnerability and adaptation to climate change.

b- Preparing inventories and calculating greenhouse gas emissions.

c- Mobilizing climate finance.

#### Article 65 .- Scope

This Part applies to all legal norms, policies, strategies, and action plans related to climate change in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 66 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall serve as the secretariat of the Royal Government in monitoring the implementation of legal norms, policies, strategies, and action plans related to climate change in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 67 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the following roles and duties:

a- To develop and coordinate visions, goals, policies, strategies, plans, and legal norms related to climate change.

b- To inspect, monitor, and evaluate the implementation of policies, strategies, and plans related to climate change.

c- To develop and disseminate laws and legal norms related to climate change.

d- To lead, coordinate, and implement work as the focal point for international instruments on climate change.

e- To strengthen and promote cooperation in the preparation and monitoring of the implementation of policies, strategies, and plans related to climate change.

f- To mobilize resources and technical assistance for the implementation of policies, strategies, action plans, programs, and projects related to climate change.

g- To manage, coordinate, monitor, and evaluate the implementation of action plans, programs, and projects related to climate change.

h- To lead, coordinate, and implement action plans to provide scientific information to support the preparation and evaluation of the implementation of policies, strategies, and plans related to climate change.

i- To lead, coordinate, and establish data systems, research centers, and museums related to climate change.

### CHAPTER 3: CLIMATE CHANGE RESPONSE

### SECTION 1: POLICIES, STRATEGIES, AND PLANS FOR CLIMATE CHANGE RESPONSE

#### Article 68 .- Updating and Coordinating Climate Change Response

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall update and coordinate the climate change response in accordance with policies and strategies on climate change and international instruments on climate change and sustainable development to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

#### Article 69 .- Preparation of National and Sub-national Development Strategic Plans and Sectoral Strategies

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Planning, shall ensure the integration and inclusion of climate change considerations into national and sub-national development strategic plans and sectoral strategies of relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 70 .- Preparation of Sectoral Action Plans and Investment Plans

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall ensure the integration and inclusion of climate change considerations into sectoral action plans and investment plans.

2- The preparation and inclusion as provided for in Paragraph 1 above shall be in accordance with the laws, legal norms, policies, and national strategies related to climate change and international instruments on climate change to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

#### Article 71 .- Preparation of Development Plans and Investment Plans

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance, the Ministry in charge of Planning, and relevant ministries and institutions, shall ensure the integration and inclusion of climate change considerations and response measures into development plans and investment programs.

2- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall ensure the integration and inclusion of climate change considerations and response measures into all of its development plans and investment programs.

### SECTION 2: CLIMATE CHANGE RESPONSE

#### Article 72 .- Climate Change Response

To respond to climate change, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and relevant parties, shall implement the following measures:

a- Measures related to the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions include, among others:

- Promoting and improving energy efficiency.

- Promoting the development and use of renewable energy.

- Promoting the development and use of public transport and the use of transport that saves fuel and produces low carbon.

- Promoting the restoration, conservation, and sustainable management of forests to protect carbon sinks and increase the capacity to absorb greenhouse gases.

- Promoting and supporting the implementation of Clean Development Mechanisms (CDM), Nationally Appropriate Mitigation Actions (NAMA), and the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation, as well as forest conservation, sustainable forest management, and the enhancement of forest carbon stocks (REDD+), and other greenhouse gas emission reduction activities.

- Promoting awareness and encouraging the implementation of land use management laws to address climate change.

b- Measures related to adaptation to climate change include, among others:

- Assessing the vulnerability of biological, social, and economic systems to climate change, including areas susceptible to climate issues and geographical regions prone to climate-related disasters, and establishing climate scenarios for adaptation to climate change.

- Preparing and building climate-resilient and low-carbon infrastructure, including agriculture, fisheries, transport, and clean energy.

- Developing and implementing management measures for natural protected areas to adapt to climate change.

- Improving early warning systems and timely dissemination of climate information, and promoting sustainable living habits that increase resilience to climate change.

c- Mobilizing and providing finance, capacity building, raising awareness, and technology transfer, including:

- Mobilizing climate finance from development partners and relevant climate funds.

- Strengthening technical and institutional capacity to respond to climate change.

- Strengthening knowledge of laws and technologies for adaptation and mitigation of climate change that are suitable for the context of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

- Promoting research, education, technology transfer, and international cooperation on climate change.

- Raising public awareness of climate change.

### SECTION 3: PREPARATION OF FINANCING FRAMEWORKS AND RESOURCE MOBILIZATION

#### Article 73 .- Preparation of Financing Frameworks for Climate Change Activities

1- The preparation of financing frameworks for climate change activities, in cooperation with relevant ministries and institutions, shall include:

a- Analysis of financing needs.

b- Strategies to meet financing needs using domestic and international financial sources.

c- Capacity-building measures.

d- Institutional reform or financial mechanisms to mobilize and manage funds related to climate change.

2- The preparation of the financing framework as provided for in Paragraph 1 above shall be done by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance.

#### Article 74 .- Mobilizing International Finance for the Implementation of Climate Change Activities

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the competence to coordinate the mobilization of international finance for the implementation of activities related to climate change.

2- Requests for international finance for the implementation of climate change activities shall be submitted to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources for review and approval.

3- The procedures for review and approval of requests for international finance as provided for in Paragraph 2 above shall be determined by a Prakas of the Minister of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 75 .- Mobilizing Domestic Finance for the Implementation of Climate Change Activities

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources may coordinate with the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance to mobilize domestic finance for the implementation of climate change activities by requesting the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance to consider financing requests from relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 76 .- Reporting on the Use of Finance for the Implementation of Climate Change Activities

Relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, or project owners shall provide reports on the use of finance for the implementation of climate change activities upon request from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, to collect information related to the use of finance for the implementation of activities related to climate change.

### SECTION 4: REDUCTION OF GREENHOUSE GAS EMISSIONS

#### Article 77 .- Purpose of Greenhouse Gas Emission Reduction

The reduction of greenhouse gas emissions aims to mitigate climate change, ensuring economic, social, environmental development and the well-being of the people, in accordance with the Royal Government's policies related to climate change response and appropriately implementing international instruments related to climate change to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

#### Article 78 .- Preparation of Legal Norms Related to the Management of Greenhouse Gas Emission Reduction

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall prepare legal norms related to the management of greenhouse gas emission reduction.

### CHAPTER 4: CLIMATE CHANGE INFORMATION AND REPORTING

### SECTION 1: CLIMATE CHANGE INFORMATION

#### Article 79 .- Management of Climate Change Information

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall:

a- Prepare legal norms and guidelines for the production and management of data and information related to climate change.

b- Prepare and manage information systems accessible to the public, to collect information on climate change produced by relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and other sources.

2- Relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations shall cooperate with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources to update the management and provide public access to climate change information relevant to their sectors.

3- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources may enter into agreements on the production and sharing of data with relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and/or the private sector to strengthen and increase access to data and information on climate change related to the implementation of climate change response in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### SECTION 2: PREPARATION OF REPORTS

#### Article 80 .- Preparation of Climate Change-Related Reports

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall prepare reports and may prepare plans and strategies to implement international instruments related to climate change, including:

a- National Communications on Climate Change.

b- Nationally Determined Contributions (NDC) to address climate change.

c- Transparency Reports on Climate Change.

d- Long-term Strategies for Carbon Neutrality.

e- Reports on Measurement, Reporting, and Verification (MRV) related to climate change.

f- Other reports as necessary.

#### Article 81 .- Preparation of National Communications on Climate Change

National Communications on Climate Change shall be prepared every 4 (four) years for submission to the Secretariat of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC).

#### Article 82 .- Nationally Determined Contributions (NDC) to Address Climate Change

Nationally Determined Contributions to address climate change may be prepared every 5 (five) years for submission to the Secretariat of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC).

#### Article 83 .- Preparation of Transparency Reports on Climate Change

Transparency Reports on Climate Change shall be prepared every 2 (two) years for submission to the Secretariat of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC).

#### Article 84 .- Preparation of Long-term Strategies for Carbon Neutrality

Long-term Strategies for Carbon Neutrality may be developed to demonstrate high-level commitment to reducing greenhouse gas emissions.

#### Article 85 .- Preparation of Measurement, Reporting, and Verification (MRV) Reports Related to Climate Change

1- To monitor and evaluate the implementation of activities related to climate change, relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations shall prepare reports on Measurement, Reporting, and Verification (MRV) related to climate change.

2- The reports as provided for in Paragraph 1 above shall include:

a- Relevant indicators for the Measurement, Reporting, and Verification framework on climate change.

b- Information on national and sectoral Measurement, Reporting, and Verification on climate change.

c- Other information as necessary.

3- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the right to request reports on Measurement, Reporting, and Verification related to climate change from relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and relevant parties to collect information for reports to the Royal Government and for reports to be submitted to the Secretariat of the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC) in accordance with international instruments related to climate change and to disseminate to the public for understanding and participation in climate change response activities.

## PART 7: SUSTAINABLE CONSUMPTION AND PRODUCTION

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 86 .- Purpose

This Part aims to promote sustainable consumption and production to contribute to the promotion of the sustainable use of natural resources, reduction of environmental impacts, ecosystem impacts, and public health, moving toward a circular economy and green economy approach in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 87 .- Scope

This Part applies to sustainable consumption and production, including, among others: sustainable public procurement, resource efficiency assessment, producer responsibility, eco-labeling, verification, and assessment of eco-friendly technologies.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 88 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall be responsible for monitoring the implementation of sustainable consumption and production.

#### Article 89 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the following roles and duties:

a- To develop draft policies, strategic plans, legal norms, and technical guidelines for sustainable consumption and production in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions.

b- To prepare management plans for sustainable consumption and production.

c- To cooperate in the development of technical guidelines for sustainable public procurement with competent ministries and institutions.

d- To determine criteria, standards, and eco-friendly technologies.

e- To review and decide on applications for eco-labeling.

f- To inspect and evaluate products and services based on eco-friendly criteria and standards.

g- To prepare an inventory of products and services that have received eco-recognition.

h- To evaluate resource efficiency to reduce environmental impacts.

i- To develop guidelines and legal norms for the assessment of resource efficiency and investment in eco-friendly technologies.

j- To verify and evaluate the implementation of eco-friendly technologies.

k- To develop guidelines for eco-friendly technologies.

### CHAPTER 3: MEASURES TO PROMOTE SUSTAINABLE CONSUMPTION AND PRODUCTION

#### Article 90 .- Measures to Promote Sustainable Consumption and Production

Measures to promote sustainable consumption and production include, among others:

a- Sustainable public procurement.

b- Eco-labeling.

c- Resource efficiency assessment.

d- Producer responsibility.

e- Verification and assessment of eco-friendly technologies.

### SECTION 1: SUSTAINABLE PUBLIC PROCUREMENT

#### Article 91 .- Purpose of Sustainable Public Procurement

The purpose of sustainable public procurement is as follows:

a- To encourage resource efficiency.

b- To determine the types and priority levels of products and services according to criteria, including, among others: eco-friendly technology, safety, sustainability, finance, and social factors.

c- To determine minimum environmental criteria and requirements for operations.

d- To determine reporting requirements for different types of products, services, and procurement sizes.

e- To provide guidance on selecting suitable suppliers.

f- To provide guidance for decision-making with environmental considerations, including integrating environmental criteria with purchasing criteria such as economic factors, price, quality, performance, and disposal after use.

g- To clearly determine common aspects for promoting and encouraging production and services that prioritize the environment according to the timeline.

h- To clearly determine incentive measures for relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations responsible for procurement to encourage and assist parties in their responsibility for sustainable public procurement.

#### Article 92 .- Preparation of Procedures and Forms for Sustainable Public Procurement

The Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall prepare procedures and forms for sustainable public procurement.

### SECTION 2: ECO-LABELING

#### Article 93 .- Eco-labeling

1- An eco-label is a mark used on products and services to assist consumers and buyers in identifying environmental friendliness criteria.

2- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with relevant ministries and institutions, shall develop criteria and standards for eco-labeling.

#### Article 94 .- Application for Eco-labeling

1- Any person may apply for an eco-label to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

2- The application for an eco-label as provided for in Paragraph 1 above shall be made in writing.

3- Upon receiving the application for an eco-label, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall inspect and evaluate the products and services based on eco-friendly criteria and standards. If necessary, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources may cooperate with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations.

4- The procedures for deciding to grant or not grant an eco-label shall be determined by a Prakas of the Minister of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 95 .- Inventory of Products and Services Receiving Eco-labels

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall prepare an inventory of products and services that have received eco-labels and publish that inventory to the public.

### SECTION 3: RESOURCE EFFICIENCY ASSESSMENT

#### Article 96 .- Purpose

The purpose of resource efficiency assessment is to contribute to saving resources, promoting product productivity, reducing environmental impacts, and contributing to improving the quality of life of the people.

#### Article 97 .- Preparation of Guidelines and Legal Norms for Resource Efficiency Assessment

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with relevant ministries and institutions, shall prepare guidelines, legal norms, forms, procedures, and scope for resource efficiency assessment.

#### Article 98 .- Preparation of Resource Efficiency Assessment Reports

1- Any person who uses resources and causes environmental impacts shall prepare a resource efficiency assessment report.

2- The preparation of the resource efficiency assessment report as provided for in Paragraph 1 above shall be done by resource efficiency assessment consultants who have been recognized by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

3- The cost of hiring consultants to prepare the resource efficiency assessment report shall be the responsibility of the person causing the environmental impact.

4- The resource efficiency assessment report as provided for in Paragraph 2 above shall be submitted by the person causing the environmental impact to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 99 .- Review of Resource Efficiency Assessment Reports

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall review the resource efficiency assessment report:

a- If the resource efficiency assessment report contains sufficient information, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall approve the report.

b- If the resource efficiency assessment report does not contain sufficient information or if there is doubt, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall conduct a resource efficiency audit for verification.

#### Article 100 .- Preparation of Guidelines to Access Finance

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions, shall prepare guidelines to encourage financial institutions to provide financing for resource efficiency projects and investment in eco-friendly technologies.

### SECTION 4: PRODUCER RESPONSIBILITY

#### Article 101 .- Producer Responsibility

To encourage producer responsibility for waste that poses a high risk to the environment after use, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations shall establish mechanisms and measures including:

a- Voluntary or mandatory participation in producer responsibility applied to products, product groups, and waste streams.

b- Targets for collection, recycling, refining, and reuse.

c- Limits on the amount of product take-back.

d- Responsibility of stakeholders in the supply chain, including, among others, domestic producers and importers of products and technology components from abroad.

e- Capacity building for stakeholders.

f- Responsibility of producers and other groups that have legal obligations.

### SECTION 5: VERIFICATION AND ASSESSMENT OF ECO-FRIENDLY TECHNOLOGIES

#### Article 102 .- Purpose

The purpose of verifying and evaluating eco-friendly technologies is to acquire eco-friendly technologies that are economical and easy to implement in sustainable consumption and production.

#### Article 103 .- Establishing Mechanisms for Verification and Assessment of Eco-friendly Technologies

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall:

a- Establish mechanisms for verification and assessment based on best practices, in accordance with national and international contexts, to acquire eco-friendly technologies.

b- Prepare guidelines on the list, procedures, and forms for registering eco-friendly technologies.

## PART 8: LAND USE PLANNING WITH ENVIRONMENTAL CONSIDERATIONS AND SUSTAINABLE CITIES

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 104 .- Purpose

The purpose of this Part is to determine measures for land management, land use, urban planning, and construction with environmental considerations and sustainable cities in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 105 .- Scope

This Part applies to policies, strategic plans, action plans, land use plans, land management plans, land use activities, land resources, and construction with environmental considerations and sustainable cities in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 106 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall be responsible for the management and use of land within natural protected areas.

#### Article 107 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the following roles and duties:

a- To develop draft policies, strategic plans, and land use plans within natural protected areas in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction.

b- To provide comments on land use plans and sustainable cities in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions.

c- To participate in training and dissemination of laws and legal norms related to land use with environmental considerations and sustainable cities.

#### Article 108 .- Preparation of Land Use Plans with Environmental Considerations and Sustainable Cities

1- The Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall develop draft policies and land use plans with environmental considerations and sustainable cities.

2- The preparation of draft policies, land use plans, and sustainable cities as provided for in Paragraph 1 above shall be based on specific scientific information and data and shall include the following points:

a- Environmental conservation and sustainable development.

b- Mechanisms for the management and reduction of environmental risks.

c- Public participation and the right to information on land management and use with environmental considerations and sustainable cities.

### CHAPTER 3: LAND MANAGEMENT AND USE WITH ENVIRONMENTAL CONSIDERATIONS

#### Article 109 .- Cooperation in Land Management and Use with Environmental Considerations

The Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall participate in...

...management and use of land and construction to improve environmental conservation and efficient, sustainable, transparent, equitable, inclusive, social well-being, and integrated land and resource use.

### CHAPTER 4: PREPARATION OF LAND MANAGEMENT AND USE PLANS WITH ENVIRONMENTAL CONSIDERATIONS

#### Article 110 .- Preparation of Guidelines and Requirements for Land Use Planning with Environmental Considerations

1- The Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, shall prepare guidelines and requirements for land use planning with environmental considerations.

2- The preparation of land use plans with environmental considerations shall fulfill all the following requirements:

a- There must be consultation with relevant parties on the project owner's land use plan.

b- Prior consultation must be conducted with local people who are affected by the project implementation.

c- There must be confirmation of the accuracy of the requested project, attached to the land use management plan and land use master plan.

d- There must be research and scientific data at specific locations on the special characteristics of the relevant areas related to land use, including, among others: land resource inventory, information related to land registration, current land use, infrastructure, demographic status, land tenure rights, customary recognized rights, social structures, culture, wildlife transboundary movement, natural disturbances, ecosystem functions, energy resources, and all other relevant information.

e- Appropriate technology must be used in mapping and zoning, including, among others: location determination, assessment of natural resources and services related to natural resources, land use data, land tenure rights, and cultural heritage areas located within development zones.

f- Provide appropriate solutions to reduce environmental impacts and address climate change.

g- Include plans for promoting sustainable forest management within the project implementation locations.

h- Include target measures to reduce environmental impacts and plans for maintaining historical and cultural resource values located at the project implementation locations.

i- Determine the obligations and responsibilities of the project owner in case of impacts on the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 111 .- Inspection, Monitoring, and Evaluation of Project Land Use Requirements

The inspection, monitoring, and evaluation of project land use requirements is the competence of the Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction.

#### Article 112 .- Adjustment of Land Use Plans

1- Any adjustment of the land use plan of a project implementation location shall be requested by the competent authority or project owner to the Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction.

2- The application as provided for in Paragraph 1 above shall be attached with the following documents:

a- Information on social, economic, and scientific reasons leading to the adjustment of the land use plan.

b- Demonstration of efforts to maintain the current land use status.

c- Demonstration of impacts on the livelihoods of local communities and various businesses.

d- Other necessary information.

### CHAPTER 5: SUSTAINABLE CITIES

#### Article 113 .- Preparation and Implementation of Sustainable City Strategic Plans

The Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall prepare sustainable city strategic plans.

#### Article 114 .- Sustainable City Mechanisms

The Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall establish sustainable city mechanisms by:

a- Determining urban vulnerability.

b- Preparing sustainable transport plans for the city.

c- Preparing sustainability plans for the city.

d- Preparing sustainable production plans for the city.

e- Preparing solid and liquid waste management plans for the city.

f- Preparing public green spaces and cultural heritage plans.

g- Preparing green building construction plans.

h- Preparing clean water, ecosystem services plans for the city for water and soil sources, and urban drainage.

#### Article 115 .- Preparation, Implementation, and Revision of Sustainable City Action Plans

1- The Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and relevant ministries and institutions, shall support sub-national administrations in preparing and implementing sustainable city action plans in accordance with the sustainable city strategic plan.

2- Sub-national administrations may adjust their sustainable city action plans based on actual changes in economic, environmental, demographic, and social development, with the approval of the Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction.

## PART 9: GREEN BUILDINGS

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 116 .- Purpose

This Part aims to establish mechanisms for the management of assessment systems and to promote efficient, transparent, and socially equitable green building practices to avoid and minimize negative impacts on the environment, economy, and society.

#### Article 117 .- Scope

This Part applies to assessments starting from design, construction, use, repair, demolition, and management of specialized activities of relevant sectors in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 118 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall be responsible for the following:

a- In cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction, prepare guidelines and assessment systems for green buildings by determining solutions for planning, construction, use, repair, and demolition based on environmental, economic, social, and cultural sustainability. This is based on voluntary principles with the aim of environmental protection, promoting energy efficiency, water saving, use of eco-friendly construction materials and equipment, waste reduction, promoting a circular economy, and participating in the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions while providing comfort and promoting the health of building users in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

b- In cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction and the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance, prepare financial and non-financial incentive systems for buildings and constructions that operate according to green building guidelines and certifications.

#### Article 119 .- Criteria for Developing Green Building Certification Guidelines and Green Building Operations

1- Green building certification guidelines shall include the following criteria:

a- Criteria, indicators, and assessment mechanisms for green buildings.

b- Institutions, committees, national technical working groups, and secretariats.

c- Identification marks for green building symbols and quality levels for green building awards.

d- Requirements for licensing green building auditors and consultants.

2- The preparation of guidelines, green building certification, and green building management activities shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prakas of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction.

#### Article 120 .- Adjustment of Green Building Guidelines and Certifications

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Land Management, Urban Planning, and Construction, may adjust green building guidelines, green building certifications, and green building operations based on changes in environmental conditions and socio-economic progress, to ensure implementation efficiency.

## PART 10: RESPONSIBLE ELECTRICITY ENERGY

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 121 .- Purpose

This Part aims to promote the use of eco-friendly technology, renewable energy, and solar energy in management and preparing a framework for electricity supply and services.

#### Article 122 .- Scope

This Part applies to activities promoting the use of electricity, renewable energy, and solar energy in the supply, provision of services, and use of electricity in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 123 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall be responsible for the preparation of policies, development plans, and management of electricity, renewable energy, and solar energy use.

#### Article 124 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the following roles and duties:

a- Coordinate and provide comments to the Ministry in charge of the energy sector in preparing legal norms and principles related to responsible energy.

b- Participate in promoting the use of renewable energy and solar energy.

c- Participate in education and dissemination of legal norms and guidelines on the use of renewable energy and solar energy.

d- Promoting the use of eco-friendly energy technologies within the energy sector.

e- Participating in providing comments on environmental issues, biodiversity and energy issues, and climate change issues in the preparation of plans and investment projects in the energy sector.

#### Article 125 .- Principles of Responsible Electric Power

1- The Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall prepare principles for responsible electric power. Responsible electric power is electric power that has been developed in accordance with the following conditions:

a- Meeting electricity needs sufficiently and timely according to growth.

b- Providing a reliable, quality electricity supply at a reasonable price for all types of users throughout the Kingdom of Cambodia.

c- Prioritizing and increasing the development of clean energy, including renewable energy and maximum energy efficiency as much as possible.

2- The Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall ensure that all energy development projects implement the principles of responsible electric power above.

#### Article 126 .- Measures to Achieve Responsible Electric Power

To achieve responsible electric power, the following measures shall be implemented:

a- In electricity development planning, choose options that cover both energy security and energy analysis conditions.

b- Prioritize investment in projects that fall within development plan options providing the best energy security and energy analysis.

c- Promote the development and use of technologies that cause minimal environmental impact.

d- Evaluate and approve energy project development plans with responsible energy security and energy analysis.

### CHAPTER 3: PREPARATION AND IMPLEMENTATION OF RESPONSIBLE ELECTRIC POWER STRATEGIC PLANS

#### Article 127 .- Promoting Eco-Friendly Energy Technologies

1- In energy development planning, the Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall encourage and prioritize projects that use energy technologies with efficiency, low carbon emissions, and waste that does not exceed determined environmental standards.

2- Environmental standards shall be determined by a Sub-decree.

3- The Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall prioritize development for any electric power projects in cases where they fall within development plan options providing the best energy security and energy analysis.

#### Article 128 .- Best Available High-Efficiency Technologies and Lower Impact Options

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall not approve initial or full Environmental Impact Assessment reports for projects that cause serious environmental impacts. The Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall not approve the development of any projects that use inefficient technologies.

2- Project owners shall include low-waste and low-carbon options during the project design phase to achieve project objectives.

#### Article 129 .- Promoting Electric Power Supply and Consumer Rights

1- The Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall promote and encourage a sufficient, sustainable, stable, quality, and affordable electric power supply throughout the Kingdom of Cambodia, whether through the national grid or isolated systems.

2- Electricity consumers shall be allowed to set up standalone systems to meet their own electricity needs, subject to prior permission and compliance with regulations, procedures, technical standards, and safety requirements determined by the Ministry in charge of the energy sector.

3- Electricity consumers have the right to choose an electricity supply that they consider appropriate and beneficial to themselves.

#### Article 130 .- Household Production and Use of Renewable Energy

Household production and use of renewable energy shall comply with guidelines of the Ministry in charge of the energy sector in cases where such production and use are not connected to the supply system in a way that affects the operation and safety of the collective supply system.

### CHAPTER 4: FRAMEWORK BY ENERGY TYPE OR ELECTRIC POWER PROJECT

### SECTION 1: RENEWABLE ENERGY

#### Article 131 .- Incentives for Renewable Energy Technology Installation Materials

All materials used solely for the installation of renewable energy technologies shall receive incentives through tax exemptions in accordance with investment regulations in force.

#### Article 132 .- Management and Use of Electricity Supplemental to National Grid Supply

1- The Ministry in charge of managing the production, transmission, and distribution of electricity and managing the use of electricity shall manage the addition and supply of electricity to complement the national grid supply through the installation of rooftop solar connected to the national grid supply system. The Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall prepare policies and legal norms to manage the supply...

...additional supply mentioned above. Private persons wishing to install rooftop solar systems for supplemental personal use and wishing to connect to the national grid supply system must comply with the policies and legal norms for managing supplemental supply prepared by the Ministry in charge of the energy sector as mentioned above.

2- The Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall prepare technical, economic, and safety regulations related to managing the use of electricity supplemental to the grid supply system.

#### Article 133 .- Electricity Payment Mechanisms for Consumers with Rooftop Solar Systems

The Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall prepare legal norms on electricity payment mechanisms for rooftop solar systems connected to the national grid supply system, in accordance with the policies and legal norms for managing supplemental supply prepared by the Ministry in charge of the energy sector as mentioned above and in accordance with the Law on Electricity of the Kingdom of Cambodia.
