#### Article 134 .- Conditions for Connecting Any Solar Systems to the National Grid Supply System

Solar systems belonging to persons wishing to connect to the national grid supply system shall comply with policies, legal norms, technical regulations, economic and safety standards in force, prepared by the Ministry in charge of the energy sector.

#### Article 135 .- Trial Tax Incentive System for Renewable Energy

1- The Ministry in charge of the energy sector, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance and relevant ministries and institutions, shall prepare legal norms on a tax incentive system for renewable energy for a 1 (one) year trial period.

2- These legal norms shall include the following content:

a- Provision of funds to compensate by the Royal Government as a fixed rate for evaluating electricity produced from solar power supply sites or renewable energy production systems.

b- Ensuring guaranteed energy price levels for a certain period, with the aim of reducing the price paid by end-users and facilitating the recovery of investment and maintenance costs for project owners.

c- Establishing an electricity tariff list that electricity consumers must pay for using electricity from renewable energy sources during the trial period.

### SECTION 2: NUCLEAR ENERGY FOR PEACEFUL PURPOSES

#### Article 136 .- Management of Nuclear Energy for Peaceful Purposes

1- Nuclear energy for peaceful purposes shall be established under the management and ownership of the Royal Government, with the Ministry in charge of the energy sector as the secretariat, with management and operation according to international standards for health, safety, security, and environmental protection.

2- The Royal Government, with the Ministry in charge of the energy sector as the secretariat, shall prepare legal norms on nuclear energy for peaceful purposes.

3- The Royal Government shall absolutely not accept nuclear waste from abroad under any conditions or circumstances.

4- The Royal Government shall return to the country of origin all equipment, materials, and components that are contaminated and/or exposed to radioactive substances for which use has ended or the period of validity for use has expired.

#### Article 137 .- Education, Dissemination, and Human Resource Development on Nuclear Energy for Peaceful Purposes

1- The Ministry in charge of nuclear energy for peaceful purposes shall prepare programs to build and develop scientific and technological capacity for its staff and relevant institutions, in cooperation with the International Atomic Energy Agency and relevant parties.

2- Relevant ministries and institutions shall promote public awareness of the use of nuclear energy technology for peaceful purposes through education, dissemination, and the establishment of a nuclear energy information center and a nuclear energy science and technology research information center to build and develop human resources.

#### Article 138 .- Research Programs on Nuclear Energy for Peaceful Purposes

Relevant ministries and institutions shall prepare research programs to:

a- Identify innovative and eco-friendly nuclear energy for peaceful purposes.

b- Assess the potential impacts of nuclear energy development for peaceful purposes on the environment and related sectors.

c- Determine conditions for health, safety, security, and environmental protection regarding the disposal of radioactive waste.

#### Article 139 .- Requirements for Distribution Network Efficiency

Electricity supply network projects shall use efficient technology to reduce electricity losses on the network system. The Ministry in charge of the electricity sector shall be responsible for studying the level of technology to be used along with the economic efficiency of investment to lower electricity prices and reduce environmental impacts. The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources is responsible for reviewing and deciding on Environmental Impact Assessments and the use of eco-friendly technology when applications are submitted.

#### Article 140 .- National Grid Extension

1- The extension of the national grid to various areas throughout the country shall consider environmental, economic, and social factors based on cost-benefit considerations of sufficiency, sustainability, stability, quality, and affordability of the national grid.

2- The Royal Government may stop any national grid extension to specific areas in the country if such extension truly causes serious environmental, economic, and social impacts. In this case, the Royal Government shall provide decentralized electricity supply solutions to the people living in those areas.

#### Article 141 .- Right to Information on Electricity

The Ministry in charge of the energy sector shall examine the possibility of measuring and publicly disseminating information on the cost and quality of supplied electricity to prevent damage to distribution network infrastructure or disruptions in electricity supply.

## PART 11: RESPONSIBLE EXTRACTIVE INDUSTRIES

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 142 .- Purpose

This Part aims to strengthen and improve the management, protection, conservation, and restoration of extractive industry sites by determining limits on extractive projects and relevant information disclosure.

#### Article 143 .- Scope

This Part applies to extractive industry resources, including, among others, oil, gas, minerals, and various geological materials extracted from the earth and other sources as established by legal norms of relevant ministries and institutions.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 144 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall be responsible for monitoring the implementation of extractive industry projects that may affect natural resources, biodiversity, and ecosystems in natural protected areas.

#### Article 145 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the following roles and duties:

a- Review and decide on environmental assessments and strategies related to extractive industries.

b- Review and provide approval on Environmental Impact Assessment reports for extractive industry projects in natural protected areas.

c- Monitor the implementation of environmental protection regulations on extractive industry projects in natural protected areas.

#### Article 146 .- Artisanal and Small-Scale Mining Operations

The Ministry in charge of the mining sector or sub-national administrations shall prepare legal norms on small-scale and artisanal mining operations to:

a- Determine the rights of citizens to conduct artisanal and small-scale mining operations.

b- Ensure that artisanal and small-scale mining operations are conducted with environmental sustainability, safety, and efficiency.

c- Encourage and implement international standards and best practices in managing artisanal and small-scale mining operations.

d- Establish a process for determining zones for artisanal mining operations through consultation with stakeholders and local communities.

e- Require and provide assistance to artisanal mining operators in establishing and implementing safety and security plans.

#### Article 147 .- Avoidance of Environmental Damage

Project owners shall avoid causing pollution or serious environmental damage by using best available technologies and best practices to maximize efficiency during extractive industry activities.

#### Article 148 .- Obligation to Provide Documentation of Cyanide and Mercury Use

Project owners of gold and silver mining projects shall provide documentation to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, through the Ministry in charge of the mining sector, to confirm that cyanide and mercury used in gold and silver mining operations are kept in safe conditions that do not impact the environment.

#### Article 149 .- Requirements for the Use of Cyanide and Mercury

Regarding the use of cyanide and mercury in gold and silver mining projects, project owners shall ensure that storage areas for these substances are safe and do not impact the environment, in accordance with legal norms on the management of cyanide and mercury.

# BOOK 3: ENVIRONMENTAL PROTECTION

## PART 1: GENERAL PRINCIPLES OF ENVIRONMENTAL PROTECTION

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 150 .- Purpose

This Part aims to strengthen the efficiency of waste management, to protect, prevent, and reduce environmental pollution by determining general principles of obligations and responsibilities and by determining measures for implementation on the management of waste, pollutants, and environmental pollution, to ensure public health protection and environmental quality.

#### Article 151 .- Scope

This Part applies to waste management activities, pollutants, and activities for protecting, preventing, and reducing environmental pollution in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 152 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall be responsible for environmental protection to protect, prevent, and reduce environmental pollution and impacts on public health.

#### Article 153 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft policies, strategic plans, legal norms, action plans, and guidelines related to environmental protection.

b- Coordinate with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations in implementing policies, strategic plans, legal norms, and action plans related to environmental protection, implementation of preparedness and emergency response plans for environmental pollution accidents, and environmental quality restoration.

c- Manage data on water, soil, and air quality and data on pollution sources, waste, and pollutants, including electromagnetic data.

d- Inspect and audit sites or means of production, business, or services related to the generation, production, use, recycling, resource extraction, treatment, discharge, and final disposal of waste, pollutants, noise, vibration, and electromagnetism, as well as inspecting the implementation of environmental pollution management plans of investment projects.

e- Review and provide technical comments related to environmental protection on public and private investment projects.

f- Study, research, and assess the spread of toxic chemicals into the environment, and assess levels of environmental pollution and impacts on public health.

g- Provide guidelines and technical support, resources, and means to sub-national administrations related to the management of garbage, solid waste, and urban wastewater, and monitor and evaluate the implementation by sub-national administrations.

h- Manage the provision of public services related to environmental protection and environmental laboratory services.

i- Promote and encourage investment and resource mobilization for environmental protection work, waste management, environmental pollution prevention, and environmental quality restoration.

j- Develop specialized environmental protection capacity for environmental officials of the Capital and Provinces, relevant units, and sub-national administrations.

k- Fulfill duties as the National Focal Point, promoting and coordinating the implementation of international standards and cooperation with national and international development partners related to environmental protection.

### CHAPTER 3: WASTE MANAGEMENT OBLIGATIONS

#### Article 154 .- Waste Management Obligations

Every citizen has the obligation to be responsible for their own waste management, as determined by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 155 .- Obligation to Pay for Waste Services

Every citizen has the obligation to pay for waste collection, transport, treatment, and disposal services, as determined by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 156 .- Compliance with Waste Discharge Standards

1- The discharge of waste or pollutants into the environment must comply with waste discharge standards.

2- Any person who directly performs, orders, or coordinates an act that violates the provision in Paragraph 1 above shall be responsible for such violation.

#### Article 157 .- User Charges

1- The use of products or materials that may pose a risk to the environment, public health, or that cannot be recycled after becoming waste, shall be subject to user charges.

2- Funds collected from user charges shall be used to improve waste management and resource recovery from waste.

3- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance shall be responsible for preparing legal norms and implementation mechanisms for collecting and managing funds from user charges as provided for in Paragraphs 1 and 2 above.

#### Article 158 .- Responsibility of Producers, Importers, or Exclusive Suppliers

Any person who produces, imports, or exclusively supplies equipment, appliances, or materials considered hazardous waste after use shall be responsible for establishing and implementing a system for taking back such used equipment, appliances, or materials and returning them to the country of origin or treating, recycling, or destroying them domestically without causing environmental or public health hazards.

#### Article 159 .- Prohibition of Illegal Waste Disposal

1- It is prohibited to drop, discharge, or destroy waste in violation of the provisions of this Code, laws, or relevant legal norms in force.

2- Any person who directly performs, orders, or coordinates an act that violates the provision in Paragraph 1 above shall be responsible for such violation.

### CHAPTER 4: RESPONSIBILITY FOR ENVIRONMENTAL POLLUTION

#### Article 160 .- Obligation Not to Cause Environmental Pollution

No person shall conduct activities that lead to environmental pollution of water, soil, or air or cause impacts on the health or interests of the public or private individuals.

#### Article 161 .- Obligation Not to Cause Noise, Vibration, or Light Nuisance

No person shall conduct activities discharging noise, vibration, or light that cause nuisance to living conditions or cause impacts on the health or interests of the public or private individuals.

#### Article 162 .- Responsibility for Environmental Pollution Issues or Causing Accidents

Any person who causes environmental pollution issues shall be responsible for compensation for impacts on health, life, or property damage resulting from the influence of environmental pollution, and shall be responsible for compensation for environmental damage and restoration of environmental pollution, in accordance with the provisions of this Code and the Civil Code of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 163 .- Obligation to Pay Environmental Pollution Fees

Any person whose production, business, or service activities cause an increase in pollutant load in the environment through the production or use of hazardous substances or discharge of waste, shall have the obligation to pay environmental pollution fees.

#### Article 164 .- Waste Deposit-Refund System

The use of products, equipment, appliances, or materials that become hazardous waste after use shall require a waste deposit paid by the user, which shall be refunded when the waste is returned.

#### Article 165 .- Management and Use of Funds from Environmental Pollution Fees

1- Funds collected from environmental pollution fees shall be used for research and improving environmental quality or restoring environments affected by pollution.

2- The management and use of funds from environmental pollution fees as provided for in Paragraph 1 above shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prakas of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance.

### CHAPTER 5: ENVIRONMENTAL POLLUTION CONTROL MEASURES

### SECTION 1: ENVIRONMENTAL PROTECTION STANDARDS

#### Article 166 .- Environmental Protection Standards

1- Environmental protection standards include, among others:

a- Environmental quality standards.

b- Waste discharge standards.

c- Noise and vibration standards.

d- Standards for hazardous substances in products.

e- Other relevant environmental protection standards, in accordance with scientific and technological development and its application.

2- Environmental protection standards as provided for in Paragraph 1 above shall be determined by a Sub-decree.

#### Article 167 .- Environmental Quality Standards

Environmental quality standards shall be considered as standards related to public water quality, soil quality, ambient air quality, and indoor air quality that determine the concentration limits of chemical substances or pollutants that pose a risk to public health, for assessing the quality of water, soil, and air.

#### Article 168 .- Waste Discharge Standards

Waste discharge standards shall be considered as standards related to liquid waste or wastewater discharge, air pollutant emissions, solid waste discharge, hazardous pollutant discharge, and radioactive emissions that determine the concentration limits of chemical substances or pollutants allowed to be discharged into the environment without posing a risk to the environment and public health, which must be verified and assessed in accordance with standards set in laws and relevant legal norms in force.

#### Article 169 .- Noise and Vibration Standards

Noise and vibration standards shall be considered as standards related to noise and vibration emissions that determine noise and vibration levels allowed to be emitted indoors or outdoors without causing a nuisance to living conditions and without causing harm to health or property damage, for verification and assessment of law enforcement.

#### Article 170 .- Standards for Hazardous Substances in Products

Standards for hazardous substances in products shall be considered as standards related to product quality containing or contaminated with hazardous substances at allowable levels that do not pose a risk to user health and the environment, for verification and assessment of law enforcement.

#### Article 171 .- Updating Environmental Protection Standards

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall update environmental protection standards in accordance with the progress of the national economy, national or global environmental conditions, technologies, and public interests.

#### Article 172 .- Right to Information on Environmental Protection Standards

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall establish an environmental protection standards database system for relevant parties and the public to search for and receive information in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

### SECTION 2: WASTE PERMITS

#### Article 173 .- Application and Types of Waste Permits

1- Any person wishing to discharge waste or conduct business shall have a permit from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

2- Types of waste permits include:

a- Waste discharge permits, including, among others: solid waste discharge permits, hazardous waste discharge permits, liquid waste discharge permits, air pollutant emission permits, noise emission permits, and vibration emission permits.

b- Waste business permits, including, among others: waste service permits or waste collection business permits, waste transport permits, waste storage permits, waste recycling and resource recovery business permits, waste treatment permits, or final waste disposal site permits.

3- Procedures for waste permit applications as provided for in Paragraphs 1 and 2 of this Article shall be determined by a Prakas of the Minister of Environment.

4- Notwithstanding the provisions in Paragraphs 1 and 2 of this Article, the discharge of waste from daily household residential locations for non-business purposes does not require a permit and shall follow guidelines of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 174 .- Conditions for Granting Waste Permits

Waste permits may be granted only if:

a- There are appropriate materials, equipment, and means according to technical specifications for waste discharge or for providing services or businesses in collection, transport, recycling, resource recovery, treatment, or waste disposal sites that ensure no environmental pollution.

b- There is compliance with waste discharge standards in accordance with legal norms determined under the provisions of this Code or relevant legal norms in force.

c- Compliance with environmental pollution prevention plan conditions determined by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

d- Payment of public service fees for granting waste permits as determined by legal norms of competent ministries and institutions.

#### Article 175 .- Suspension or Revocation of Waste Permits

Waste permits shall be temporarily suspended or permanently revoked if:

a- Use of materials, equipment, and means for waste discharge and for conducting business in collection, transport, recycling, resource recovery, treatment, or waste disposal sites that do not comply with technical specifications.

b- Failure to properly implement environmental standards and safety measures.

c- Negligence or committing offenses in waste management leading to environmental pollution or health hazards.

d- Failure to implement requirements determined in the waste permit.

### SECTION 3: ENVIRONMENTAL POLLUTION MANAGEMENT

#### Article 176 .- Environmental Pollution Management

Any person or owner of a production, business, or service site that generates and discharges waste, pollutants, noise, or vibration shall be responsible for:

a- Installing materials, equipment, and means for managing and discharging waste, pollutants, noise, or vibration properly according to technical specifications and in accordance with legal norms determined by this Code or relevant legal norms in force.

b- Operating materials, equipment, and means for managing waste, pollutants, noise, or vibration properly according to technical specifications and ensuring discharge meets waste discharge standards.

c- Arranging for specialists or technical staff to be responsible for environmental pollution management work and operation of materials, equipment, and means for managing and discharging waste, pollutants, noise, or vibration of the site.

#### Article 177 .- Recognition of Environmental Pollution Management Specialists or Technical Staff

1- Specialists or technical staff appointed to be responsible for environmental pollution management work and operation of materials, equipment, and means for managing and discharging waste, pollutants, noise, or vibration, as provided for in Point c of Article 176 (Environmental Pollution Management) of this Code, shall have official recognition from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

2- Procedures for official recognition of specialists or technical staff appointed to be responsible for environmental pollution management work and operation of materials, equipment, and means for managing and discharging waste, pollutants, noise, or vibration, as provided for in Paragraph 1 above, shall be determined by a Prakas of the Minister of Environment.

### SECTION 4: SELF-MONITORING AND SELF-REPORTING

#### Article 178 .- Implementation of Environmental Pollution Self-Monitoring

Any person or owner of a production, business, or service site whose waste discharge may pose a high risk of environmental pollution shall implement environmental pollution self-monitoring within their site, in compliance with legal norms determined by this Code or relevant legal norms in force.

#### Article 179 .- Implementation of Self-Reporting on Hazardous Substance and Hazardous Waste Management

Any person or owner of a production, business, or service site that uses hazardous substances or is involved in generating hazardous waste shall implement self-reporting measures on hazardous substance and hazardous waste management within their site, in compliance with requirements of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and relevant legal norms in force.

#### Article 180 .- Reporting on Emissions and Pollutants Discharged into the Environment

1- Any person or owner of a production or business site whose production or business activities cause emissions and pollutant discharge into the environment shall provide emission data reports on emissions and pollutant discharge to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

2- The types of production or business sites subject to the obligation to provide emission data reports on emissions and pollutant discharge into the environment, as provided for in Paragraph 1 above, shall be determined by a Prakas of the Minister of Environment.

#### Article 181 .- Right to Information from Emission and Pollutant Discharge Database Systems

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall make available to the public access to or download of information from database systems related to environmental pollution monitoring and emission and pollutant discharge, in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

### SECTION 5: EMERGENCY RESPONSE TO ENVIRONMENTAL POLLUTION ACCIDENTS

#### Article 182 .- National Mechanism for Managing Emergency Response to Environmental Pollution Accidents

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions and relevant administrations, shall establish a national mechanism for managing emergency response to environmental pollution accidents, with the following duties:

a- Determine environmental pollution risks or public health impacts in areas vulnerable to pollution from development activities or natural sources.

b- Propose measures to prevent environmental pollution risks or public health impacts.

c- Assess impacts on public health and environmental damage from accidents causing toxic substance or hazardous substance emissions into the environment.

d- Determine and coordinate the implementation of response and emergency relief measures when environmental pollution accidents occur.

2- The establishment of the national mechanism for managing emergency response to environmental pollution accidents, as provided for in Paragraph 1 above, shall be determined by legal norms of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 183 .- Emergency Response Plans for Environmental Pollution Accidents

Any person or owner of a production, business, or service site determined to pose a strong risk of environmental pollution shall prepare emergency response plans for cases of environmental pollution accidents occurring from their production, business, or service activities, as required by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

## PART 2: CONTROL OF HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCE EMISSIONS

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 184 .- Purpose

This Part aims to control hazardous substance emissions by issuing measures to strengthen hazardous substance management according to environmental safety measures, with the objective of ensuring environmental protection, biodiversity conservation, and public health protection.

#### Article 185 .- Scope

This Part applies to activities of production, import, distribution, or use of hazardous substances in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 186 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations, shall be responsible for leading the control of hazardous substance management and emissions into the environment.

#### Article 187 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare strategic plans and action plans for managing hazardous substance emissions into the environment and for emergency response to cases of accidents involving explosion, spill, or leakage of hazardous substances.

b- Prepare legal norms related to hazardous substances in products for promoting emission control and ending environmental pollution related to hazardous substances.

c- Prepare a list of prohibited hazardous substances and a list of hazardous substances allowed for production, import, distribution, or use under conditions.

d- Prepare and manage data on hazardous substances produced, imported, distributed, and used in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

e- Prepare technical guidelines for managing hazardous substance emissions and emergency response for preventing and reducing hazardous substance emissions into the environment, to be applied to sites or activities related to production, import, transport, storage, distribution, use, and disposal of hazardous substances.

f- Inspect sites or activities related to production, import, transport, storage, distribution, use, and disposal of hazardous substances.

g- Study and research levels of hazardous substance emissions in the environment, in biological samples, and in food products, consumer goods, or toys.

h- Fulfill duties as the National Focal Point for international instruments related to hazardous substance management to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a Party.

#### Article 188 .- Research, Monitoring, and Control of Hazardous Substance Emissions

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations, shall research and control sites for production, storage, and use of hazardous substances and products containing hazardous substances to promote the implementation of measures to prevent emissions and reduce risks to public health and the environment.

2- Results of research and control of hazardous substance emissions shall be disseminated and information provided to the public, in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

### CHAPTER 3: REGISTRATION OF HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCES AND CHEMICAL PRODUCTS CONTAINING HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCES

#### Article 189 .- Registration of Hazardous Substances

1- Producers, importers, or distributors of hazardous substances or chemical products containing hazardous substances shall register with the relevant ministry or institution, in compliance with relevant legal norms in force.

2- The types of hazardous substances or chemical products containing hazardous substances to be registered falling within the competence of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall be determined by a Prakas of the Minister of Environment.

#### Article 190 .- Providing Registration Information for Hazardous Substances

Producers, importers, or distributors of hazardous substances or chemical products containing hazardous substances registered as provided in Article 189 (Registration of Hazardous Substances) of this Code shall provide a copy of the registration to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources for the purpose of organizing hazardous substance data, monitoring, risk assessment, and taking preventive or response measures against risks caused by hazardous substances, and providing relevant information on hazardous substances to the public.

#### Article 191 .- Registration of Products or Consumer Goods and Toys Containing Hazardous Substances

1- Producers, importers, or distributors of products, consumer goods, and toys containing hazardous substances as provided for in the provisions of this Code shall register with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

2- Procedures for the registration of products, consumer goods, and toys containing hazardous substances as provided in Paragraph 1 above shall be determined by a Prakas of the Minister of Environment.

#### Article 192 .- Refusal of Registration Applications

Competent ministries and institutions and the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall refuse registration applications for hazardous substances and products, consumer goods, or toys containing hazardous substances as provided in Article 189 (Registration of Hazardous Substances) and Article 191 (Registration of Products or Consumer Goods and Toys Containing Hazardous Substances) of this Code if:

a- The hazardous substances and products, consumer goods, or toys containing hazardous substances for which registration is requested are on the prohibited list under the provisions of this Code or relevant legal norms in force for production, import, distribution, or use.

b- The hazardous substances and products, consumer goods, or toys containing hazardous substances for which registration is requested are on the list prohibited for production or use in accordance with international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a Party.

c- The hazardous substances and products, consumer goods, or toys containing hazardous substances for which registration is requested are found to potentially pose high risks to public health and the environment.

d- Documentation confirming the presence of technical characteristics of the hazardous substances is insufficient.

#### Article 193 .- Revocation of Registration Certificates

Competent ministries and institutions and the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall revoke registration certificates for hazardous substances and products, consumer goods, or toys containing hazardous substances if:

a- Use or emission of the hazardous substances is found to cause serious risks to public health and the environment.

b- There is a total prohibition by relevant legal norms in force or international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a Party.

### CHAPTER 4: HAZARDOUS LABELLING, HAZARD WARNINGS, CHEMICAL LABELS, AND HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCE SAFETY DATA SHEETS

#### Article 194 .- Hazardous Labelling on Packaging Materials and Hazardous Substance Transport Means

All packaging materials and hazardous substance transport means as provided in accordance with the provisions of this Code shall have hazardous labelling and hazard warnings and be accompanied by hazardous substance safety data sheets, in compliance with relevant legal norms in force.

#### Article 195 .- Placement of Hazard Warnings on Products or Consumer Goods and Toys Containing Hazardous Substances

Products or consumer goods and toys containing hazardous substances shall have hazard warnings before being distributed for sale, in compliance with relevant legal norms in force.

#### Article 196 .- Labelling on Chemical Products and Consumer Chemical Products

1- All chemical products and consumer chemical products shall have hazardous chemical labels and various hazard warnings, in compliance with relevant legal norms in force.

2- Hazardous chemical labels and hazard warnings affixed to chemical products shall include the following information:

a- Information on the product and product source.

b- Hazard symbols.

c- Warning words.

d- Hazard tables arranged by sequence, type, and hazard level of the chemical products and consumer chemical products.

e- Information on preventive measures or instructions for use.

f- Information on the producer or supplier.

g- Labels must be clear, easy to read, not easily torn, not easily peeled off or erased.

h- All the above information must be written in the Khmer language.

### CHAPTER 5: OBLIGATIONS OF PERSONS RELATED TO HAZARDOUS SUBSTANCES

#### Article 197 .- Providing Annual Reports on Hazardous Substances

Legal entities that produce, import, distribute, or use hazardous substances in production, business, or service activities shall provide an annual report to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources on:

a- Types and quantities of hazardous substances produced, imported, distributed, used, or disposed of.

b- Measures and means for managing and preventing the emission of hazardous substances or causing risks to public health and the environment.

c- Measures and means for managing hazardous substance waste and waste containing or contaminated with hazardous substances.

#### Article 198 .- Providing Annual Reports on Products or Consumer Goods and Toys Containing Hazardous Substances

Legal entities that produce, import, or distribute products, consumer goods, and toys containing hazardous substances shall provide an annual report to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources on:

a- Types and quantities of products, consumer goods, and toys containing hazardous substances produced, imported, distributed, or disposed of.

b- Implementation of hazard warnings on products, consumer goods, and toys containing hazardous substances.

#### Article 199 .- Preparation of Emergency Response Plans

Owners or managers of sites for production, storage, distribution, or use of hazardous substances or products containing hazardous substances shall prepare emergency response plans for cases of spills or leakages of hazardous substances or products containing hazardous substances and submit them to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 200 .- Obligations of Producers, Importers, Storers, Transporters, Distributors, or Users of Hazardous Substances in Production, Business, or Service Activities

In production, business, or service activities, if an accident or an incident involving a spill, leakage, explosion, fire, or emission of hazardous substances or products containing hazardous substances occurs, the producer, importer, storer, transporter, distributor, or user of the hazardous substances shall have the following obligations:

a- Immediately inform the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, competent ministries and institutions, competent authorities, or rescue forces about the event or accident.

b- Take immediate measures to reduce, prevent, or stop the spill or leakage and collect, package, and clean up the spilled or leaked hazardous substances.

c- Cooperate with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, competent ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations in providing detailed information related to the spilled or leaked hazardous substances and studying and assessing impacts on public health, property, and the environment.

#### Article 201 .- Responsibility for Degraded or Expired Hazardous Substances

1- Producers, importers, distributors, or users of hazardous substances in production, business, or service activities shall be responsible for the storage, treatment, or disposal of degraded or expired hazardous substances, in compliance with hardware hazardous waste management provisions of this Code.

2- Degraded or expired hazardous substances shall be considered as hazardous waste.

#### Article 202 .- Responsibility for Damage

Any person who causes a spill or leakage of hazardous substances into the environment shall be responsible for compensation for property damage, public health impacts, environmental impacts, and restoration of environment, in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 203 .- Environmental Pollution Fees

1- Any person involved in production, business, or service activities that produce or use hazardous substances shall pay environmental pollution fees.

2- The types of sites required to pay environmental pollution fees and the rates of environmental pollution fees as provided in Paragraph 1 above shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prakas of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance.

### CHAPTER 6: PROHIBITIONS

#### Article 204 .- Prohibition of Hazardous Substances

No person shall produce, import, distribute, or use hazardous substances that:

a- Are on the list of prohibited hazardous substances, in accordance with relevant legal norms in force.

b- Are not registered with the relevant ministry or institution.

c- Do not have labels or hazard warnings on packaging materials or have counterfeit hazardous labels.

d- Are found to pose serious risks to public health or the environment.

#### Article 205 .- Prohibition of Products Containing Hazardous Substances

No person shall produce, import, or distribute products, consumer goods, and toys containing hazardous substances where:

a- The hazardous substance is on the prohibited list of relevant legal norms in force.

b- Production or import is not registered with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

c- The hazardous substance in the product exceeds concentration limits determined by relevant legal norms in force.

d- There are no hazard warnings on the product.

e- Serious risks to public health or the environment are found.

#### Article 206 .- Prohibition of Causing Hazardous Substance Emissions

It is strictly prohibited to conduct activities that cause hazardous substance emissions into the environment, including among others:

a- Discharge or disposal of hazardous substances in public places or into public water sources.

b- Burial or burning of hazardous substances on private property, in open fields, or in public places.

c- Causing spills or leakages of hazardous substances.

#### Article 207 .- Seizure of Hazardous Substances or Products Containing Hazardous Substances

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with competent authorities, has the right to seize prohibited hazardous substances or products containing hazardous substances, in accordance with the provisions of Section 9 of this Code.

## PART 3: SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 208 .- Purpose

This Part aims to manage solid waste by issuing various measures to eliminate solid waste dumping from the generation site to the final disposal site, with the objective of ensuring environmental protection, biodiversity conservation, and public health protection.

#### Article 209 .- Scope

This Part applies to activities of import, export, separation, packaging, collection, transport, recycling, resource recovery, treatment, and disposal of garbage, municipal solid waste, plastic waste, and industrial solid waste in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 210 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations, shall be responsible for leading and strengthening the efficiency of management of garbage, municipal solid waste, plastic waste, and industrial solid waste.

#### Article 211 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft policies, national strategic plans, legal norms, action plans, and technical guidelines related to solid waste management and lead the coordination of implementation between competent ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations.

b- Research technologies related to recycling, resource recovery, treatment, and final disposal of solid waste with environmental safety.

c- Provide support and promote recycling and resource recovery activities from solid waste.

d- Provide technical guidance and capacity development to sub-national administrations to ensure efficiency in performing solid waste management functions.

e- Cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions, sub-national administrations, and development partners in mobilizing financial resources, technology, means, and materials to support and improve solid waste management from the beginning to the end of the chain.

f- Lead, coordinate, and promote the improvement of formal and public education and dissemination on solid waste management, aimed at encouraging behavioral changes in solid waste management and participation in using and paying for solid waste services.

g- Inspect and provide technical advice on site selection for establishing disposal sites, site organization, and environmental protection measures during operation and closure of solid waste disposal sites.

h- Cooperate with competent ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations in monitoring and assessing the implementation of solid waste management functions.

### CHAPTER 3: MANAGEMENT OF GARBAGE AND MUNICIPAL SOLID WASTE

#### Article 212 .- Establishment of the Committee for Management of Garbage and Municipal Solid Waste

1- A Committee for Management of Garbage and Municipal Solid Waste shall be established as a national mechanism to lead policy and strategy and provide financial and technical resource support for garbage and municipal solid waste management.

2- The roles and duties of the Committee for Management of Garbage and Municipal Solid Waste shall be determined by a Sub-decree.

#### Article 213 .- Data Management for Garbage and Municipal Solid Waste

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall establish a data management system for garbage and municipal solid waste and shall determine measures, resource requirements, or necessary means for promoting the increase in capacity and efficiency of cleaning, collection, and transport services and disposal site services for garbage and municipal solid waste to cover the entire country and ensure environmental safety sustainability.

#### Article 214 .- Action Plans for Reducing Garbage and Municipal Solid Waste Generation

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions and all relevant parties, shall determine strategies and action plans for implementing programs to reduce waste generation through waste separation at source, segregated transport, and promoting recycling, resource recovery, and waste treatment activities.

#### Article 215 .- Sub-national Administrations

Sub-national administrations shall be responsible for implementing policies and relevant legal norms on garbage and municipal solid waste management within their jurisdiction and shall have the following duties:

a- Prepare necessary legal norms to promote the implementation of policy measures, laws, national strategic plans, and relevant legal norms on garbage and municipal solid waste management.

b- Prepare action plans and budget plans for management, cleaning, collection, transport, and eliminating the scattered dumping of garbage and municipal solid waste.

c- Promote the implementation of waste separation and programs for reducing generation, reuse, and recycling of garbage and resource recovery from garbage and municipal solid waste.

d- Establish, manage, and arrange for cleaning, collection, and transport services for garbage and municipal solid waste and disposal site services.

e- Educate and disseminate to improve public awareness on hygiene, disposal of garbage and municipal solid waste, and participation in using and paying for collection, transport, and disposal site services for garbage and municipal solid waste.

f- Inspect, monitor, and assess the efficiency of expanding garbage and municipal solid waste management services.

g- Implement educational and legal measures against offenses of scattered waste dumping, abandonment of use, and failure to pay for garbage and municipal solid waste services.

h- Prepare annual reports on data, situation, and operation of garbage and municipal solid waste management for submission to competent ministries and institutions.

### SECTION 1: RESPONSIBILITY OF GARBAGE AND MUNICIPAL SOLID WASTE GENERATORS

#### Article 216 .- Obligations of Garbage and Municipal Solid Waste Owners

Any person who generates garbage and municipal solid waste shall have the obligation to follow principles determined by sub-national administrations on garbage and municipal solid waste management, including among others: separation, packaging, storage, discharge, cleaning, service use, and payment for garbage and municipal solid waste management services.

#### Article 217 .- Measures for Product or Material Users

1- Use of products or materials that may pose risks to the environment, public health, and are difficult to recycle after becoming waste shall require a fee imposed on users in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

2- The types of products or materials subject to a fee on users shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prakas of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance.

#### Article 218 .- Prohibition of Dumping and Burning of Garbage and Municipal Solid Waste

1- It is strictly prohibited to dump garbage or municipal solid waste on roads, in public places, in open fields, in drainage systems, canals, public water sources, or on private property that is not an authorized temporary waste storage site or a final disposal site.

2- It is strictly prohibited to burn garbage or municipal solid waste on roads, in public places, on private property, in open fields, or burning in incinerators without a permit from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 219 .- Responsibility for Damage

Any person who generates garbage and municipal solid waste that causes environmental pollution, public health impacts, or property damage resulting from their management activities shall be responsible for damage and environmental restoration, in compliance with the provisions of this Code.

### SECTION 2: COLLECTION, TRANSPORT, AND DISPOSAL SITE SERVICES FOR GARBAGE AND MUNICIPAL SOLID WASTE

#### Article 220 .- Organizing Collection, Transport, and Disposal Site Services for Garbage and Municipal Solid Waste

1- Sub-national administrations shall be responsible for organizing and operating collection, transport, and disposal site services for garbage and municipal solid waste with quality, efficiency, and environmental safety within their administrative jurisdiction.

2- Procedures and requirements for organizing and managing collection, transport, and disposal site services for garbage and municipal solid waste, in accordance with relevant legal norms in force.

#### Article 221 .- Obligations of Cleaning, Collection, Transport, and Disposal Site Service Providers for Garbage and Municipal Solid Waste

Any person who receives the right to provide cleaning, collection, transport, and disposal site services for garbage and municipal solid waste shall ensure:

a- Implementation of requirements determined in the service contract for cleaning, collection, transport, and disposal site services for garbage and municipal solid waste.

b- Providing services that do not cause impacts on the environment, health, or public interests.

c- Cooperate with local authorities in solving waste congestion issues.

d- Transport garbage and municipal solid waste to designated garbage and municipal solid waste disposal sites.

e- Responsibility for property damage, public health impacts, and environmental restoration caused by their service providing activities.

f- Provide regular reports on their service operations to competent authorities.

#### Article 222 .- Permit for Establishing Garbage and Municipal Solid Waste Disposal Site Services

Investment in establishing garbage and municipal solid waste disposal site services requires a permit from sub-national administrations, with prior agreement from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources on technical aspects and environmental safety protection measures of the disposal site service.

### CHAPTER 4: PLASTIC WASTE MANAGEMENT

#### Article 223 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions for Plastic Waste Management

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions, shall be responsible for:

a- Prepare draft policies, strategic plans, legal norms, action plans, and technical guidelines for plastic waste management, with the goal of reducing plastic product use, plastic waste generation, and increasing recycling and resource recovery activities from plastic waste.

b- Determine types, standards, and necessary conditions for plastic products and materials allowed for import or domestic production.

c- Determine types of plastic products or materials prohibited from use or allowed for use under conditions.

d- Determine locations or areas that are plastic bag-free or destinations that require the implementation of measures prohibiting use or allowing use of plastic products or materials under conditions.

e- Promote and provide support for the production and use of products or materials that can substitute plastic products or materials.

f- Encourage and provide support for recycling and resource recovery from plastic waste.

g- Educate and improve public awareness and participation of citizens in implementing policies and programs to reduce plastic bag use in the Kingdom of Cambodia through avoiding excessive plastic bag use, using cloth bags or reusable bags, grouping items in one bag, and using biodegradable materials.

#### Article 224 .- Obligation to Participate in Reducing Plastic Bag Use

Every citizen has the obligation to participate in reducing plastic bag use through reusing used plastic bags or using other items to substitute plastic bags.

#### Article 225 .- Incentives for Reducing Plastic Bag Use

Owners of business sites or various service providers, supermarket owners, or shopping centers are encouraged to voluntarily participate in reducing plastic bag use.

#### Article 226 .- Import and Production of Bags or Packaging Materials from Biodegradable Film or Bioplastic

The import and production of bags or packaging materials made from biodegradable film or bioplastic shall have a special tax borne by the state. Relevant parties shall apply for the special tax to be borne by the state at the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance after obtaining agreement from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 227 .- Measures for Import and Production of Plastic Products or Materials

The import and production of plastic products or materials shall have permits from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources on types and various requirements, in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 228 .- Measures for Imposing Fees on Users of Plastic Products or Materials

1- Use of plastic products or materials that may pose risks to the environment, public health, and are difficult or impossible to recycle after becoming waste shall require a fee imposed on users in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

2- The types of plastic products or materials subject to a fee on users and target locations for implementation shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prakas of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance.

#### Article 229 .- Measures for Plastic Waste Management at Generation Sites

Any person or owner of a site using plastic products or materials, including hotel owners, shall be responsible for separation, storage, and discharge of plastic waste generated as required by competent ministries or instituciones or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 230 .- Prohibition of Dumping and Burning of Plastic Waste

1- It is strictly prohibited to dump plastic waste on roads, in public places, in open fields, in drainage systems, canals, and into public water sources.

2- It is strictly prohibited to burn plastic waste in public places, in open fields, in landfills, or in incinerators without a permit from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

### CHAPTER 5: INDUSTRIAL SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT

#### Article 231 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions for Monitoring Industrial Solid Waste

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources is the institution responsible for leading the monitoring of industrial solid waste management, including among others: separation, temporary storage, transport, recycling, resource recovery, treatment, and final disposal.

#### Article 232 .- Obligations of Owners of Industrial Solid Waste Generation Sites

Any person who owns a site that generates industrial solid waste shall be responsible for their waste management, including among others: separation, storage, transport, treatment, and final disposal at sites in compliance with relevant legal norms in force.

#### Article 233 .- Industrial Solid Waste Discharge Permits

Industrial solid waste owners wishing to discharge industrial solid waste from their site shall have a prior industrial solid waste discharge permit from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 234 .- Industrial Solid Waste Business Permits

Operators conducting business in collection, transport, treatment services, and final disposal sites for industrial solid waste shall have a prior industrial solid waste business permit from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

### CHAPTER 6: RECYCLING, RESOURCE RECOVERY, TREATMENT, EXPORT, AND IMPORT OF SOLID WASTE

#### Article 235 .- Permits for Recycling and Resource Recovery Services or Businesses from Solid Waste

Providing services or conducting business in recycling, resource recovery, or treatment of solid waste requires a permit from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 236 .- Export of Solid Waste or Raw Materials from Solid Waste Recycling

The export of solid waste or raw materials from solid waste recycling requires a prior permit from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 237 .- Import of Solid Waste

The import of solid waste from abroad into the Kingdom of Cambodia is prohibited, except for solid waste as raw materials imported strictly for the supply chain of domestic waste recycling investment projects. In this case, the import of solid waste requires authorization from the Royal Government, implemented according to procedures in force.

## PART 4: HAZARDOUS WASTE MANAGEMENT

### CHAPTER 1: GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 238 .- Purpose

This Part aims to manage hazardous waste by issuing various measures to eliminate hazardous waste dumping from the generation site to the final disposal, with the objective of ensuring environmental protection, biodiversity conservation, and public health protection.

#### Article 239 .- Scope

This Part applies to activities of import, export, transport, collection, storage, recycling, resource recovery, treatment, and final disposal of hazardous waste with safety within the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### CHAPTER 2: RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 240 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations, shall be responsible for leading the management and monitoring of hazardous waste.

#### Article 241 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft policies, strategic plans, legal norms, action plans, and technical guidelines for hazardous waste management.

b- Prepare a list of hazardous waste types in accordance with international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a Party.

c- Manage data and information on hazardous waste generation sources and locations of collection, transport, recycling, treatment services, and final hazardous waste disposal sites.

d- Inspect, monitor, and assess hazardous waste management at production, business, or service sites, including import, export, and transit activities of hazardous waste.

e- Inspect and provide permits for proposed service projects for collection, transport, recycling, treatment, destruction, and final disposal of hazardous waste, and applications for discharge, export, and transit of hazardous waste.

f- Research and disseminate information on the types, hazard levels, or impacts of hazardous waste emitted into the environment.

g- Coordinate with competent ministries and institutions in promoting the preparation of action plans, material readiness, means, and resources for emergency response to cases of accidents involving explosion, spill, or leakage of hazardous waste.

h- Fulfill duties as the national focal point for international instruments related to hazardous waste management to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a Party.

### CHAPTER 3: MEASURES FOR MANAGEMENT OF BATTERY AND DRY CELL WASTE

#### Article 242 .- Obligation to Arrange for Storage of Battery and Dry Cell Waste

Residences, markets, shopping centers, entertainment venues, public buildings, educational institutions, business, service, and handicraft activities shall have the obligation to store and separate battery and dry cell waste from other waste, in accordance with guidelines of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 243 .- Collection, Storage, Transport, Dismantling, and Treatment of Battery and Dry Cell Waste

Collection, storage, transport, dismantling, and treatment of all types of battery and dry cell waste and disposal shall have a permit from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and shall comply with environmental safety rules.

#### Article 244 .- Applying for Permits

1- Any battery waste business characterized as a family business shall apply for a permit from the Capital/Provincial Department of Environment.

2- Any battery waste business characterized as small and medium enterprise or large-scale enterprise shall apply for a permit from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 245 .- Export of Battery and Dry Cell Waste

Any export of battery and dry cell waste from the Kingdom of Cambodia abroad can be done only with agreement from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and a prior import permit from the importing country.

#### Article 246 .- Prohibition of Burning or Breaking Battery and Dry Cell Waste

It is strictly prohibited to burn or break battery and dry cell waste that causes impacts or hazards to public health and the environment.

#### Article 247 .- Service Fees for Permit Applications

Service fees for permit applications for collection, storage, transport, dismantling of battery and dry cell waste for the purpose of recovering lead or other substances, treatment, disposal, and establishment of safe disposal sites shall be in accordance with an inter-ministerial Prakas of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance.

#### Article 248 .- Obligation to Report

Business owners shall have the obligation to report to the Capital/Provincial Department of Environment or the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources every 6 (six) months on the progress of collection, storage, transport, dismantling, treatment, and disposal of battery and dry cell waste at their business site to ensure environmental safety and public health.

#### Article 249 .- Monitoring and Reporting Obligations

The capital and provincial institutions in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall have the obligation to monitor and report to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector every 6 (six) months on the progress of all collection, storage, placement, transport, dismantling, treatment, and disposal of battery and small battery waste within their jurisdiction properly in order to ensure environmental safety and public health.

#### Article 250 .- Monitoring, Inspection, and Instruction

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector is responsible for monitoring and inspecting pollution caused by business activities, battery waste, and small battery waste, and for providing instructions to business owners on proper management plans.

### Chapter 4 Measures for Managing Hazardous Waste

#### Section 1 Generation of Hazardous Waste

#### Article 251 .- Notification of Hazardous Waste Generation

Persons who generate hazardous waste in their production, business, or services must notify the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector in writing regarding the type, source, and quantity of hazardous waste generated and the management measures implemented.

#### Article 252 .- Obligations of Hazardous Waste Owners

1- Persons who generate hazardous waste are responsible for packaging, labeling with warning signs, placing, storing, treating, and discharging hazardous waste in accordance with environmental safety rules.

2- Environmental safety rules shall be determined by a sub-decree.

#### Article 253 .- Permit for Discharging Hazardous Waste

1- Persons who generate hazardous waste in their production, business, or services and intend to discharge hazardous waste from their location must obtain a prior permit from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

2- Persons who generate hazardous waste in their production, business, or services and intend to treat, destroy, or final dispose of hazardous waste at their location must obtain a prior permit from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Section 2 Responsibility of Manufacturers, Importers, or Suppliers of Raw Materials, Equipment, and Materials that Generate Hazardous Waste

#### Article 254 .- Responsibility for Collecting Hazardous Waste

1- Legal entities that supply raw materials, equipment, or electrical and electronic appliances shall be responsible for organizing and implementing a system for collecting waste from electrical and electronic equipment back to be sent for destruction locally without causing harm to the environment or public health.

2- Types of electrical and electronic equipment required to be sent back to the country of origin or destroyed locally shall be determined by a Prokas of the Minister of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 255 .- Implementation of Hazardous Waste Deposits

1- Legal entities that produce or import equipment, appliances, or materials that transform into hazardous waste after use must implement the inclusion of a waste deposit value in addition to the price of the appliances or materials, which shall be refunded when the waste is returned for collection from customers or users.

2- Types of equipment, appliances, or materials required to include the waste deposit value in addition to the price of the equipment, appliances, or materials as stated in paragraph 1 above shall be determined by a Prokas of the Minister of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 256 .- Request for Business Permission

Persons who intend to operate a business in collecting, storing, dismantling, treating, or incinerating waste, or creating disposal sites for electrical and electronic equipment waste, must submit an application for permission and complete the forms at the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 257 .- Obligations Regarding Electrical and Electronic Equipment Waste

Persons who have electrical and electronic equipment waste must fulfill all the following obligations:

A- Keep the waste in a safe place and separate it from household waste.

B- Dispose of the waste at a recycling site or a collection point for electrical and electronic equipment waste as determined by the competent ministry or institution.

#### Article 258 .- Obligations of Business Owners

Owners of business premises for collecting, storing, dismantling, assembling, or recycling electrical and electronic equipment waste must fulfill all the following obligations:

A- Follow the technical guidelines on the environmentally safe management of electrical and electronic equipment waste in their business operations.

B- Prepare data on waste generated from their business.

C- Use waste collection services generated from repairs in accordance with the determination of the competent institution.

#### Article 259 .- Obligations of Persons Manufacturing Electrical and Electronic Equipment

Persons manufacturing electrical and electronic equipment must fulfill the following obligations:

A- Collect and store waste from the manufacturing process of electrical and electronic equipment by following the technical guidelines on the environmentally safe management of electrical and electronic equipment waste.

B- Install equipment to monitor environmental toxic discharge.

C- Prepare data on waste generated from their production.

D- Use waste collection services generated from repairs in accordance with the determination of the competent ministry or institution.

#### Article 260 .- Prohibition of Disposal of Electrical and Electronic Equipment Waste

The disposal of electrical and electronic equipment waste into water sources, landfills, or public places, and the burning or dismantling of electrical and electronic equipment waste that causes harm to the environment or public health, is prohibited.

#### Section 3 Responsibility of Hazardous Waste Operators

#### Article 261 .- Permit for Service and Business of Hazardous Waste

Hazardous waste operators providing services for collection, transportation, treatment, incineration, or final disposal of hazardous waste, or operating a business in buying, selling, recycling, or recovering resources from hazardous waste, must have a service or business permit for hazardous waste from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 262 .- Obligations of Hazardous Waste Operators

Hazardous waste operators providing services or conducting business in hazardous waste are responsible for:

A- Managing hazardous waste at their service location in accordance with environmental safety rules and the conditions of the service or business permit for hazardous waste.

B- Placing hazardous warning signs on means of transport and at the service or business locations.

C- Providing reports on the types and quantities of hazardous waste related to the service or business and hazardous waste management measures to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector once a year or as necessary.

#### Article 263 .- Environmental Pollution Fee Payment

1- Hazardous waste operators who conduct business in resource recovery, treatment, incineration, or final disposal of hazardous waste must pay an environmental pollution fee in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

2- Types of locations required to pay environmental pollution fees and the rates of environmental pollution fees as stated in the above paragraph shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prokas of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the ministry or institution in charge of the economy and finance sector.

#### Section 4 Import, Export, and Transit of Hazardous Waste

#### Article 264 .- Prohibition of Hazardous Waste Import

The import of hazardous waste from abroad into the Kingdom of Cambodia is prohibited, except for hazardous waste that is a supplementary raw material for domestic hazardous waste recycling investment projects. In this case, the formalities and procedures for the import of hazardous waste shall be determined by a sub-decree.

#### Article 265 .- Permission for Export of Hazardous Waste

The export of hazardous waste from the Kingdom of Cambodia is permitted only if the following conditions are met:

A- There is an import permit from the receiving country.

B- There is an export permit from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

C- There is a guarantee from the exporter confirming that the exported hazardous waste will not be returned to the Kingdom of Cambodia.

D- Formalities for exporting hazardous waste are fulfilled in accordance with international instruments related to hazardous waste to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party and other necessary conditions determined by the Royal Government.

#### Article 266 .- Permission for Transit of Hazardous Waste Through the Kingdom of Cambodia

The transit of hazardous waste from one country to another through the Kingdom of Cambodia is permitted only if the following conditions are met:

A- Obtaining permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and from the relevant ministries and institutions at least 30 (thirty) days before the transit.

B- Paying transit fees as determined by the ministry or institution in charge of the economy and finance sector.

C- Having a contract to pay for damages and environmental restoration in case of accidental leakage of hazardous waste during transit.

D- Temporary docking for transit is allowed for a maximum period of 15 (fifteen) days from the date of entry into the Kingdom of Cambodia.

E- Formalities for exporting hazardous waste are fulfilled in accordance with international instruments related to hazardous waste to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party and other necessary conditions determined by the Royal Government.

#### Section 5 Measures for Hazardous Waste Dispersion

#### Article 267 .- Emergency Response Planning

Owners of production and business sites that generate hazardous waste and hazardous waste operators must prepare emergency response and rescue plans for accidents such as dispersion, explosion, fire, or spread of hazardous waste and must submit them to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector for review and comments.

#### Article 268 .- Obligations of Owners of Production, Business, and Service Sites Generating Hazardous Waste and Hazardous Waste Operators in Production, Business, or Service Activities

In production, business, or service activities, if an accident or unexpected event such as dispersion, explosion, fire, or spread of hazardous waste occurs, the owners of production, business, and service sites generating hazardous waste and hazardous waste operators must fulfill the following obligations:

A- Notify immediately and request intervention from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, relevant ministries and institutions, sub-national administrations, and rescue agencies regarding the incident or accident.

B- Take immediate measures to prevent or stop burial or leakage, and to collect, package, and clean up the buried or leaked hazardous waste.

C- Cooperate with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and relevant ministries and institutions in providing detailed information related to buried, leaked, exploded, or burned hazardous waste and assessing the impact or danger to humans, animals, or the environment from the burial or leakage of hazardous waste.

#### Article 269 .- Responsibility for Damages from Burial or Leakage of Hazardous Waste

Owners of hazardous waste and hazardous waste operators are responsible for compensation for damages affecting public health, property, and the environment, and for environmental restoration from the burial or leakage of hazardous waste into the environment in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Section 6 Prohibitions

#### Article 270 .- Use of Unauthorized Hazardous Waste Services

The handing over, selling, or gifting of hazardous waste to persons who do not have legal permission to provide hazardous waste services from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector is strictly prohibited.

#### Article 271 .- Unauthorized Dismantling Activities

Dismantling activities of equipment or materials considered as hazardous waste without permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector is strictly prohibited.

#### Article 272 .- Activities Causing Impact

Storing, discharging, burning, burying, or disposing of hazardous waste that impacts public health or the environment or causes property damage is strictly prohibited.

### Chapter 5 Inspection of Hazardous Waste Management

#### Article 273 .- Inspection Competence

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector is responsible for inspecting all means of hazardous waste management at hazardous waste generation sites and hazardous waste service or business locations that are required to be equipped in accordance with environmental safety rules and the requirements of hazardous waste discharge permits or hazardous waste service/business permits.

#### Article 274 .- Hazardous Waste Inspection

1- In case of reports or complaints regarding the illegal discharge of hazardous waste or hazardous waste service operations that cause impact on public health, biodiversity, and the environment, or cause damage to public or private property, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with relevant competent ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations, shall conduct an immediate inspection in accordance with the environmental pollution inspection procedures stated in this Code.

2- In case serious hazards to public health, the environment, or property are found to be caused by the illegal discharge of hazardous waste and storage or hazardous waste service operations, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the right to issue a written notice to suspend the illegal discharge or production/service activities related to the immediate danger and shall proceed with legal procedures.

### Part 5 Water Pollution Control

### Chapter 1 General Provisions

#### Article 275 .- Purpose

This Part aims to prevent and reduce water pollution through the implementation of monitoring measures for related activities that cause water pollution, aimed at ensuring environmental protection, biodiversity conservation, and public health protection.

#### Article 276 .- Scope

This Part applies to the discharge of liquid waste or pollutants from point sources and non-point sources into water sources within the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### Chapter 2 Responsible Institutions

#### Article 277 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with relevant competent ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations, is responsible for leading the management and monitoring of water pollution caused by point sources and non-point sources.

#### Article 278 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in Charge of the Environment and Natural Resources Sector

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the following roles and duties:
