A- Prepare draft policies, strategic plans, action plans, legal standards, guidelines, and technical manuals related to water pollution management and liquid waste and wastewater discharge management in cooperation with relevant competent ministries and institutions.

B- Prepare environmental safety rules for the management of liquid waste, wastewater, and pollutants.

C- Investigate, monitor, and assess the status of public water pollution and vulnerable areas and disseminate this information to the public.

D- Manage data on water pollution sources, public water quality data, and pollutants dispersed into public water sources.

E- Inspect, monitor, and assess treatment technologies and management means for liquid waste, wastewater, and treatment systems at production, business, and service sites.

F- Review and provide technical environmental comments and environmental recognition for liquid waste and wastewater treatment systems.

G- Research and disseminate techniques and technologies to prevent pollution in public water areas and the hazards of pollutants to public health, biodiversity, and the environment.

H- Prepare action plans for emergency response in case of burial or leakage of environmental pollutants, in cooperation with relevant competent ministries and institutions.

I- Provide support, technical instructions, and capacity development to sub-national administrations to ensure effectively the management of wastewater treatment systems within their jurisdiction.

J- Fulfill the duties of the national focal point for international agreements related to water pollution management to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

#### Article 279 .- Roles and Duties of Sub-national Administrations

Sub-national administrations have the roles and duties of managing drainage and wastewater treatment systems within their jurisdiction in accordance with this Code and the regulations in force.

### Chapter 3 Monitoring of Public Water Pollution

#### Article 280 .- Action Plan for Public Water Pollution Management

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall prepare an action plan for preventing, stopping, and reducing public water pollution and improving the water quality of public water sources that are polluted.

#### Article 281 .- Preparation of Public Water Quality Standards and Classification

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with relevant competent ministries and institutions, shall prepare public water quality standards and the classification and index of water quality for the protection of public health, biodiversity, and for the assessment of water quality status in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 282 .- Monitoring of Public Water Pollution

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall monitor public water quality and water quality in areas vulnerable to pollution for the assessment of public water quality status in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 283 .- Provision of Information on Public Water Pollution

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall make information and reports on monitoring and assessment results of public water pollution available to the public in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 284 .- Taking Measures in Case of Public Water Pollution

In case the water quality status or self-purification capacity of any public water area decreases, which may cause water pollution problems or serious impact on public health, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall:

A- Take measures to prevent and reduce public water pollution and improve the water quality of public water areas in cooperation with relevant competent ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and stakeholders.

B- Review the establishment of separate liquid waste or wastewater discharge standards for public water areas that are vulnerable to pollution or areas requiring conservation.

#### Article 285 .- Transboundary Water Pollution

In case of transboundary water pollution, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and relevant ministries and institutions shall take immediate intervention measures in accordance with the emergency response plan and international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party, and shall determine the scope of damages caused by transboundary water pollution.

### Chapter 4 Monitoring of Water Pollution from Point Sources

#### Article 286 .- Liquid Waste Discharge Permit

The discharge of liquid waste from point sources into public water areas, onto or under the ground, and into public drainage systems must have a permit from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 287 .- Responsibility of Point Source Owners

Owners or operators of point sources are responsible for implementing the management and treatment of their liquid waste and wastewater before discharging it into public water areas and shall be responsible for the following activities:

A- Install and operate liquid waste treatment equipment according to technical specifications so that the liquid waste discharge meets the discharge standards in accordance with the regulations in force.

B- Implement self-monitoring and self-reporting measures on management and liquid waste discharge.

C- Appoint a person responsible for environmental management for the operation of their liquid waste treatment equipment.

#### Article 288 .- Notification Requirements

Owners or operators of point sources who have received a liquid waste discharge permit must immediately notify the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector in case of:

A- Abnormalities in the operation of the liquid waste treatment equipment system or unexpected accidents.

B- Abnormalities in the operation of the automatic self-monitoring equipment for liquid waste discharge quality.

C- Failure to implement the requirements specified in the liquid waste discharge permit.

#### Article 289 .- Liquid Waste Sample Analysis

1- Liquid waste samples collected during inspection by inspection officers and during monitoring by the sample owner must be analyzed by the laboratory of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or other laboratories that have received analysis certification from national or international institutions.

2- Liquid waste sample owners are responsible for paying the analysis fees for their liquid waste samples.

#### Article 290 .- Response Measures in Case of Pollution

1- In case the discharge of liquid waste or a leakage accident from a point source causes impact on public health, the environment, production, agriculture, industry, aquaculture, or public clean water safety, the owners or operators of the point source must take immediate response measures to stop and reduce the danger or impact and must immediately notify the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the sub-national administrations.

2- In case of serious pollution, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall order a partial or total suspension of the point source operations related to the environmental pollution.

#### Article 291 .- Management of Sludge from Treatment Tanks

Owners or operators of point sources are responsible for managing sludge generated from the liquid waste treatment process in accordance with the technical guidelines of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the regulations in force.

#### Article 292 .- Requirement to Pay Environmental Pollution Fee

1- Persons who discharge liquid waste from point sources and cause an increase in pollutant load in the environment must pay an environmental pollution fee.

2- Types of point sources required to pay environmental pollution fees and the rates of environmental pollution fees as stated in paragraph 1 above shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prokas of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the ministry or institution in charge of the economy and finance sector.

### Chapter 5 Monitoring of Water Pollution from Non-Point Sources

#### Article 293 .- Action Plan for Preventing Water Pollution from Non-Point Sources

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, relevant competent ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations shall:

A- Prepare action plans for prevention and stopping of water pollution from non-point sources.

B- Take specific immediate response measures for the reduction or restoration of water pollution from non-point sources.

C- Prepare technical guidelines on best environmental practices for the management of water pollution from non-point sources.

#### Article 294 .- Permit Requirements for Projects or Activities

Relevant ministries and institutions with competence to provide permits for projects or activities that may cause water pollution must review and include project or activity permit requirements for the implementation of best environmental practices in the management of water pollution from non-point sources.

#### Article 295 .- Obligation to Implement Best Environmental Practices

Persons whose activities relate to land use or cause dispersion of pollutants into public water areas have the obligation to implement best environmental practices in preventing water pollution from their project or business activities.

### Chapter 6 Marine Pollution Control

#### Article 296 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with relevant competent ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, is responsible for leading, managing, and coordinating marine pollution monitoring and has the following roles and duties:

A- Prepare strategic plans, action plans, and legal standards related to prevention, stopping, and reduction of marine pollution.

B- Prepare and manage data related to marine pollution.

C- Monitor pollution source activities from land, islands, oil pumping sites, and vessels.

D- Coordinate the preparation and implementation of emergency response plans in case of marine pollution accidents.

E- Implement education and dissemination to raise public awareness of plans and measures for response, prevention, and reduction of marine pollution.

#### Article 297 .- Cooperation with Relevant Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall cooperate with relevant competent ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations in implementing emergency response plans related to marine pollution from land, vessel transport, oil or subsea mineral businesses, and addressing transboundary marine environmental pollution issues.

#### Article 298 .- Prohibition of Waste Discharge

The discharge of liquid waste, wastewater, oil waste, and chemical waste from land, islands, oil pumping sites, and fishing equipment into the sea without treatment according to standards or without permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector is strictly prohibited.

#### Article 299 .- Responsibility of Vessel Owners

1- Vessel owners are responsible for managing waste and must take that waste for disposal on land in accordance with the regulations in force and international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

2- Vessels as specified in paragraph 1 above include, among others, cargo vessels, oil tankers, passenger vessels, tourist vessels, fishing vessels, pilotage and inspection vessels.

3- Owners of international vessels are responsible for the leakage of chemicals, fuel, and all types of waste that causes marine environmental pollution.

#### Article 300 .- Responsibility of Owners of Oil or Subsea Mineral Business Projects

Owners of oil or subsea mineral business projects are responsible for:

A- Preparing emergency response plans for cases of burial accidents, leaks, or fires of oil and chemicals.

B- Cooperating and providing immediate information to competent ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations in case of hazardous accidents.

C- Equipping means for waste storage and accident response means in accordance with the provisions of this Code and international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

#### Article 301 .- Responsibility of Owners of Public and Private Ports

Owners of public and private ports, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, shall prepare means for receiving and disposing of waste from vessels in accordance with the provisions of this Code and international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

#### Article 302 .- Responsibility of Owners of Vessel Construction or Repair Sites

Owners of vessel construction or repair sites are responsible for managing waste generated from construction, repair, and vessel cleaning activities in accordance with environmental safety rules for managing waste and pollutants as specified in Article 278 (Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector) of this Code.

#### Article 303 .- Transboundary Marine Pollution

In case of transboundary marine pollution, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and relevant competent ministries and institutions shall take immediate response measures in cooperation with relevant regional and international mechanisms in accordance with the emergency response plan, the regulations in force, and international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

#### Article 304 .- Responsibility for Compensation for Damages from Marine Pollution

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, relevant competent ministries and institutions, and relevant national mechanisms shall cooperate with regional and international mechanisms to determine the scope of damages caused by marine pollution.

2- Formalities and procedures for claiming and paying compensation for damages from marine pollution and environmental restoration shall be in accordance with the provisions of this Code and international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

### Part 6 Air, Noise, and Vibration Pollution Control

### Chapter 1 General Provisions

#### Article 305 .- Purpose

This Part aims to prevent and reduce air, noise, and vibration pollution through the implementation of monitoring measures for activities that cause air, noise, and vibration pollution, aimed at ensuring environmental protection, biodiversity conservation, and public health protection.

#### Article 306 .- Scope

This Part applies to the discharge of air pollutants, noise, and vibration from mobile and stationary sources within the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### Chapter 2 Responsible Institutions

#### Article 307 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with relevant competent ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, is responsible for leading the management and coordinating the monitoring of air, noise, and vibration pollution.

#### Article 308 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in Charge of the Environment and Natural Resources Sector

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the following roles and duties:

A- Prepare draft policies, strategic plans, legal standards, action plans, and technical guidelines related to the management of air, noise, and vibration pollution.

B- Prepare ambient air quality standards, indoor air quality standards, air pollutant discharge standards, noise and vibration standards, and the ambient air quality index.

C- Monitor and assess ambient air quality and disseminate info on the status of ambient air quality.

D- Manage ambient air quality data, air pollution sources, and pollutants dispersed into the air.

E- Inspect, monitor, and assess air pollutant treatment technology and enhance the monitoring of air pollutant discharge at production, business, or service sites.

F- Lead, coordinate, and cooperate with relevant competent ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations in monitoring noise and vibration discharge from mobile and stationary sources.

G- Research and disseminate techniques and technologies for managing air pollution from mobile and stationary sources and the hazards of air pollutants in ambient air and indoor air.

H- Fulfill the duties of the national focal point for international agreements related to the protection and monitoring of air pollution to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

### Chapter 3 Monitoring of Air Pollution from Mobile Sources

#### Article 309 .- Prohibition of Air Pollutant Discharge Exceeding Standards from Mobile Sources

The discharge of air pollutants from mobile sources that does not meet the air pollutant discharge standards, as specified in Article 168 (Waste Discharge Standards) of this Code, is strictly prohibited.

#### Article 310 .- Preparation of Legal Standards Related to Installing Equipment to Reduce Air Pollutant Discharge

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with relevant competent ministries and institutions, shall prepare legal standards to determine the types of vehicles required to be equipped with devices to reduce air pollutant discharge.

#### Article 311 .- Air Pollutant Discharge Certificate

Vehicles operating in the Kingdom of Cambodia must have an air pollutant discharge certificate from the relevant competent ministry or institution.

### Chapter 4 Monitoring of Air Pollution from Stationary Sources

#### Article 312 .- Prohibition of Air Pollutant Discharge Exceeding Standards

The discharge of air pollutants from stationary sources into the ambient air that does not meet the air pollutant discharge standards, as specified in Article 168 (Waste Discharge Standards) of this Code, is strictly prohibited.

#### Article 313 .- Prohibition of Offensive Odor Discharge Affecting Living and Public Health

The creation or discharge of offensive odors that disturb living conditions or affect public health is strictly prohibited.

#### Article 314 .- Air Pollutant Discharge Permit

The discharge of air pollutants from stationary sources must have an air pollutant discharge permit from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 315 .- Monitoring of Air Pollution from Stationary Sources

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector is responsible for monitoring air pollutant discharge from stationary sources.

#### Article 316 .- Responsibility of Owners and Operators Regarding Air Pollutant Discharge

Owners or operators of stationary sources must install and operate treatment equipment and monitoring equipment for air pollutant discharge into the ambient air at all discharge points.

#### Article 317 .- Response Measures and Notification of Air Pollution Problems

In case the air pollutant discharge treatment equipment and monitoring equipment are not functioning or cause air pollution, the owners or operators of stationary sources must take immediate measures to stop the air pollution and must immediately notify the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 318 .- Storage and Provision of Air Pollutant Discharge Monitoring Data

Owners or operators of stationary sources must store air pollutant discharge monitoring data and provide the data to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 319 .- Requirement to Pay Environmental Pollution Fee

1- Persons who discharge air pollutants from stationary sources and cause an increase in pollutant load in the ambient air are required to pay an environmental pollution fee.

2- Types of stationary sources required to pay environmental pollution fees and the rates of environmental pollution fees as stated in paragraph 1 above shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prokas of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the ministry or institution in charge of the economy and finance sector.

### Chapter 5 Monitoring of Indoor Air Quality

#### Article 320 .- Ministries and Institutions Responsible for Indoor Air Quality Monitoring

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector is responsible for:

A- Determining requirements for indoor air quality management and determining indoor air quality standards.

B- Conducting indoor air quality monitoring.

#### Article 321 .- Requirements for Construction Project Approval

In every approval of a building construction project of all types, the competent ministries and institutions shall:

A- Include indoor air quality requirements to ensure health and safety for long-term residency or work in the building.

B- Require building owners or operators to install equipment to ensure that indoor air quality meets the standards.

#### Article 322 .- Indoor Air Quality Certificate

1- Building owners or operators must have an indoor air quality certificate from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

2- Determining types of building locations and procedures for applying for an indoor air quality certificate shall be determined by legal standards in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

### Chapter 6 Ambient Air Quality Monitoring

#### Article 323 .- Ambient Air Quality Monitoring

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall monitor ambient air quality and ambient air quality in areas vulnerable to air pollution and around sources or locations with potential for air pollution to assess ambient air quality status.

2- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall make information related to ambient air quality status available for public access in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 324 .- Notification of Air Pollution Risks

In case air pollution risks are found that could impact public health or the environment, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall notify the public of the air pollution status, potential hazards, and health protection measures.

#### Article 325 .- Ambient Air Quality Monitoring Around Stationary Source Locations

Persons, business owners, or stationary source operators who have received an air pollutant discharge permit are responsible for monitoring ambient air quality around their location and providing monitoring reports to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 326 .- Prohibition of Activities Causing Air Pollution

The following activities that cause air pollution are strictly prohibited:

A- Burning forests, grasslands, stubble, agricultural waste, and burning trash or solid waste in open fields or landfills.

B- Burning waste or any combustible materials in public places, along roads, at public or private building sites, or on private land that impacts public health or affects social security and order.

C- Transportation of earth, sand, stone, or waste materials that cause dust dispersion affecting air quality, public health, traffic, and public order.

### Chapter 7 Monitoring of Air Pollutants in Fossil Fuels

#### Article 327 .- Institution for Inspecting Technical Chemical Composition of Air Pollutants in Fossil Fuels

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with relevant competent ministries and institutions, is responsible for:

A- Preparing standards for the level of chemical composition in fossil fuels that causes serious air pollution problems.

B- Preparing procedures for inspecting the chemical composition in fossil fuels that creates air pollutants.

#### Article 328 .- Permission for Fossil Fuel Import

1- The import, production, distribution, and use of fossil fuels must have permission from the ministry or institution in charge of the petroleum sector.

2- Procedures for reviewing and providing import permits shall be determined by legal standards in accordance with the regulations of the ministry or institution in charge of the petroleum sector.

### Chapter 8 Transboundary Air Pollution

#### Article 329 .- Transboundary Air Pollution

In case of receiving notification, reports, or study results on transboundary air pollution that causes harm to the environment of the Kingdom of Cambodia, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall cooperate with relevant competent ministries and institutions to find means to reduce the impact of transboundary air pollution through bilateral, multilateral, and regional mechanisms.

#### Article 330 .- Transboundary Air Pollution from Domestic Sources

In case of air pollution from domestic sources where the pollution status could cause transboundary impacts on neighboring countries and regions, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with relevant competent ministries and institutions, shall take measures to reduce the impact as much as possible and notify the affected countries and regions.

### Chapter 9 Noise and Vibration Monitoring

#### Article 331 .- Prohibition of Noise or Vibration Exceeding Standards

Activities that create noise or vibration exceeding the noise and vibration standards as specified in Article 169 (Noise and Vibration Standards) of this Code are strictly prohibited.

#### Article 332 .- Obligation to Install Equipment to Reduce Noise or Vibration

Location owners or operators are responsible for installing equipment to reduce noise or vibration discharge in accordance with the provisions of this Code or the regulations in force.

#### Article 333 .- Monitoring Requirements

Relevant competent ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations shall include requirements in the permits for building construction projects or projects that may cause impact through noise or vibration discharge to ensure compliance with noise and vibration discharge standards.

#### Article 334 .- Responsibility for Damages

Persons who cause noise or vibration discharge exceeding standard levels are responsible for property damage, impacts on public health and the environment.

### Part 7 Management of Ozone-Depleting Substances, Refrigerants, and Products Containing or Made for Use with Ozone-Depleting Substances

### Chapter 1 General Provisions

#### Article 335 .- Purpose

This Part aims to reduce and eliminate the use of ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants through the implementation of monitoring measures for ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products related to the use of ozone-depleting substances, aimed at ensuring environmental protection, biodiversity conservation, and public health protection.

#### Article 336 .- Scope

This Part applies to the production, import, export, transshipment, storage, sale, use, transfer, treatment, and disposal of ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products related to the use of ozone-depleting substances within the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### Chapter 2 Responsible Institutions

#### Article 337 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with relevant competent ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, is responsible for leading the management and monitoring of the use of ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants.

#### Article 338 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in Charge of the Environment and Natural Resources Sector

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the following roles and duties:

A- Prepare draft policies, strategic plans, legal standards, action plans, and technical guidelines on management and monitoring of the use of ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants.

B- Lead, coordinate, and cooperate with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations in monitoring and tracking the production, import, export, transshipment, storage, sale, use, transfer, treatment, and disposal of ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants.

C- Manage data and information related to the use of ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants.

D- Prepare lists of types of ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants.

E- Inspect, monitor, and assess the use of ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants.

F- Review and decide on proposals for production, business, use, and annual quotas for production, import, export, transshipment, storage, sale, use, transfer, treatment, and disposal of ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with these substances.

G- Fulfill the duties of the national focal point for international instruments related to ozone layer protection to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

### Chapter 3 Registration, Annual Quota Application, Import, Export, Transshipment, and Labeling

#### Article 339 .- Application for Registration and Annual Quota Permit

1- Persons who intend to import, export, transship, or produce ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants must register and apply for an annual quota permit at the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

2- Procedures and formalities for registration and annual quota permit application shall be determined by a Prokas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 340 .- Application for Import, Export, and Transshipment

The import, export, and transshipment of ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants must have a permit from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 341 .- Labeling on Containers for Ozone-Depleting Substances or Refrigerants

Persons who have imported ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants must notify the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector for inspection and labeling on every container containing the imported ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants.

### Chapter 4 Business and Use of Ozone-Depleting Substances, Refrigerants, and Products Containing or Made for Use with Ozone-Depleting Substances and Refrigerants

#### Article 342 .- Application for Business Permit for Sale and Distribution of Ozone-Depleting Substances

Persons who sell, distribute, or transfer containers for ozone-depleting substances or refrigerants must apply for a permit from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 343 .- Monitoring of Ozone-Depleting Substance Business

1- The sale or distribution of ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants must have identification labels issued by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

2- Sale or distribution locations must have at least 1 (one) professional staff member who has received technical training on the management of ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants from the ministry or institution in charge of labor and vocational training or the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 344 .- User Registration

Persons who use ozone-depleting substances, refrigerants, and products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants in their production and business processes must register as a user at the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

### Chapter 5 Installation, Maintenance, and Repair Services for Products Containing or Made for Use with Ozone-Depleting Substances and Refrigerants

#### Article 345 .- Registration of Installation, Maintenance, and Repair Services

Persons who provide installation, maintenance, and repair services for products containing or made for use with all types of ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants must register the service at the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 346 .- Permit for Treatment or Disposal of Ozone-Depleting Substances and Refrigerants

Persons who intend to treat or dispose of ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants must have a permit from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 347 .- Professional Staff

All import locations, sale or distribution locations, and installation, maintenance, and repair services for products containing or made for use with ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants, and conversion, cleaning, or disposal locations for ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants must have at least 1 (one) professional staff member who has received technical training on the management of ozone-depleting substances and refrigerants from the ministry or institution in charge of labor and vocational training or the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

### Book 4 Management of Protected Areas and Management of Natural Resources in Coastal, Island, and Marine Areas

### Part 1 Protected Area Management

### Chapter 1 General Provisions

#### Article 348 .- Purpose

This Part aims to ensure the sustainable management, protection, and conservation of protected areas by maintaining cultural and natural heritage, richness of natural resources and biodiversity, ecosystem functions and services, and biological evolution to support income sources, food, and daily materials for local communities, contributing to supporting economic, social, cultural, and environmental development processes.

#### Article 349 .- Scope

This Part applies to matters related to the management, protection, conservation, and development within protected areas in accordance with the provisions of this Code, as well as Royal Government policies and international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

### Chapter 2 Responsible Institutions

#### Article 350 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations are responsible for managing protected areas in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

2- Roles, responsibilities, and specific cooperation between relevant competent ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations regarding protected area management shall be determined by the Royal Government.

#### Article 351 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in Charge of the Environment and Natural Resources Sector

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector serves as the staff to the Royal Government in managing, conserving biodiversity, and using natural resources within protected areas in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

2- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the following duties:

A- Prepare draft national policies, national strategic plans, management plans, legal standards, and technical guidelines for protected area management.

B- Conduct study, assessment, and determination of protected area boundaries for registration.

C- Prepare national landmark maps, slope maps, and protected area maps in an official sense.

D- Propose the establishment or modification of any protected area according to the needs of the Royal Government or in accordance with international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

E- Conduct assessment and classification of forest resource types, fishery products, and biodiversity within protected areas.

F- Study, collect, compile, and establish information databases on forest resources, fishery products, and biodiversity.

G- Educate and disseminate information to raise public awareness of participating in preserving and protecting natural resources in protected areas.

H- Take measures to inspect, research, prevent, and suppress natural resource offenses in protected areas and build case files according to procedures in force and act as a civil plaintiff to claim compensation for damages and restoration of natural resource damages before competent courts.

I- Develop capacity and provide technical support to sub-national administrations and local communities in protected area management.

J- Conduct inspection, monitoring, and assessment of protected area management.

3- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall have a specialized unit in charge of protected areas as the staff for protected area management. The specialized unit in charge of protected areas shall be determined by an Anukret.

#### Article 352 .- Roles and Duties of Sub-national Administrations

1- Sub-national administrations have the role to manage, conserve biodiversity, and ensure the use of natural resources within protected areas under their jurisdiction in accordance with national strategic plans, legal standards, and technical guidelines for protected area management and the provisions of this Code.

2- Sub-national administrations have the following roles and duties:

A- Ensure sustainable protected area management and increase reforestation on bare forest land or vacant land.

B- Prepare and implement action plans for protected area management within their jurisdiction.

C- Take measures to inspect, prevent, research, and suppress natural resource offenses in protected areas and build case files for court according to legal procedures.

D- Prevent and take measures to stop all activities that cause damage or negative impact in protected areas caused by various activities, including, among others, clearing, exploring, encroaching on forest land, flooded forests, mangroves, seagrass, corals, harvesting forest products and sub-products, fishery products, water pollution, poisoning, use of chemicals, disposal of solid or liquid waste into water or on or under the ground, use of electric shock equipment, poisoning of biodiversity habitats, forest fires, fires in peatland areas, shifting agriculture, import of invasive alien species and animals, plants, or seeds.

E- Inspect permits and other documents specified by this Code.

F- Inspect all exports and imports of animal species, plants, seeds, and specimens within protected areas.

G- Support the use of natural resources according to the traditions and customs of local communities in conservation zones and sustainable use zones.

H- Provide principle permission and recognition for the establishment of protected area communities in sustainable use zones and community zones that have received agreement from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

I- Manage and develop protected area communities and ecotourism.

J- Educate and disseminate information to raise citizens' awareness of the importance of management, biodiversity conservation, as well as restoration of natural ecosystems and sustainable use of natural resources.

K- Mobilize resources to support protection, conservation, and restoration of biodiversity.

L- Cooperate with relevant ministries, institutions, development partners, national organizations, international organizations, civil society organizations, and philanthropists to strengthen capacity for management, conservation, and development of natural resources.

3- The Royal Government or the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the right to transfer additional functions or modify other transferred functions related to protected area management to sub-national administrations.

4- The division of specific functions, roles, and duties to each level of sub-national administrations shall be determined by an Anukret.

5- Sub-national administrations shall have a unit in charge of protected area management to serve as the staff for management and conservation of natural resources in protected areas under their jurisdiction.

#### Article 353 .- Limitation of Sub-national Administration Rights

In the absence of permission from the Royal Government, sub-national administrations cannot decide on the following matters:

A- Permitting clearing, exploring, levelling, or cleaning forest to build houses, huts, or sheds to take ownership of land or plant agricultural crops in protected areas.

B- Permitting the construction of physical infrastructure of all types in protected areas.

C- Selling, renting, providing, or transferring ownership rights of land in protected areas, whether in part or in whole.

D- Permitting mineral and stone exploration or business of all types and the removal or entry of such materials in protected areas.

E- Issuing certificates or permits for the purchase, sale, and possession of land of all types in protected areas.

F- Permitting the planting of invasive alien species or genetically modified plant species that have the potential to impact the flora of original plant species in protected areas.

G- Transforming any part of a protected area into residential land or settlements.

H- Permitting the harvesting of forest products and sub-products for economic purposes in protected areas.

### Chapter 3 Policies, National Strategic Plans, and Action Plans for Protected Area Management

#### Article 354 .- National Policy on Protected Areas

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector is responsible for preparing draft national policies on protected areas to direct effective protected area management.

2- The national policy on protected areas shall be implemented by the Royal Government.

#### Article 355 .- Preparation of National Strategic Plan for Protected Area Management

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall prepare national strategic plans for protected area management in accordance with the national policy on protected areas, in consultation with relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and various stakeholders.

2- The national strategic plan for protected area management shall be decided by the Royal Government upon the request of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 356 .- Review and Modification of National Strategic Plan on Protected Areas

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall prepare, review, and modify the national strategic plan for protected areas every 5 (five) years, in consultation with relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and various stakeholders.

2- The national strategic plan for protected areas may be reviewed and modified before the 5 (five) year period in the following necessary cases:

A- Clear scientific information and understanding of natural resources and ecosystems, including animal species, plants, geological resources, genetic resources, economy, society, and culture.

B- Imminent threats to protected areas.

C- Implementation in accordance with Royal Government policies.

#### Article 357 .- Preparation of Action Plan

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations shall prepare action plans for protected area management in accordance with the national strategic plan on protected areas, in consultation with local communities and stakeholders.

2- The action plan for protected area management shall be implemented by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

### Chapter 4 Establishment and Modification of Protected Areas

#### Article 358 .- Establishment of Protected Areas

1- The establishment of protected areas shall be determined by an Anukret upon the request of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or upon the request of sub-national administrations to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in consultation with relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and stakeholders.

2- The establishment of any protected area shall be based on research results, characteristics, value, and potential of natural resources, biodiversity, ecosystems, historical locations, geographical factors, economy, society, and environment, management purposes, natural resource use rights, and other relevant factors, as well as Royal Government policies and strategies.

3- The establishment of protected areas shall be based on at least one of the following criteria:

A- Habitats of animal and plant species that are rare, endangered, or threatened.

B- Habitats of one or more species that migrate to other areas.

C- Water source or watershed areas, topography, peatlands, and forest areas that support ecosystems, biodiversity, and create unique landscapes for recreation.

D- Special features related to history as cultural heritage, geological history, physical or biological history offered by human intervention.

E- Coral habitats, marine mud sources, or natural assets or habitats of marine species that provide benefits for research, conservation, and recreation.

4- Other criteria for establishing protected areas shall be determined by an Anukret.

#### Article 359 .- Types of Protected Areas

Types of protected areas include:

A- National Parks.

B- Marine National Parks.

C- Wildlife Sanctuaries.

D- Protected Landscapes.

E- Multiple Use Areas.

F- Ramsar Sites.

G- Tonle Sap Biosphere Reserve Core Areas.

H- Natural Heritage Sites.

I- Other types of protected areas.

#### Article 360 .- Modification of Protected Areas

1- The modification of any protected area shall be determined by an Anukret upon the request of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or upon the request of sub-national administrations to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in consultation with relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and stakeholders.

2- The modification of any protected area shall be based on research results, criteria, management purposes, natural resource use rights, land ownership rights, and other relevant factors.

3- The modification of any protected area shall comply with:

A- Importance of biodiversity, topography, geology, history, culture, and conservation of the area requested for modification.

B- Conditions and status of the area requested for modification, accompanied by maps with clearly defined scales showing locations, boundaries, and size of the protected area.

C- Management purposes and imminent threats.

D- Use of natural resources and land within the protected area.

E- Consultation results with relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and stakeholders.

F- Royal Government policies and strategies.

#### Article 361 .- Inclusion of Protected Areas into International Frameworks

1- The Royal Government may modify any protected area that has national and international importance to apply for inclusion as World Natural Heritage or regional sites in accordance with the criteria of international or regional instruments.

2- For any protected area included as World Natural Heritage or regional natural heritage, the Royal Government shall have necessary plans to ensure the management and conservation of those areas in accordance with international or regional instruments.

#### Article 362 .- Determination of Protection and Conservation Zones within Protected Areas

1- Within each protected area, there may be determination of protection and conservation of original genetic resources and classification of groups and types of forest resources, forest sub-products, wildlife, fishery products, and biodiversity.

2- Locations for protecting and conserving original genetic resources and classification of groups and types of forest resources, forest sub-products, wildlife, fishery products, and biodiversity shall be determined by a Prokas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 363 .- Maintenance and Protection of Natural Heritage

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage shall cooperate in the preparation, establishment, modification, protection, maintenance, and conservation of natural heritage sites.

### Chapter 5 Determination and Allocation of Management Zones

#### Article 364 .- Allocation of Management Zones

1- Each protected area may be divided into management zones as follows:

A- Core Zone: A management zone with high value for conserving rare, endangered, vulnerable, or threatened animal and plant species, wildlife breeding sites, and other ecosystems. Entry and exit in this zone are prohibited, except for protected area management officials on duty and natural science researchers for the purpose of protecting and conserving natural resources, biodiversity, and the environment, who must have prior permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, and except in case of necessity for national security and defense.

B- Conservation Zone: A management zone adjacent to the core zone with value for natural resource conservation, ecosystems, watershed areas, and natural landscapes. Entry and exit in this zone must have prior permission from the site-based protected area management officials, except in case of necessity for national security and defense. The use of natural resource sub-products in this zone for local community livelihood that does not significantly impact biodiversity shall be under the supervision of protected area management officials.

C- Sustainable Use Zone: A management zone with economic value serving national economic development, direct management and conservation of protected areas, and participating in improving local communities' livelihoods. The Royal Government may permit development while ensuring biodiversity sustainability in this zone upon the request of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in consultation with relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and local communities in accordance with legal procedures.

D- Community Zone: A management zone serving the economic and social development of local communities with existing activities, including residential areas, rice fields, plantations, and public physical infrastructure. Providing ownership certificates or permission for land use in this zone must have prior approval from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector in accordance with the law and regulations in force.

2- The determination and allocation of management zones in each protected area shall be determined by an Anukret.

3- These protected areas do not cover the determination and management of the APSARA Authority or other authorities to which the Royal Government has assigned duties.

#### Article 365 .- Criteria for Determination and Allocation of Management Zones

1- The allocation and modification of types of management zones as specified in Article 364 (Allocation of Management Zones) of this Code shall be based on the following criteria:

A- Management purposes of the area.

B- High potential value of natural resources in each zone.

C- Economy, society, and culture that impact that area.

D- Carrying capacity of natural resources.

E- Geographic location of the area.

2- Guidelines for the allocation and management of each management zone within protected areas shall be determined by a Prokas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 366 .- Modification of Management Zone Boundaries

The modification of boundaries of each management zone as specified in Article 364 (Allocation of Management Zones) of this Code can be done based on:

A- Clear scientific and ecosystem information, including animal species, plants, biodiversity resources, genetic resources, economy, society, and culture that have changed or are threatened.

B- Compliance with Royal Government policies and strategies.

#### Article 367 .- Map Preparation, Boundary Post Installation, and State Public Land Registration

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall prepare official maps for each protected area and define them on maps with clear reference coordinates, with the participation of the ministry or institution in charge of land management, urban planning, and construction, relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and in consultation with local communities.

2- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, with the participation of relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall study and prepare the allocation and determination of management zones as specified in Article 364 (Allocation of Management Zones) and Article 365 (Criteria for Determination and Allocation of Management Zones) of this Code and install boundary posts and register state public land in each protected area according to the points defined on the maps.

### Chapter 6 Participation and Use Rights of Local Communities

#### Article 368 .- Participation

Local communities, the public, and civil society are encouraged to participate in providing and receiving information related to management, protection, conservation, and development in protected areas.

#### Article 369 .- Natural Resource Use Rights

1- The State recognizes and guarantees traditional use rights, customs, and religious beliefs of local communities living in protected areas.

2- Traditional and customary natural resource use rights according to the family needs of local communities may take place in sustainable use zones and conservation zones, in accordance with the guidelines determined by a Prokas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 370 .- Use of Natural Resources

1- The use of natural resources in accordance with the provisions of this Code takes place in sustainable use zones and community zones. This condition does not prevent the use of natural resource sub-products in conservation zones as permitted under Article 364 (Natural Resource Use Rights) of this Code.

2- The use of natural resources shall follow management plans and technical guidelines of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector to ensure the sustainability of natural resources in protected area communities.

#### Article 371 .- Establishment of Protected Area Communities

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector is competent to organize the establishment of protected area communities in sustainable use zones under the administrative management of sub-national administrations.

2- Sub-national administrations may initiate, coordinate, support, provide principle permission, and recognize the establishment of protected area communities after receiving approval from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

3- Sub-national administrations shall study the feasibility of establishing protected area communities, defining clear locations and appropriate sizes through consultation and coordination with local communities and stakeholders.

4- Protected area communities shall enter into an agreement with sub-national administrations, and this agreement shall be valid for a period not exceeding 25 (twenty-five) years.

5- In case a protected area community fails to implement the agreement and management plan of the protected area community, the sub-national administration has the right to suspend the operation of the protected area community for review and re-assessment.

6- Sub-national administrations have the right to terminate the protected area community agreement after receiving approval from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in case the community acts contrary to the agreement and management plan of the protected area community.

7- In case it is found that a protected area community has not properly implemented the agreement, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the right to require the sub-national administration to terminate the agreement.

8- Measures for establishing and managing protected area communities shall be determined by an Anukret.

9- Guidelines on procedures and processes for organizing protected area communities shall be determined by a Prokas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 372 .- Prohibited Activities for Protected Area Communities

1- Protected area communities and/or members of protected area communities have no right to clear or level forest land in the protected area and convert it into agricultural land or private property, or sell, exchange, rent, mortgage, donate, deliver, divide, swap, or transfer the management area of the protected area community to any person.

2- Providing land for additional production for local communities shall be determined by an Anukret upon the request of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in consultation with sub-national administrations, or upon the request of sub-national administrations to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 373 .- Participation in Organizing Protected Area Communities

Philanthropists, the private sector, and civil society are encouraged to participate in supporting and coordinating the organization and operation of protected area communities.

#### Article 374 .- Preparation of Protected Area Community Plans

Protected area communities shall prepare natural resource management plans with recognition from sub-national administrations. These plans shall be reviewed every 3 (three) years or before the specified time in case of necessity. Natural resource management plans of protected area communities shall be integrated with sub-national administration plans.

### Chapter 7 Education, Dissemination, Restoration, and Improvement of Protected Areas

#### Article 375 .- Participation in Protection, Conservation, and Restoration of Natural Resources

Citizens, monks, students, civil servants, armed forces, and citizens with public mandates by election and other individuals, among others, have the duty to participate in protecting, conserving, and restoring natural resources in protected areas.

#### Article 376 .- Education and Dissemination Programs

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations shall prepare widespread education and dissemination programs in all forms regarding protection and conservation of natural resources, restoration, and improvement of biodiversity and ecosystems that have been degraded and lost.

2- Every year, on National Environment Day, World Environment Day, and international commemorative days, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations shall organize activities to restore biodiversity and ecosystems in protected areas for the purpose of educating and disseminating to the public an understanding of the value of protected areas and promoting participation in protecting and conserving natural resources.

3- National Environment Day and World Environment Day shall be organized on June 5 every year under the high presidency of the King of the Kingdom of Cambodia or the Royal Representative with the participation of the public.

Participation from the Senate, the National Assembly, the Royal Government, embassies, national and sub-national civil servants, monks, development partners, and the public. The high presidency of the King shows the Royal Will in paying attention to environmental protection, natural resource and biodiversity conservation, and sustainable development through, among others, tree planting, release of wildlife and fish, and organizing other activities that provide multiple benefits for humanity and the planet.

#### Article 377 .- Environmental Improvement in Protected Areas

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations shall restore and improve the environment in degraded areas within protected areas in cooperation with local communities, protected area communities, philanthropists, the private sector, and civil society.

### Chapter 8 Permissions and Prohibitions

#### Article 378 .- Permitted Projects and Activities

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the right to issue permits or enter into agreements or contracts for the purpose of conserving and managing natural resources in protected areas, in consultation with ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and stakeholders for the following activities or projects:

A- Conservation cooperation projects.

B- Ecotourism development projects.

C- Sport hunting.

D- Wildlife breeding of permitted species groups.

E- Study tours to understand protected area management.

F- Research projects on natural resources, biodiversity, natural heritage, and culture.

G- Other activities or projects as determined by a Prokas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

2- Sub-national administrations have the right to issue permits or enter into agreements or contracts for the above activities or projects after requesting prior approval from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

3- Procedures and forms for permit applications, agreements, or contracts for the above activities or projects shall be determined by a Prokas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 379 .- Public or Private Projects

1- The preparation of projects for constructing other types of public physical infrastructure development in protected areas must involve prior consultation with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations.

2- All projects for constructing and building private structures in protected areas must receive prior permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

3- All construction or public infrastructure projects passing through or inside protected areas must have clear and correct identification signs for each protected area location.

4- Signs or regulations on the use of signs for construction, building, or public infrastructure projects passing through or inside protected areas shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prokas of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the ministry or institution in charge of public works and transport.

#### Article 380 .- Stationing Locations of Armed Forces

1- For the installation or relocation of permanent or temporary stationing locations of armed forces in protected areas, the ministry or institution in charge of armed forces shall cooperate with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations to conduct studies and request decision from the Royal Government through the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

2- Armed forces base locations currently stationed in protected areas that are to be disbanded or relocated to other places shall return those base locations to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations for management.

3- Military exercises, training, and stationing of armed forces in the core zone of protected areas are prohibited, except in case of necessity for national security and defense. These activities can only take place in sustainable use zones and/or conservation zones with permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 381 .- Export, Import, and Breeding of Invasive Animal and Plant Species

All exports and imports of plant seeds, breeding of invasive animal species, invasive plant species, or genetically modified plant species and all types of natural seeds in protected areas must undergo study, research, analysis, and clear assessment and must have permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 382 .- Export, Import, or Exchange

All non-commercial exports, imports, or exchanges of animal species, plants, plant seeds, breeding, and specimens of those species originating from protected areas of the Kingdom of Cambodia with other countries must undergo study, research, analysis, and clear assessment and must have permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 383 .- Forestry Fires

1- Causing forest fires in protected areas is prohibited.

2- Use of fire can be excused for protected area management officials for technical purposes, fire lines, or forest hygiene.

3- Citizens, armed forces, and sub-national administrations have the obligation to cooperate in preserving and protecting forests from forestry fire offenses.

#### Article 384 .- Prohibition of Activities Causing Impact on Natural Resources

Each protected area must protect, prevent, and prohibit all clearing, exploring

encroaching on forest land, conducting business, polluting biodiversity habitats, forest fires, peatland fires, shifting agriculture, importing soil, invasive insects, and invasive plant or animal species that cause damage or impact in protected areas and some other prohibited activities as follows:

A- Moving, removing, or destroying landmarks or boundary signs of protected areas.

B- Using natural resource rights differently from the rights recognized or permitted by law.

C- Cutting, felling, uprooting, clearing, thinning trees, poisoning, or digging up tree roots.

D- Hunting, collecting eggs, young of wildlife and marine animals in all forms.

E- Water pollution in all forms, poisoning, use of chemicals, disposal of solid or liquid waste into water or on land, and use of electric shock equipment.

F- Trading, transporting, storing, buying, displaying wildlife for sale, all types of wildlife specimens, or breeding wildlife for trade and forest plants without permission.

G- Destroying natural grasslands, original plants, and wildlife habitats.

H- Releasing livestock and establishing animal farms.

I- Fishing contrary to the provisions of this Code that causes damage to natural resources in both marine and freshwater areas, flooded forests, mangroves, corals, and seagrass.

J- Establishing bases and processing agarwood, *Cinnamomum parthenoxylon*, yellow vine, or other prohibited natural resource sub-products and conducting freshwater or saltwater business contrary to the provisions of this Code and techniques leading to pollution or destruction of biodiversity habitats and ecosystems.

K- Using various equipment that causes disturbance by noise, vibration, waves, and smoke that could cause damage to natural resources and environmental protection in the core zone and conservation zone.

L- Destroying, changing, eliminating, or damaging all types of signs.

M- Taking stones, gravel, sand, minerals, digging, levelling, or excavating soil without permission.

N- Filling or pouring soil into water bodies, rainwater catchment areas, waterways, natural lakes, wetlands, lowlands, floodplains, or reservoirs without permission.

O- Importing vehicles and various machinery without permission.

P- Destroying public infrastructure.

Q- Obstructing the environmental flow of lakes, rivers, streams, creeks, or canals without permission.

R- Installing poles for fencing, or placing identification marks on land that is state property in protected areas for the purpose of possession to commit business misconduct over ownership.

#### Article 385 .- Prohibition of Processing Natural Resource Products and Sub-products

1- Processing natural resource products and sub-products, establishing and operating workshops, production sheds, wood processing factories, wood processing factories, handicrafts for processing natural resource products and sub-products, and all types of equipment using natural resource products and sub-products in the core zone and conservation zone are prohibited, except in sustainable use zones and community zones with permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

2- Processing natural resource products and sub-products, establishing and operating workshops, production sheds, wood processing factories, wood processing factories, handicrafts for processing natural resource products and sub-products, and all types of equipment using natural resource products and sub-products in protected areas are prohibited.

#### Article 386 .- Prohibition of Individual Rights

Individuals have no right to provide permission, whether directly or indirectly, for forest land, land possession, tree cutting, catching all types of wildlife, or conducting any activities to collect natural resource products and sub-products in protected areas that are contrary to the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 387 .- Environmental Impact Assessment

1- To maximize reduction of environmental impacts and achieve the strategic objectives of protected area management, all physical investment development projects in sustainable use zones and community zones, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall conduct prior environmental impact assessments in cooperation with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations for the purpose of ensuring development or investment projects achieve sustainable progress.

2- Procedures for environmental impact assessment for any development, investment projects, or activities shall comply with the regulations in force related to the environmental impact assessment process.

## Title 2 Natural Resource, Cultural Property, and Ecotourism Management in Protected Areas

### Chapter 1 General Provisions

#### Article 388 .- Purpose

This Title aims to organize mechanisms and procedures for managing, protecting, and conserving natural resources, including water, land, forests, wildlife, plants, fishery products, watersheds, minerals, tourism, and cultural property in protected areas for the benefit of the environment, economic and social development, and local community livelihoods.

#### Article 389 .- Scope

This Title applies to all activities of management, protection, and conservation of biodiversity and natural resources, cultural property, ecotourism, and development in protected areas.

### Chapter 2 Responsible Institutions

#### Article 390 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations are responsible for management, protection, and conservation of biodiversity and natural resources in protected areas in cooperation with relevant ministries and institutions in accordance with the provisions of this Code, laws, and regulations in force.

#### Article 391 .- Roles and Obligations

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations have roles and obligations for management, protection, conservation of biodiversity, natural resources, and cultural property, as well as development in protected areas, in accordance with Title 1 (Protected Area Management) of Part 4 of this Code.

2- Competent ministries and institutions have the roles and obligations for managing natural resources outside protected areas and cultural property in accordance with the sectoral laws and regulations in force.

3- Competent ministries and institutions shall cooperate and consult with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations before taking measures, withdrawing, or conducting any activity on natural resources that could impact the core zone and conservation zone of protected areas.

### Chapter 3 Watershed Management

#### Article 392 .- Collaborative Watershed Management

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, competent ministries and institutions, sub-national administrations, and stakeholders shall cooperate to manage watersheds sustainably, aiming to ensure sustainable management and use of natural resources for the benefit of the environment, economic, and social development.

2- Decisions in watershed management shall ensure consultative participation from stakeholders.

#### Article 393 .- Determination of Zones and Classification of Watersheds

1- The determination of zones and classification of watersheds shall be conducted with the participation of relevant ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations responsible for the management and use of various resources in watershed areas.

2- Watershed classification shall be based on any of the following criteria:

A- Physical characteristics and geographical status of the land.

B- Slope degree, elevation, soil type, landform, and soil erosion risk.

C- Quantity, quality, and flow level of water at all times, seasons, and years.

D- Quantity and quality of forests and other original plants.

E- Use, functions, and potential of the watershed area.

F- Rainwater catchment area within the watershed.

G- Other criteria as determined by an Anukret.

3- The determination of zones and classification of watersheds shall be determined by an Anukret upon the request of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations submitting a request to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 394 .- National Coordination Mechanism for Watershed Management

1- A national coordination mechanism for watershed management shall be established by an Anukret with the aim of coordinating management of operations and organizing techniques for managing watersheds, rainwater catchment areas, and river basins.

2- Members participating in the national coordination mechanism for watershed management include the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, relevant ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 395 .- Sub-national Coordination Mechanism for Watershed Management

1- A sub-national coordination mechanism for watershed management shall be determined by a Prokas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector in accordance with the actual situation.

2- Members participating in the sub-national coordination mechanism for watershed management include sub-national administrations, relevant departments, and local communities within or near watershed areas.

#### Article 396 .- Implementation in Watershed Management

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the role and obligation to coordinate with ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, stakeholders, and local communities to prepare management plans for each watershed based on the laws and regulations in force.

#### Article 397 .- Strategic Plans and Action Plans

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall prepare strategic plans and action plans for watershed management with the participation of sub-national administrations and stakeholders.

2- The content and types of activities related to the use of natural resources and ecosystems permitted in each part of watershed areas shall comply with a Prokas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 398 .- Monitoring, Evaluation, and Updating

1- Watershed management plans shall include regular monitoring and evaluation according to indicators appropriate for each watershed area.

2- Monitoring and evaluation of the plans shall be conducted with the participation of stakeholders in watershed areas, and results of monitoring and evaluation shall be properly prepared and disseminated to stakeholders.

3- Watershed management plans shall be updated regularly based on the results of monitoring and evaluation of the plans.

#### Article 399 .- Activities Causing Damage to Watersheds

Activities causing damage to watersheds shall be determined by a Prokas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

### Chapter 4 Conservation and Management of Cultural Property in Protected Areas

#### Section 1 Responsible Institutions

#### Article 400 .- Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, and sub-national administrations shall cooperate in preserving, protecting, and determining cultural heritage sites in protected areas.

protected areas in cooperation with relevant ministries and institutions in accordance with the provisions of this Code, laws, and regulations in force.

#### Article 401 .- Roles and Obligations

The ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage and the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector have roles and obligations as follows:

A- Cooperate and determine cultural heritage sites in protected areas.

B- Cooperate in preserving, protecting, and conserving cultural property in protected areas.

C- Study, evaluate, collect, and compile data on cultural heritage sites in protected areas.

D- Cooperate in managing, preserving, protecting, and conserving natural heritage in protected areas.

#### Article 402 .- Consultation with Stakeholders

The ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage shall consult with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, sub-national administrations, local communities, and stakeholders before making decisions to designate any cultural heritage site within a protected area as a cultural heritage resort, protected archaeological site, or heritage city as specified in Article 404 (Allocation of Cultural Heritage Protected Zones) of this Code.

### Section 2 Establishment of Cultural Heritage Protected Zones in Protected Areas
