#### Article 403 .- Establishment of Cultural Heritage Protected Zones in Protected Areas

1- Cultural heritage protected zones in protected areas shall be established by regulations upon the request of the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage or upon the request of sub-national administrations to the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage, in consultation with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and stakeholders.

2- The establishment of any cultural heritage protected zone in a protected area shall be based on research results, characteristics, value, and potential of heritage, cultural property, beliefs, history, economy, society, and culture, management purposes, and other relevant factors, as well as Royal Government policies and strategies.

3- Criteria for establishing cultural heritage protected zones in protected areas shall be determined by an inter-ministerial Prokas of the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage and the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 404 .- Allocation of Cultural Heritage Protected Zones

Cultural heritage protected zones shall be divided into 3 (three) types as follows:

A- Cultural heritage resorts.

B- Protected archaeological sites.

C- Heritage cities.

#### Article 405 .- Request for Establishment of Cultural Heritage Protected Zones

Cultural heritage protected zones shall be determined by an Anukret upon requested of the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage, in consultation with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, relevant ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 406 .- Cooperation in Establishing Cultural Heritage Protected Zones

The ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage shall cooperate with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector to organize the establishment of cultural heritage protected zones within protected areas in case such protected areas contain cultural heritage.

#### Article 407 .- Criteria for Establishing Cultural Heritage Protected Zones

Criteria for establishing cultural heritage protected zones shall be determined by regulations of the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage.

#### Article 408 .- Cooperation in Establishing Protected Archaeological Sites

The ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage shall cooperate with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector to organize the establishment of protected archaeological sites within protected areas in case such protected areas contain cultural property.

#### Article 409 .- Protection of Protected Archaeological Sites

Protected archaeological sites are absolute protection zones where various development activities are not permitted, including agricultural activities of local citizens, except for national defense purposes with a decision from the Royal Government.

#### Article 410 .- Protection Perimeter of Protected Archaeological Sites

The determination of the protection perimeter of protected archaeological sites within protected areas shall be determined by a Prokas of the Minister of the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage, in consultation with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

### Section 3 Accidental Discovery during Various Activities

#### Article 411 .- General Obligation for Notification of Accidental Discovery

Any person who learns of or accidentally discovers any objects suspected to be cultural property, ancient structures, or various ancient objects during various activities must immediately report to relevant ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 412 .- Obligation to Cease Work and Notify Sub-national Administration

Any person who accidentally discovers cultural property, ancient structures, or ancient objects during transport, excavation, construction, or other activities must temporarily cease that work

and must report and deliver those cultural properties to the sub-national administration or relevant competent institutions immediately.

#### Article 413 .- Notification to the Ministry or Institution in Charge of Cultural Heritage

1- Sub-national administrations shall report to the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage and take necessary measures to protect cultural property and sites containing those cultural properties until the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage conducts an inspection of the site.

2- The ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage shall notify the relevant ministries, institutions, or sub-national administrations to take measures to support and manage the discovered cultural property for its preservation.

#### Article 414 .- Preliminary Determination of Cultural Property Significance

1- The ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage shall conduct a preliminary determination of the significance of cultural property within 14 (fourteen) days after receiving notification or report. This period can be extended only once for an additional 7 (seven) days.

2- The ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage shall notify in writing regarding this extension to the possessor or owner of the immovable property where the objects were discovered and suspected to be cultural property, ancient structures, or various ancient objects.

#### Article 415 .- Decision on Temporary Suspension or Continuation of Work Suspension

1- The ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage shall issue a decision on temporary suspension or continuation of work suspension at any site where cultural property has been discovered as specified in Article 411 (General Obligation for Notification of Accidental Discovery) of this Code.

2- The decision on temporary suspension or continuation of work suspension shall be valid for only 30 (thirty) days after the preliminary determination as specified in Article 414 (Preliminary Determination of Cultural Property Significance) of this Code.

3- In case the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage does not issue a new decision after the above 30 (thirty) day period has elapsed, the possessor or owner may continue their work activities.

#### Article 416 .- Obligation to Record

The possessor or owner as specified in Article 414 (Preliminary Determination of Cultural Property Significance) of this Code shall cooperate with expert officials in recording and photographing cultural property or ancient objects in accordance with the guidelines of the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage.

#### Article 417 .- Measures for Failure to Report

Competent ministries and institutions shall suspend, withdraw, or cancel any permits, licenses, or certificates for any individuals as specified in Article 411 (General Obligation for Notification of Accidental Discovery) of this Code who fail to report the accidental discovery before the determination of the value and significance of that cultural property.

### Section 4 Preventive Excavation

#### Article 418 .- Requirement for Preventive Excavation

Investment project owners who receive permission from the Royal Government, in accordance with the Law on Investment of the Kingdom of Cambodia, to construct or develop various infrastructures have the obligation to conduct preventive excavation.

#### Article 419 .- Implementation of Preventive Excavation

Investment project owners shall contact expert officials of the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage or ancient archaeological units as specified in Section 5 (Legal Entities as Expert Archaeological Units) of Chapter 4 (Conservation and Management of Cultural Property in Protected Areas) of Title 2 (Natural Resource, Cultural Property, and Ecotourism Management in Protected Areas) of Part 4 of this Code, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector to implement preventive excavation work.

#### Article 420 .- Designating as State Property

Cultural heritage or various ancient objects discovered during preventive excavation shall be designated as state property.

#### Article 421 .- Expense Burden for Preventive Excavation Work

Investment project owners shall bear the expenses for preventive excavation work.

#### Article 422 .- Obligation to Record

1- Expert officials of the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage or ancient archaeological units implementing preventive excavation have the obligation to record and maintain all cultural properties or ancient objects collected from preventive excavation.

2- Movable cultural properties or ancient objects shall be kept at state museums for public benefit.

### Section 5 Legal Entities as Expert Archaeological Units

#### Article 423 .- Recognition of Legal Entities as Expert Archaeological Units

1- For national benefit, the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage shall grant rights to national private legal entities that are expert archaeological units to conduct study, research, conservation, and repair of cultural property or implement preventive excavation or rescue excavation at development project sites.

2- Procedures and conditions for registering legal entities as expert archaeological units shall be determined by regulations of the ministry or institution in charge of cultural heritage.

#### Article 424 .- Duties of Legal Entities as Expert Archaeological Units

Legal entities that are expert archaeological units shall implement:

a- Research on cultural property and cooperate with national or international scientific institutions in the repair and conservation of cultural property.

b- Conduct excavation for display or rescue excavation and prepare reports on the excavation to be submitted to the Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector.

c- Record all cultural property found or encountered and hand over all such cultural property to the Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector upon completion of each stage of the excavation.

d- Provide regular reports to competent authorities on the implementation of each excavation.

e- Collect, compile, and publish the results of research.

### Section 6 Protection of Underwater Cultural Heritage

#### Article 425 .- Underwater Cultural Heritage

Underwater cultural heritage refers to tangible cultural property, whether movable or immovable, that is submerged in water, partially or totally, periodically or continuously, such as:

a- Sites, structures, buildings, artifacts, or human skeletal remains and animal remains.

b- Ships, boats, vessels, aircraft, other vehicles or their parts, and other objects.

c- Various objects with prehistoric, historical, or artistic value.

#### Article 426 .- Reporting Obligation

Persons shall report to the Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector and sub-national administration regarding the accidental discovery or encounter of any underwater cultural property.

#### Article 427 .- Prohibition of Damage, Salvage, or Destruction of Underwater Cultural Heritage

All activities of salvage, causing damage, or destruction of underwater cultural heritage are prohibited.

#### Article 428 .- Protection of Scientific Assessment and Exploration of Underwater Cultural Heritage

The Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector shall prepare legal instruments to allow for the protection, scientific assessment, and exploration of underwater cultural heritage.

#### Article 429 .- Issuance of Permits for Underwater Cultural Property Research for Activities that Impact Underwater Cultural Property

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector may issue permits for underwater cultural property research for activities that impact underwater cultural property.

2- The conditions and procedures for granting permits for underwater cultural property research for activities that impact underwater cultural property shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector.

#### Article 430 .- Storage, Preservation, and Display of Underwater Cultural Heritage

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector shall store, preserve, and display underwater cultural heritage discovered as movable property that can be relocated or transported from its original site while maintaining its original value, in museums.

2- The procedures and methods for museum management shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector.

### Section 7 Dispute Resolution and Preventive Measures

#### Article 431 .- Representing Parties in Dispute Resolution

The Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector shall act as the civil plaintiff in dispute resolution proceedings that impact cultural property.

#### Article 432 .- Suspension of Activities Threatening or Endangering Cultural Property

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector or sub-national administration shall suspend work, construction, or clearing in cases where sites or cultural property are in danger or being destroyed, regardless of whether the site or cultural property has been registered in the national heritage list or not.

2- During the suspension of such work activities, the provisions of this Code, laws, and legal instruments in force shall be implemented.

### Section 8 Promotion and Protection of Intangible Cultural Heritage

#### Article 433 .- Protection and Promotion of Intangible Cultural Heritage

Intangible cultural heritage shall receive protection and promotion in accordance with the law and legal instruments in force.

#### Article 434 .- Measures for Protection and Promotion of Intangible Cultural Heritage

The Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector shall prepare legal instruments to protect and promote intangible cultural heritage in accordance with various international instruments related to intangible cultural heritage to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

#### Article 435 .- Value Enhancement of Intangible Cultural Heritage

The Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, and sub-national administrations shall promote the value enhancement and conservation of intangible cultural heritage through the creation of major local, national, regional, and international events such as festivals, artistic performances, or exhibitions, etc.

#### Article 436 .- Training and Transfer of Intangible Cultural Heritage Forms

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector shall encourage educational and dissemination programs in cooperation with relevant ministries and institutions with the aim of sharing intangible cultural heritage forms existing in the territory of the Kingdom of Cambodia, especially intangible cultural heritage forms that are near extinction.

2- The Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector shall establish various programs for training and transfer of intangible cultural heritage knowledge to future generations to ensure sustainability.

#### Article 437 .- Support for Research and Publication of Intangible Cultural Heritage Forms

The Ministry or institution in charge of the cultural heritage sector shall promote support and encouragement for research and publication of intangible cultural heritage forms, especially intangible cultural heritage forms that require urgent conservation and rescue.

## Chapter 5 Sustainable Water Resources Management

### Section 1 Responsible Institution

#### Article 438 .- Responsible Institution

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector has the competence to manage, inspect, and implement the provisions related to water resources and relevant legal instruments by consulting with relevant competent ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations when necessary.

2- In cases where development may cause impacts on core zones or conservation zones of natural protected areas, the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall consult with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 439 .- Roles and Duties

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall act as the staff for the Royal Government in the management, inspection, and implementation of the provisions related to water resources of this Code and other relevant legal instruments.

2- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft national policies, national strategic plans, action plans, legal instruments, and technical guidelines for water resources management.

b- Prepare draft policies for the management, conservation, and development of water resources, management plans, master plans, and data maps for water resources management.

c- Inspect and submit proposals to the Royal Government for participating in the implementation of relevant international instruments.

d- Study, assess, collect, compile, and store water resources information data.

e- Manage river basins, sub-river basins, surface water, groundwater, and the sea.

f- Educate, disseminate, and promote public awareness in participating in the maintenance and protection of water resources.

g- Develop capacity and provide technical support to sub-national administrations and communities in water resources management.

h- Conduct inspections, monitoring, and evaluation of water resources management.

#### Article 440 .- Principles of Integrated Water Resources Management

To manage and develop water resources sustainably, relevant ministries and institutions shall respect the following principles:

a- Social Equity: Ensure equality in obtaining water in sufficient quantity and quality, with special attention to groups of people who are not adequately represented in obtaining water in sufficient quantity and quality to support well-being.

b- Economic Efficiency: Provide maximum financial resource benefits from water resource use to users.

c- Environmental Sustainability: Require sufficient water allocation to maintain the sustainability of natural functions of threatened flora and fauna species and ecosystems.

#### Article 441 .- Factors to be Considered for Implementation

When implementing multi-sectoral water resources management, the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector and relevant ministries and institutions shall consider the following points:

a- All aspects of water resources.

b- Relationships between water resources and other components of the natural environment, such as land, fisheries, forests, plants, and animals.

c- Necessary conditions for efficient and sustainable water use by citizens and the natural environment.

#### Article 442 .- Cooperation between Competent Ministries and Institutions

1- All competent ministries and institutions responsible for water use, development, and sustainable management of national water resources, living aquatic genetic resources and plants, and relevant ecosystems shall cooperate in implementing integrated water resources management.

2- Cooperation in implementing integrated water resources management shall be determined by legal instruments in force.

#### Article 443 .- Contributions of Stakeholders

Competent ministries and institutions shall cooperate with public and private legal entities to manage investment, business operations, conservation, and development of water resources.

#### Article 444 .- National Water Resources Inventory

The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall maintain all national water resources inventories.

#### Article 445 .- Water Resources Data

1- Data regarding water quantity, water quality, and other water-related information collected by competent ministries and institutions shall be submitted to the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector in accordance with technical standards determined by the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

2- Water resources-related data shall be submitted to the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector at least once a year.

#### Article 446 .- Searchable Data

1- Information and data shall be provided free of charge to competent ministries, institutions, and stakeholders interested in serving the public interest.

2- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector may require payments for requests made for business purposes.

3- The information mentioned above does not include information considered as confidential information.

#### Article 447 .- Preparation of Legal Instruments

The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector may prepare legal instruments by including points such as:

a- Prohibited areas for water use or infrastructure construction.

b- Preparation of regulations related to infrastructure for withdrawing water from water resources.

c- Establishment and management of river basin authorities.

d- Priority orders for inclusion in river basin water resources plans.

e- Encouragement of research or innovative development that contributes to waste reduction and water quality improvement and increases water use efficiency.

f- Incentives and disincentives related to financial mechanisms, such as use fees and regressive rate schedules, to encourage efficient water use.

g- Establishment and management of water user associations.

h- Declaration of water use areas requiring natural protection.

i- Determination and management of flood and drought protection areas.

j- Elimination of traces or natural water flows.

k- Preparation of regulations on groundwater development and groundwater development industries, such as:
- Qualifications for well construction contractors and well builders.
- Requirements specifying that well construction machinery operates in accordance with capacity certifications in wells of the contracting company.
- Sampling, testing, analysis, study, and inclusion of borehole data and submission of such data to the Ministry or institution in charge of rural water supply.
- Well rehabilitation and the methods and requirements to be applied to well rehabilitation operations.

- Well construction and repair activities.
- Inspection, monitoring, and evaluation of well structures.
- Methods for well construction and determination of groundwater sources.
- Measures and precautions to be implemented before and during the construction and improvement of wells.
- Sanitary maintenance principles for non-operational wells.
- Repair and maintenance of non-operational wells.
- Regular maintenance and repair operations for wells.

l- Determination of service charges and other expenses for water resources management and payment for ecosystem services in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

### Section 2 Coordination of the Royal Government

#### Article 448 .- National Committee for River Basin Management and Planning

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector, in cooperation with competent ministries, institutions, and the Cambodia National Mekong Committee, shall prepare legal instruments to establish the National Committee for River Basin Management and Planning.

2- The roles and duties of the National Committee for River Basin Management and Planning shall be determined by sub-decree.

### Section 3 Water Resources Planning

#### Article 449 .- National Water Resources Strategic Plan and River Basin Water Resources Plan

The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall prepare plans for the sustainable allocation and management of water resources to meet future water needs through the preparation of the National Water Resources Strategic Plan and River Basin Water Resources Plan.

#### Article 450 .- Participation of Stakeholders

The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall cooperate with competent ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and stakeholders to prepare the National Water Resources Strategic Plan and River Basin Water Resources Plan.

#### Article 451 .- Delegation of Power

The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector may delegate power to the National Committee for River Basin Management and Planning to prepare the National Water Resources Strategic Plan and River Basin Water Resources Plan.

#### Article 452 .- Preparation and Implementation of River Basin Water Resources Plan

The river basin authority in charge of preparing and implementing the river basin water resources plan shall implement it in accordance with the provisions of this Content.

#### Article 453 .- Information for Planning Purposes

The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall collect, store, and use information for planning purposes by:

a- Regularly verifying and maintaining records of water quantity and quality.

b- Collecting information from competent ministries, institutions, and stakeholders regarding water demand and information on the impacts of water management on ecosystems, wetlands, freshwater fisheries resources, and the sea that are under threat.

c- Collecting information on current and future water use and demand.

d- Regularly improving the national observation network to collect relevant information.

#### Article 454 .- Technical Advisory Council

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall establish a Technical Advisory Council.

2- The composition of the Technical Advisory Council shall consist of technical experts with sufficient qualifications. The qualifications of technical experts shall be determined by the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

3- Technical experts shall provide advice on:

a- Draft National Water Resources Strategic Plan and draft River Basin Water Resources Plan.

b- Amendment or updating of the National Water Resources Strategic Plan and River Basin Water Resources Plan.

c- Other granting of powers in accordance with the provisions of this Code, laws, and legal instruments in force.

### Section 4 National Water Resources Strategic Plan and River Basin Water Resources Plan

#### Article 455 .- National Water Resources Strategic Plan

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall cooperate with relevant ministries, institutions, and stakeholders to prepare the National Water Resources Strategic Plan, which determines:

a- Long-term water supply security.

b- Sustainability of water resources.

c- Protection of ecosystems dependent on water flow and the environment.

d- Efficient and equitable allocation and use of water resources to prevent disaster risks and disputes.

2- Requirements for preparing the National Water Resources Strategic Plan shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

#### Article 456 .- River Basin Water Resources Plan

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall prepare River Basin Water Resources Plans to:

a- Ensure water availability for each purpose.

b- Provide a framework for sustainable water management and use.

c- Determine priorities and mechanisms for addressing future water demand.

d- Provide a framework for increasing water allocation.

e- Provide a framework for water allocation.

f- Provide a framework for the restoration of threatened or damaged areas resulting from destruction in riverine wetland ecosystems leading to loss of water resources.

g- Determine the rights to obtain water resources.

2- Requirements, procedures, and methods for preparing river basin water resources plans shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector through consultation with relevant ministries and institutions.

#### Article 457 .- Declaration of Temporary Suspension of Development

Development in wetland areas of river basins shall be temporarily suspended in cases where there is a notification regarding the preparation of river basin water resources plans by the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector. The temporary suspension shall be valid until:

a- The water resources plan is approved.

b- There is a notification regarding the cancellation and modification of the preparation of water resources plans for river basins, wetlands, and wetland areas again.

#### Article 458 .- Rules for Temporary Suspension of Development

1- In cases where development is temporarily suspended as stated in Article 457 (Declaration of Temporary Suspension of Development) of this Code:

a- All development proposal projects shall not be considered until the water resources plan is approved or until there is a notification regarding the cancellation and modification of the preparation of water resources plans for river basins, wetlands, and wetland areas again.

b- Development operations shall not be allowed in cases where such permission may cause the following impacts:
- Increase in water quantity to be withdrawn for use.
- Relocation of sites for water withdrawal for use.
- Increase in water withdrawal rate.
- Changes to water flow conditions for withdrawal for use.
- Changes to the purpose of water withdrawal for use.

2- New work shall not be allowed to start operations in temporarily prohibited areas.

3- Work that has already started operations may be allowed to proceed until completion but must have written permission from the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector and must respect the completion date and other conditions determined by the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

4- Completed work shall not be further expanded or modified.

#### Article 459 .- Validity of River Basin Water Resources Plan

The river basin water resources plan of a river basin is valid only for the river basin water resources plan of that same river basin, unless there is a separate planning arrangement for surface water and groundwater.

#### Article 460 .- Implementation of River Basin Water Resources Plan

Prepared river basin water resources plans shall be submitted for approval by the Royal Government of the Kingdom of Cambodia before implementation.

### Section 5 Rights, Permits, and Prohibitions in Water Resources Use

#### Article 461 .- Water Resources Use Rights

Persons have the right to use water resources without requiring a permit for the following points:

a- Basic human needs, such as drinking, washing, bathing, hygiene, watering gardens, and vegetable crops for other family purposes.

b- Firefighting and other emergency relief purposes.

#### Article 462 .- Protection of Legal Rights of Other Persons

The withdrawal and use of water resources shall not affect the legal rights of other persons.

#### Article 463 .- Activities Requiring Permits

1- All activities requiring permits shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector and relevant ministries and institutions.

2- With written approval from the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector and relevant ministries and institutions, the following activities are permitted:

a- Water use, water diversion, and water withdrawal not in accordance with the purposes of Article 461 (Water Resources Use Rights) and Article 462 (Protection of Legal Rights of Other Persons) of this Code.

b- Construction or operation for infrastructure leading to water use, water diversion, and water withdrawal not in accordance with the purposes of Article 461 (Water Resources Use Rights) and Article 462 (Protection of Legal Rights of Other Persons) of this Code.

c- Extraction of sand, land, stone, gravel, minerals, oil, and gas from the bottom or surface of the sea, rivers, streams, canals, creeks, or lakes.

d- Filling, arranging water flow systems, digging canals, diverting water of rivers, streams, canals, creeks, lakes, canals, reservoirs, clean water sources, and natural reservoirs.

#### Article 464 .- Limits on Use, Water Diversion, and Water Withdrawal

The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector may prepare legal instruments to determine limits on water use, water diversion, and water withdrawal.

#### Article 465 .- Approval of Permits for Water Resources Use

The approval of permits for water resources use shall comply with the requirements determined by this Code, laws, and legal instruments in force.

#### Article 466 .- Issuance of Infrastructure Permits

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall issue permits related to water resources use infrastructure in accordance with river basin water resources plans.

2- Requirements, procedures, and methods for issuing permits related to water resources use infrastructure shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

#### Article 467 .- Water Rights Inventory

The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall maintain a water rights inventory that can be made available for public search.

#### Article 468 .- Conditions for Water Rights

Water use, water diversion, and water withdrawal shall be conducted in accordance with the conditions for water rights determined by legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

#### Article 469 .- Legal Rights to Obtain Water

1- Owners or occupiers may apply for water rights to the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector to use water from:

a- Various water sources, lakes, or groundwater located on or adjacent to any land.

b- Groundwater reservoirs.

c- Water flow through the land.

d- Infrastructure implemented under infrastructure permits for supplying water to the relevant land.

2- Requirements, procedures, and methods for applying for water rights shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

#### Article 470 .- Minimum Environmental Flow

The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall ensure sufficient water allocation at a minimum level for environmental flow to respond to ecosystem functions.

#### Article 471 .- Declaration of Water Rights

Before each cultivation date, the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall declare the water rights for the following year in accordance with priority types and water allocation plans determined in the river basin water resources plans.

#### Article 472 .- Formal Adjustment of Quantity

1- The formal quantity for water allocation and other conditions may be adjusted at the end of the implementation of the river basin water resources plan.

2- Formal adjustment of the quantity for water allocation and other conditions mentioned above shall not be subject to compensation for damages.

#### Article 473 .- Water Supply Agreement in Accordance with Permits for Infrastructure

In cases where there is a permit to use water resources for supply on infrastructure operating under an infrastructure permit, the holder of the water resources use permit and the holder of the infrastructure permit shall enter into a water resources supply agreement together, unless both parties are the same legal entity.

#### Article 474 .- Standard Water Supply Agreement

1- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector shall prepare standard water supply agreements for the storage and/or distribution of water to water license holders and infrastructure permit holders in accordance with the provisions on water rights.

2- Standard water supply agreements may vary from one location to another.

#### Article 475 .- Request for Standard Water Supply Agreement

Standard water supply agreements for locations specifically for water rights shall be implemented in accordance with water rights, unless there is a standard water supply agreement that has:

a- Been agreed upon between the infrastructure permit holder and the water rights holder.

b- Been submitted to the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

#### Article 476 .- Application for Water Licenses or Permits

1- Persons may apply for water licenses or permits to the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector to perform the following activities:

a- Transporting water from lakes, wetlands, lowlands, springs, or reservoirs.

b- Filling or reclaiming natural lakes, wetlands, lowlands, springs, or reservoirs.

c- Well construction.

d- Installing groundwater pumps.

2- Requirements, procedures, and methods for applying for technical water licenses, permits, and certificates shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

#### Article 477 .- Evidence of Water Licenses, Permits, and Certificates

When conducting inspections, competent officials may require persons to show water licenses, permits, and certificates in cases of suspicion as follows:

a- Construction, deepening, widening, or installation of casing pipes in existing well construction operations.

b- Removal, replacement, exchange, or repair of casing pipes and screen pipes in existing well construction operations.

c- Discontinuation of borehole well construction operations.

d- Installation, replacement, exchange, or removal of motors or water pumps from existing wells.

#### Article 478 .- Requirements for Reporting

1- Persons performing work related to borehole wells for business purposes shall prepare reports to the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

2- Details for inclusion in reports shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

#### Article 479 .- Activity Records

1- Water license holders, permit holders, and certificate holders shall maintain information on activities performed in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

2- Water license holders, permit holders, and certificate holders shall provide the information stated in the above paragraph to the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector upon request.

#### Article 480 .- Permits for Water Supply and Use

Persons shall not be allowed to supply or use water unless they obtain permission from the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector in accordance with the provisions of this Code, laws, and relevant legal instruments.

#### Article 481 .- Violation of Permits

Permit holders shall not violate the conditions as determined in the permit.

#### Article 482 .- Right of Access to Immovable Property for Competent Officials

Competent officials of the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector have the right to enter the land of owners or occupiers to perform duties in technical studies, assessments, or monitoring, except where:

a- The Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector has not provided prior written notice to the owner or occupier of that land.

b- The competent official has not provided proof of identity and does not have a permit from the Ministry or institution in charge of the water resources sector.

#### Article 483 .- Exceptions to Water Rights

Persons have the right to use water resources without requiring a permit for the following activities:

a- Irrigation of land for an entire area of less than 10 (ten) hectares.

b- Clean water supply using less than 40 (forty) cubic meters of water per day.

c- Hydroelectric power using river flow or hydroelectric technology that does not require impoundment, diversion, or water withdrawal for use.

#### Article 484 .- Determination of Boundaries and Coastline

The determination of boundaries and coastline and components of river basins shall be determined by sub-decree.

## Chapter 6 Sustainable Forestry Resources Management

### Section 1 Responsible Institution

#### Article 485 .- Responsible Ministry and Institution

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall be responsible for managing, protecting, conserving, and inspecting forestry resources sustainably within natural protected areas.

2- The management of forestry resources, wildlife resources, and forest land outside natural protected areas is the competence of the Ministry or institution in charge of the forestry sector.

#### Article 486 .- Roles and Duties

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations have the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft policies, strategic plans, action plans, legal instruments, and strengthen the implementation of laws related to forestry resources and ecosystem management.

b- Manage, protect, conserve, and inspect forestry resources sustainably, including the preparation of plans for sustainable forest energy source use, issuance of permits, management, and inspection of sustainable forest technical principles.

c- Study and collect forestry data related to scientific, economic, social, and environmental goals to establish database libraries and create management plans for sustainable forestry management.

d- Conduct full and timely assessments for all forest resources and other developments related to activities that may have environmental and social impacts before granting approval for those activities.

e- Prepare proposals for the creation, modification, and cancellation of sustainable forestry management in accordance with legal instruments in force and international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

f- Promote education and disseminate information about the importance of management, maintenance, and protection of forest resources to the public to encourage participation in the conservation and protection of natural resources in sustainably managed forest areas.

g- Restore forest ecosystems through planting threatened tree species to promote and achieve the reduction of emissions from forest loss and degradation.

h- Clearly define the legal status of all lands designated as sustainably managed forest lands.

i- Define forest boundaries and register forest lands, analyze, store data, and update data for use in sustainable forestry management.

j- Establish and implement measures in cooperation with other competent ministries and institutions to protect forests systematically from unauthorized forest resource use or illegal land possession and other illegal activities.

k- Consult on public road construction projects in forest areas.

l- Publicly disseminate and implement with transparency all aspects of sustainable forestry management.

m- Participate in international forest management and product certification programs.

n- Cooperate with ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and local communities to strengthen sustainable forestry management.

o- Investigate, research, prevent, suppress, build cases, and act as the plaintiff before competent courts.

p- Strengthen capacity for timely response to forestry offenses.

q- Compile, manage, and disseminate forestry resource database systems.

2- The allocation of functions, roles, and specific duties to each level of sub-national administration shall be determined by sub-decree.

#### Article 487 .- Duties in Conservation and Reforestation

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall improve the protection, conservation, and restoration of natural environmental values and ecosystem services within sustainably managed forest areas by protecting and reducing negative impacts on:

a- Forests that impact catchment areas.

b- Environmental quality of soil, water, and air.

c- Quality of ecology and ecosystems.

#### Article 488 .- Other Duties

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall ensure:

a- Timber is harvested at or below sustainable levels.

b- Processing, services, and added value related to timber are used at authorized locations.

c- Participation in international forest product programs and sustainable forest management certifications supported by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and relevant ministries and institutions.

d- Implementation of stockpiling procedures for products not used domestically.

### Section 2 Forest Classification

#### Article 489 .- Forests

All types of forests that are components of natural resources shall be considered state property, except where there are special provisions in this Code, laws, and relevant legal instruments.

#### Article 490 .- Forest Classification

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall identify, classify, and map forests in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

2- Forest classification shall be based on updated mapping data and scientific assessments as follows:

a- Forestry ecological, economic, and social functions.

b- Forest diversity.

c- Functions and importance of the area.

d- Potential areas for forest carbon stocks and targets for reducing greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation, forest degradation, and sustainable forest management, conservation, and enhancement of forest carbon stocks through planting programs and other programs.

e- Potential areas for maintaining water habitats and wetlands.

f- Consultation with local communities.

g- Other relevant conditions in accordance with legal instruments.

3- Sustainably managed forest resources are classified into private forests, sustainable production forests, reforestation forests, reserve forests, and economic land concessions:

a- Private Forest: Any type of forest land located on land owned by a private individual.

b- Sustainable Production Forest: Any type of forest land located on state public land, such as planted forest plantations, seed orchards, community forests, concession forests, and all forest lands where reforestation can be conducted.

c- Reforestation Forest: Any type of forest land located on state public land and on lost and degraded forest land and reforestation land established through natural or artificial forest regeneration.

d- Reserve Forest: Any type of forest land located on state public land that protects systems, habitats, and natural resources, such as:
- Wildlife habitats.
- Forests for research and study.
- Forests protecting wetland areas and water sources.
- Botanical gardens.
- Recreational forests.
- Religious belief forests.
- Reserve forest land for future conservation.

4- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations may establish additional forest classifications for sustainably managed forests, such as recreational forests, production community forests, experimental forests, and reforestation forests. Additional forest classifications shall receive assessments in accordance with sustainability principles, research importance, and management plan goals.

#### Article 491 .- Changing Forest Classification

1- All proposals to modify forest classification or any part of forest land that is sustainably managed shall meet the following requirements:

a- Scientific and socio-economic reasons for the classification modification.

b- Efforts made to maintain the classification status during implementation.

c- Classification modification features shall promote the livelihoods and traditional rights of local communities.

d- Expected results of increasing conservation value and ecosystems, or by any means that lead to the loss of conservation value and ecosystems shall be compensated for those damages.

e- Appropriate consultation with relevant stakeholders and affected local communities.

f- Other information mentioned above shall be adjusted in accordance with the proposal.

2- All proposals to modify forest classification allocation or any part of forest land that is sustainably managed shall be implemented in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

3- Approval of forest classification modification shall receive written permission from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 492 .- Mapping for Sustainable Forestry Management

In implementing forestry resource management activities, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations are responsible for preparing maps as follows:

a- Forest resource assessment maps and forest land use change maps.

b- Forest classification maps.

c- Forest carbon stock assessment maps.

d- Rainfall area maps, catchment area maps, and catchment area classification maps.

e- Forest biodiversity status and vulnerable catchment area maps.

f- Forest plantations and seed orchard maps.

g- Timber processing workshop and factory maps.

h- Maps of road construction through forest areas and other potential maps.

#### Article 493 .- Forest Land Use Planning and Registration

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations are responsible for leading the production of data and forest land use maps for sustainable forestry management.

2- The process of producing data and forest land use maps for sustainable forestry management shall be conducted through consultation with relevant stakeholders and shall include studies, assessments, and the determination of forest boundaries for national forest land registration based on forest classification and land use maps.

#### Article 494 .- Data Management

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations, through consultation with relevant stakeholders, are responsible for leading, establishing, collecting, and compiling forest resource baseline information and data, and storing this data for preparing sustainable forestry management plans.

### Section 3 Preparation of Land Use Plans and Sustainable Forestry Management Plans

#### Article 495 .- Preparation of Land Use Plans and Sustainable Forestry Management Plans

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall prepare 5 (five)-year sustainable forest land use plans and 5 (five)-year sustainable forestry management plans, or as necessary.

2- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall organize separate and special consultations for areas where local communities reside or areas related to local community interests to confirm appreciation for interests and rights of a traditional and customary nature.

#### Article 496 .- Sustainable Forest Land Use Plan

Sustainable forestry resource use plans shall consider the following factors:

a- Types, quantity, and quality of forests based on forest resource data.

b- Forest ecological value.

c- Locations of local communities and livelihood requirements.

d- External administrative operations and investments.

### Section 4 Permits

#### Article 497 .- Relevant Activities

Persons requesting to perform activities in sustainably managed forest areas shall apply for permits from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations as follows:

a- Extraction of forest products and non-timber forest products for commercial use.

b- Management of forest products and non-timber forest products for commercial use.

c- Collection of forest products and forest product derivatives for commercial purposes.

d- Transportation of forest products and non-timber forest products.

e- Export of forest products and non-timber forest products.

f- Forest use for scientific or technical research.

g- Establishing timber storage sites for sale and distribution of forest products and non-timber forest products.

h- Establishing forest industry bases, sawmills, and processing workshops for forest products and non-timber forest products.

i- Establishing various charcoal kilns that use forest products and non-timber forest products as raw materials.

j- Production of charcoal and firewood.

k- Performance of various commercial activities in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

l- All commercial activities located within sustainable production forests shall have prior permits.

#### Article 498 .- Review of Permit Applications

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations shall review permit applications by respecting the following conditions:

a- Consistency with the objectives of surrounding landscape conservation.

b- Support and assistance in enhancing the livelihoods of local communities in the area.

c- Contribution to the protection of biodiversity values and ecosystems.

d- Consistency with national and sub-national land use plans and sustainable forestry management plans.

e- Compliance with guidelines in the project preparation, project operation, and project closure phases of competent ministries and institutions.

f- Fulfilling other conditions according to the actual situation.

### Section 5 Traditional Use Rights

#### Article 499 .- State Responsibility Obligations

The state shall recognize and guarantee traditional use rights, customs, beliefs, religions, and livelihoods of local communities located within or near sustainably managed forest areas in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 500 .- Traditional Use Rights

1- Traditional use rights of local communities for forest products and non-timber forest products shall not require permits.

2- Traditional use rights include:

a- Collecting dead wood, picking wild fruits, collecting honey, tapping resin, and collecting other non-timber forest products.

b- Cutting grass and grazing livestock.

c- Use of forest products and non-timber forest products in accordance with family and traditional use.

d- The right to exchange or sell non-timber forest products traditionally within local communities shall not require permits. Persons intending to commercially trade in non-timber forest products with local communities shall have permits as stated in Article 497 (Relevant Activities) of this Code.

### Section 6 Sustainable Forestry Management and Commercial Reporting Plans

#### Article 501 .- Scope of Forestry Commercial Management Plans

Forestry commercial management plans established for all forestry commercial activities that are sustainably managed shall be proportional to the size, density, and risk of those commercial activities. Forestry commercial management plans shall be implemented and updated regularly based on survey, inspection, monitoring, and assessment information to promote sustainable forestry management.

#### Article 502 .- Contents of Forestry Commercial Management Plans

Sustainable forestry commercial management plans shall:

a- Define forestry commerce for specific areas in accordance with commercial activities, including descriptions and locations of planned specific activities.

b- Define goals, objectives, and results of commercial activities related to the resources used and scientific, social, and environmental indicators.

c- Define roles and responsibilities for relevant stakeholders.

d- Define quotas for harvesting forest products and non-timber forest products.

e- Define dates and timing for implementation.

f- Define investment plans.

g- Define appropriate management implementation based on relevant legal instruments.

h- Define new or modified implementation plans.

i- Define procedures for forest product certificates, if applicable.

j- Define forest product processing and transportation.

k- Define methods for monitoring and assessment and report preparation.

#### Article 503 .- Forestry Commercial Management Reports

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall prepare annual forestry commercial management reports with the participation of relevant ministries and institutions.

2- Forestry commercial management plans and reports shall be made public in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

### Section 7 Forestry Royalties and Premiums

#### Article 504 .- Payment of Forestry Royalties and Premiums

1- Persons harvesting forest products and non-timber forest products for commercial purposes in sustainably managed forest areas shall pay forestry royalties and premiums to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

2- Rules for quality and quantity assessment shall be evaluated before transportation.

#### Article 505 .- Allocation of Forestry Royalties and Premiums

The allocation of collected forestry royalties and premiums shall be implemented in accordance with the joint proclamation of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry or institution in charge of the Economic and Financial sector.

#### Article 506 .- Exceptions to Forestry Royalties and Premiums Payments

Forestry royalties and premiums payments shall be exempted in the following cases:

a- Harvesting forest products and non-timber forest products from private forests.

b- Traditional use by local communities.

c- Scientific purposes.

#### Article 507 .- Deferral of Forestry Royalties and Premiums Payments

1- In cases where permit holders do not pay forestry royalties and premiums according to the dates determined in the agreement with the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations, those forest products and non-timber forest products shall be impounded.

2- Deferral of forestry royalties and premiums payments shall be in accordance with the criteria stated in the proclamation of the Minister in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 508 .- Procedures for Forestry Royalties and Premiums Payments

Procedures for forestry royalties and premiums payments shall be determined by the joint proclamation of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry or institution in charge of the Economic and Financial sector.

### Section 8 Sustainable Forestry Management Mechanism

#### Article 509 .- Mechanism for Reducing Greenhouse Gas Emissions from Deforestation and Forest Degradation

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions to implement the mechanism for reducing greenhouse gas emissions from deforestation and forest degradation (REDD+) to promote sustainable forestry management, increase forest carbon stocks, and enhance the livelihoods of local communities.

#### Article 510 .- Reforestation

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall promote and enhance reforestation in forest land areas to improve ecosystem values and catchment areas within sustainably managed forest areas.

#### Article 511 .- Tree Planting

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall prepare technical guidelines to determine rules on tree planting with the aim of encouraging persons to plant and maintain trees for sustainable forestry management and consistent with environmental, economic, and social goals.

#### Article 512 .- Forest Fire Prevention and Control

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall manage and control forest fires by establishing a Committee for Combating Forest Fires for implementation within forest areas.

2- Citizens, armed forces, and sub-national administrations have the obligation to protect and maintain forests and prevent forest fires.

#### Article 513 .- Issuance of Forest Certification in Accordance with International Standards and Management

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources may prepare proclamations related to the issuance of forest certificates in accordance with international standards, which include:

a- Implementation of monitoring and assessment procedures for carbon.

b- Timber and wood products, and non-timber forest products obtained from the demolition of houses or warehouses that cannot be used domestically.

#### Article 514 .- Nationwide Permit Issuance and Monitoring System

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources may establish a permit issuance and monitoring system for the production and use of all forest products originating from sustainably managed forest areas.

2- The establishment of the permit issuance and monitoring system and requirements for persons conducting operations shall be in accordance with permits as stated in this Code.

## Chapter 7 Wildlife Protection

### Section 1 Responsible Institution

#### Article 515 .- Responsible Ministry and Institution

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall be responsible for the protection, conservation, and management of wildlife within natural protected areas. The management of wildlife outside natural protected areas is the competence of the Ministry or institution in charge of the forestry sector.

#### Article 516 .- Roles and Duties

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations have the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft policies, strategic plans, action plans, and legal instruments and strengthen the implementation of laws related to wildlife management.

b- Study, monitor, and assess the economic, social, cultural, and environmental aspects of wildlife.

c- Study, assess, and manage native wildlife species or species not naturally present in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

d- Prepare classification lists, manage, and protect wildlife, especially threatened species.

e- Prepare national registries for threatened wildlife species listed in the Appendices of the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora (CITES).

f- Implement measures and mechanisms for the conservation, management, and use of wildlife and sustainable ecosystem management.

g- Restore threatened wildlife species and ecosystems that may be heading towards extinction.

h- Compile, manage, and disseminate wildlife database systems and ecosystem information.

2- The allocation of functions, roles, and specific duties to each level of sub-national administration shall be determined by sub-decree.

#### Article 517 .- Cooperation of Competent Ministries and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions in sharing information and data on wildlife and ecosystem offenses that are under threat as follows:

a- Results of national and international investigations regarding the trade and transportation of wildlife and illegal timber species.

b- Status reports on threatened wildlife species as stated in this Code and illegal timber species as stated in relevant regulations.

c- Legal complaint documents related to threatened wildlife and ecosystems.

### Section 2 Wildlife Classification

#### Article 518 .- Wildlife

All types of wildlife that are components of natural resources shall be considered state property, except where there are special provisions in this Code, laws, and relevant legal instruments.

#### Article 519 .- Wildlife Classification List

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall coordinate and cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions to prepare records and manage wildlife classification lists, including threatened species, for management and protection. The wildlife classification list shall be determined by a proclamation of the Minister in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 520 .- Wildlife Classifications

1- Wildlife included in the classification list for each species that is endangered is divided into 8 classifications as follows:

a- Classification 1: Extinct species.

b- Classification 2: Extinct in the wild species.

c- Classification 3: Critically endangered species.

d- Classification 4: Endangered species.

e- Classification 5: Vulnerable species.

f- Classification 6: Near threatened species.

g- Classification 7: Not yet threatened species.

h- Classification 8: Data deficient species.

2- The above classifications shall be made based on the actual situation through consultation with relevant stakeholders and technical experts.

3- The definition and criteria for each wildlife classification category above shall be based on the actual situation and scientific characteristics.

4- The wildlife classification list shall be updated based on the actual situation and criteria in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 521 .- Upgrading Wildlife Classification

All wildlife species may have their classification upgraded based on the grounds of risk level, threat to wildlife status, and cultural heritage value or economic importance, in accordance with the classifications stated in Article 520 of this Code.

### Section 3 Prohibited Activities Affecting Wildlife Habitats

#### Article 522 .- Prohibited Activities Affecting Wildlife Habitats

Destructive activities or activities that alter the natural habitat of threatened wildlife species directly or indirectly are prohibited, except with permission from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations, in accordance with the provisions stated in Chapter 7 (Wildlife Protection), Title 2 (Natural Resource Management, Cultural Heritage, and Natural Tourism in Natural Protected Areas) of Book 4 of this Code.

#### Article 523 .- Prohibited Activities Concerning Threatened Wildlife Species

Collection, stockpiling, destruction, use, sale, commerce, or transportation of wildlife or wildlife specimens that are threatened as stated in the national wildlife classification list is prohibited, except with permission from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations in accordance with the provisions of Chapter 7 (Wildlife Protection) of Title 2 (Natural Resource Management, Cultural Heritage, and Natural Tourism in Natural Protected Areas) of Book 4 of this Code.

### Section 4 Permit Issuance, Collection, and Commerce

#### Article 524 .- Permits for International Activities

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall issue permits related to international activities for wildlife species listed in classifications 3 to 8 of Article 520 (Wildlife Classifications) of this Code and their specimens as follows:

a- Processing.

b- Transportation.

c- Exhibition.

d- Stockpiling.

e- State gifts.

f- Participation in international activities held in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 525 .- Permit Requirements

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations may issue permits for trading or transporting wildlife species listed in classifications 6 and 7 of Article 520 (Wildlife Classifications) of this Code for family and art purposes.

2- The issuance of any permit shall be appropriately limited and timed for that activity, without affecting the restoration potential.

#### Article 526 .- Permit Exemptions

Permits are not required for the following activities:

a- Collection, transportation, and use of wildlife species in classification 7 for family and traditional use.

b- Harvesting forest products and non-timber forest products from plantations on private land.

#### Article 527 .- Conditions for Not Issuing Permits

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall not issue permits in cases where there is insufficient scientific information.

#### Article 528 .- Permit Issuance for Scientific Research Purposes

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations may issue special permission for collecting wildlife species listed in classifications 3 to 8 of Article 520 (Wildlife Classifications) of this Code from any location for scientific research purposes, provided that such collection does not cause negative impacts on wildlife conservation within and outside the Kingdom of Cambodia.

2- This permit may be issued upon request from academic institutions, research institutions, national or international wildlife conservation organizations for research studies or zoos.

#### Article 529 .- Insufficient Scientific Information

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations may deny special permits as stated in Article 528 (Permit Issuance for Scientific Research Purposes) of this Code in cases where there is insufficient scientific information to evaluate the impact on threatened wildlife species.

2- In cases where permits are issued for scientific purposes but scientific information is insufficient for clear determination, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations may make decisions to limit the extent and activities of such collection, which could cause the extinction of threatened wildlife species or cause irreparable damage.

#### Article 530 .- Wildlife Collection Permits

Applications for wildlife collection permits shall be submitted to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 531 .- Suspension or Cancellation of Permits

In necessary cases, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations have the right to temporarily suspend with specific duration or cancel permits based on new data, scientific information, or newly discovered threats to threatened wildlife species and habitats.

### Section 5 Wildlife Management and Restoration Plans

#### Article 532 .- Wildlife Management Plans

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall prepare short, medium, and long-term wildlife management and restoration plans and wildlife inventories for threatened species originating within their jurisdiction as determined in the wildlife classifications stated in Article 520 (Wildlife Classifications) of this Code.

2- Wildlife management and restoration plans shall be prepared for short, medium, and long-term durations and may cover multiple wildlife species and include bird species as well.

3- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall cooperate with relevant ministries, institutions, and technical experts to prepare management and restoration plans and inventories for threatened wildlife.

4- Wildlife management and restoration plans and inventories may be integrated into Natural Protected Area management plans.

#### Article 533 .- Wildlife Management and Restoration Plans

Wildlife management and restoration plans for threatened species shall define specific contents as follows:

a- Goals, criteria, measures, and expected results.

b- Management and conservation activities to ensure ecosystem functions and roles.

c- Direct and indirect threats, with necessary response measures to reduce threats and ensure the safety of those wildlife species.

d- Important or special habitats for wildlife conservation.

e- Integration into Natural Protected Area management plans.

f- Integration into the National Biodiversity Strategy and Action Plan and other conservation plans.

g- Timing and financial resources for implementation.

#### Article 534 .- Environmental Impact Assessment for Restoration of Threatened Wildlife Species

Restoration plans or activities for wildlife shall undergo environmental impact assessment as stated in Title 2 (Environmental Impact Assessment) of Book 5 of this Code, in cases where there are significant impacts on habitats, functions, and ecosystem roles.

#### Article 535 .- Report Preparation and Monitoring

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations, in cooperation with relevant stakeholders, shall establish monitoring systems and prepare reports on the implementation of management and restoration plans for wildlife and wildlife inventories.

### Section 6 Management of Invasive Alien Species

#### Article 536 .- List of Invasive Alien Species

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall coordinate and cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions to prepare records and manage the list of invasive alien species within their jurisdiction. The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, after receiving the list from sub-national administrations, shall review and verify the list of invasive alien species before submitting it to the Royal Government for review and decision.

2- The import of any invasive alien species without the consensus of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations is prohibited.

#### Article 537 .- Management of Invasive Alien Species

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall establish management activities for invasive alien species by considering negative impacts and risks to wildlife, wild plants, ecosystems, environment, economy, and society.

2- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations, in cooperation with relevant ministries, institutions, and technical experts, shall establish management activities for invasive alien species to determine:

a- Pathways through which invasive alien species enter their jurisdiction.

b- Methods for managing invasive alien species present within their jurisdiction.

c- Methods for eradicating invasive alien species and preventing disasters.

#### Article 538 .- Invasive Alien Species Management Plans

Invasive alien species management plans shall define specific contents as follows:

a- Goals, criteria, measures, monitoring indicators, and expected results.

b- Management activities, measures, and methods for eradication, prevention, and deterrence of invasive alien species.

c- Size, location, geography, source, and types of invasive alien species.

d- Biodiversity resources, ecosystems, and important habitats at the locations where invasive alien species are managed.

e- Direct and indirect pathways, and necessary response measures for the prevention and deterrence of invasive alien species.

f- Integration into the National Biodiversity Strategy and Action Plan and conservation plans.

g- Timing and financial resources for implementation.

#### Article 539 .- List of Invasive Alien Forest Plant Species

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, in cooperation with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall prepare specific lists of invasive alien forest plant species that cause danger within the Kingdom of Cambodia or for the import of those forest plant species into the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 540 .- Eradication Plans for Invasive Alien Forest Plant Species

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall prepare eradication plans for invasive alien forest plant species to minimize negative impacts from invasive alien forest plant species on the environment, economy, and society.

#### Article 541 .- Permit Requirements

The import of any invasive alien forest plant species into Natural Protected Areas shall have prior consensus from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations.

## Chapter 8 Management, Protection, and Conservation of Wildlife

### Section 1 Responsible Institution

#### Article 542 .- Responsible Institution

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations are responsible for the protection, conservation, and management of all wildlife species within Natural Protected Areas.

2- The management of wildlife located outside Natural Protected Areas is the competence of the Ministry or institution in charge of the forestry sector.

#### Article 543 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, competent ministries, and sub-national administrations have the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare legal instruments and procedures for management, protection, and conservation of wildlife.

b- Enhance and implement the national strategic plan, management plan, and conservation action plan for threatened wildlife species in accordance with legal instruments or international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

c- Monitor, research, and prevent all types of wildlife offenses within Natural Protected Areas, including export, import, stockpiling, circulation, commerce, wildlife use, and consumption of wildlife meat harvested without permits.

d- Strengthen the implementation of laws and relevant legal instruments, investigate, monitor, inspect, build case files for wildlife offenses, and act as the civil plaintiff unit according to legal procedures.

e- Establish, manage, and maintain database systems related to wildlife offenses.

f- Strengthen investigation mechanisms to prevent wildlife trade and develop additional new methods for combating transboundary wildlife offenses or cases where the Kingdom of Cambodia is used as a transit destination to other countries.

g- Update wildlife classification lists.

h- Establish and implement programs for protection, conservation, monitoring, and research of endangered wildlife species as stated in Article 520 (Wildlife Classifications) of this Code.

i- Prepare legal instruments, management tasks, and the issuance of hunting permits for local communities residing within or near Natural Protected Areas.

j- Review, deny, or approve wildlife hunting according to various conditions in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

k- Inspect, monitor, and/or issue operational orders for wildlife zoos, conservation breeding centers, wildlife rescue and rehabilitation centers, and wildlife harvesting sites for commercial purposes.

l- Review, deny, or approve requests for capturing live wildlife for conservation breeding purposes.

m- Ensure that permits and permit issuance processes are transparent and made public.

n- Prepare strategies for wildlife health monitoring and care, in cooperation with relevant ministries and institutions, and coordinate responses to epidemic cases, infectious diseases, and wildlife health.

o- Cooperate with relevant ministries, institutions, stakeholders, and participate in international cooperation.
