p- Promote public education and disseminate programs showing the importance of sustainable wildlife management, protection, and conservation.

q- Prohibit the destruction or alteration of natural wildlife habitats without permission.

2- The allocation of functions, roles, and specific duties to each level of sub-national administration shall be determined by sub-decree.

### Section 2 Wildlife Classification

#### Article 544 .- Wildlife

All wildlife species that are components of natural resources shall be considered state property, except where there are special provisions in this Code, laws, and relevant legal instruments.

#### Article 545 .- Wildlife Classification List

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations shall coordinate and cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions to prepare records and manage wildlife classification lists, including threatened wildlife species, for management and protection within their jurisdiction. The wildlife classification list shall be determined by a proclamation of the Minister in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 546 .- Wildlife Classifications

1- Wildlife included in the classification list for each species that is endangered is divided into 8 (eight) classifications as follows:

a- Classification 1: Extinct species.

b- Classification 2: Extinct in the wild species.

c- Classification 3: Critically endangered species.

d- Classification 4: Endangered species.

e- Classification 5: Vulnerable species.

f- Classification 6: Near threatened species.

g- Classification 7: Not yet threatened species.

h- Classification 8: Data deficient species.

2- The above classifications shall be made based on the actual situation through consultation with relevant stakeholders and technical experts.

3- The definition and criteria for each wildlife classification category above shall be based on the actual situation and scientific characteristics.

4- The wildlife classification list shall be updated based on the actual situation and criteria in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 547 .- Upgrading Wildlife Classification

All wildlife species may have their classification upgraded based on the grounds of risk level, threat to wildlife status, and cultural heritage value or economic importance, in accordance with the classifications stated in Article 546 of this Code.

### Section 3 Prohibitions on Wildlife Hunting

#### Article 548 .- Prohibitions on Wildlife Hunting

1- All hunting, trapping, catching, poisoning, killing of wildlife or harassment and destruction through various means and wildlife species are prohibited as follows:

a- Wildlife species in classifications 2 to 6 as stated in Article 546 (Wildlife Classifications) of this Code.

b- Wildlife species as stated in classification 7 using means prohibited by law.

c- Hunting by local communities without a quota permit issued by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations.

d- Possession, production, stockpiling, transportation, or bringing in equipment that can be used for wildlife hunting through all means, including traps, snares, guns or modified weapons, cables, bicycle brake cables, or motorcycle brake cables, electric shocking devices, nets, or other equipment.

e- Transportation or taking possession of wildlife species in classification 1 as stated in Article 546 (Wildlife Classifications) of this Code without hunting permits and transportation permits.

f- Illegal catching, collecting, or purchasing wildlife from Natural Protected Areas for the purpose of establishing or maintaining wildlife farms, wildlife park institutions, or personal collection.

g- Bringing domestic animals into core and conservation zones.

2- All activities stated above are exempted for activities implemented for the purpose of wildlife management and conservation, or the establishment of conservation breeding sites and genetic resources conservation, wildlife rescue centers, and wildlife park institutions for the purpose of maintaining all types of wildlife, which are permitted by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 549 .- General Prohibitions

1- Hunting, trapping, snaring, catching, poisoning, killing of wildlife or harassment and destruction of wildlife species and through various means are prohibited as follows:

a- Wildlife species in classifications 1 to 6 as stated in Article 546 (Wildlife Classifications) of this Code, except for cases determined in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

b- Wildlife species in classification 7 as stated in Article 546 (Wildlife Classifications) of this Code using means prohibited by law.

c- Production, processing, purchasing, trading, transportation, snaring, trapping, or netting on land or in the air, using modified weapons, explosive weapons, poisons, and other means.

d- Hunting, catching, or chasing wildlife using all types of vehicles on water, land, or in the air.

2- The prohibitions stated above are exempted for wildlife species in classification 7 as stated in Article 546 (Wildlife Classifications) of this Code used by local communities.

family and traditional bases. Wildlife species used by local communities for family and traditional purposes shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation.

### Section 4 Exemptions for Wildlife Hunting

### Sub-section 1 Management of Hazards to Human Life

#### Article 550 .- Management of Hazards to Human Life

1- In cases where wildlife causes hazards to human life, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and sub-national administrations may take appropriate measures to kill the provoking wildlife in necessary cases.

2- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall prepared a proclamation on controlling wildlife that causes hazards to human life.

#### Article 551 .- Methods for Managing Hazardous Wildlife Species

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall prepare methods for managing hazardous wildlife species in accordance with legal instruments to ensure human welfare and the reduction of risks to other wildlife or human life.

#### Article 552 .- Wildlife Permitted to be Killed or Destroyed

Wildlife that has caused hazards to human life is permitted to be killed or injured. In cases where the injured wildlife cannot recover, it shall be destroyed or killed in accordance with effective legal instruments.

#### Article 553 .- Rights to Manage Species in Classifications 2 to 8

Persons may manage wildlife species listed in classifications 2 to 8 that may cause hazards to human life with permission from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or competent ministries or institutions.

#### Article 554 .- Wildlife Species Non-Native to the Kingdom of Cambodia

Wildlife species listed in classifications 2 to 6 and/or non-native to Natural Protected Areas that may cause hazards or spread across biodiversity shall be managed both within and outside Natural Protected Areas. In cases where wildlife has caused hazards to human life or spread across biodiversity, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations may permit killing or injuring them.

#### Article 555 .- Capturing Live Wildlife for Conservation Purposes

Capturing live wildlife of any species as determined in any classification for the purpose of conservation breeding or management of conservation work shall have permission from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 556 .- Management of Conservation

Conservation management includes:

a- Relocating any wildlife species to appropriate habitats for species restoration, reintroduction, population supplementation, or to prevent the inevitable extinction of wildlife groups.

b- Research or management of live specimens without the need to kill or destroy wildlife.

c- Capturing wildlife for the purpose of balancing wildlife groups and conservation breeding for threatened species in registered breeding sites.

#### Article 557 .- Relocation

Relocation and capture of wildlife for conservation breeding or conservation management of species in classifications 2 to 6 may be permitted by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 558 .- Quarantine and Wildlife Health Monitoring

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations shall require wildlife quarantine to monitor health in wildlife farms before those wildlife are relocated, reintroduced, or released into quarantine areas and natural habitats, wildlife farms, conservation breeding sites, wildlife rescue centers, or wildlife park institutions. After quarantine and wildlife health monitoring, the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations shall issue certificates as follows:

a- Wildlife has undergone quarantine and health monitoring for no more than 14 (fourteen) days and must be inspected before relocating back to natural habitats or transferring elsewhere.

b- The wildlife does not show signs of disease or injuries.

c- The wildlife is healthy and equipped with identification tags.

2- In cases where wildlife is diseased or dies during the quarantine period, the owner or manager of the site shall immediately notify the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 559 .- Methods for Monitoring Wildlife Health

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations shall:

a- Identify wildlife diseases and publicly disseminate information about the discovered diseases.

b- Prepare methods for health and wildlife disease monitoring.

c- Investigate, document, and respond to wildlife disease outbreaks.

d- Report on research results, health monitoring, and wildlife disease investigations.

#### Article 560 .- Wildlife Health Monitoring

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations may require holders of hunting, collecting, or purchasing permits to hand over wildlife specimens obtained from hunting or collection for wildlife health monitoring.

### Sub-section 2 Wildlife Park Institutions

#### Article 561 .- Requirements

1- Wildlife park institutions shall request permission for establishment and for the import or export of wildlife of the wildlife park institution from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

2- Revenues collected from the issuance of licenses or permits shall be used to support wildlife and biodiversity conservation, including the implementation of wildlife restoration and management plans or other uses for conservation purposes.

#### Article 562 .- Prohibitions

Hunting, trapping, catching, or collecting wildlife and other activities from natural forests for wildlife species determined in classifications 1 to 6 of the wildlife classification list for the purpose of serving wildlife park institutions is prohibited, except with special permission from the Royal Government.

#### Article 563 .- Applications

Applications for collecting, trapping, or catching wildlife of species determined in classifications 7 and 8 for the purposes of wildlife park institutions shall have permission from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 564 .- Issuance and Temporary Suspension of Permits

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations may issue permits to wildlife park institutions to conduct wildlife business activities in accordance with effective legal procedures and the provisions of this Code.

2- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations may issue temporary suspension letters, site closure orders, or wildlife confiscation orders within the premises and inside wildlife park institutions in cases where operators or owners of wildlife park institutions do not comply with the permits and legal instruments issued by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

### Section 5 Wildlife Hunting Permits

#### Article 565 .- Wildlife Hunting Permits

1- Wildlife hunting of species determined in classifications 7 and 8 shall have permits issued by the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

2- The determination of locations, equipment, types of weapons, wildlife species, and the number of wildlife to be hunted shall be determined by sub-decree.

3- Natural persons who receive permission for hunting have the right to take wildlife specimens obtained from such hunting. The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall inspect the number of wildlife actually hunted to determine the payment into the national budget.

4- Service fees for issuing hunting permits and the price of wildlife species to be hunted shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and the Ministry in charge of Economy and Finance.

#### Article 566 .- Wildlife Hunting

1- Hunters shall carry hunting permits with them at all times.

2- Hunters shall present hunting permits to competent units before entering the permitted hunting areas.

3- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations may require hunting permit holders for research purposes or wildlife health monitoring to provide samples or any parts of the hunted wildlife.

#### Article 567 .- Application for Permits

1- Persons intending to apply for hunting permits shall submit applications to the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

2- Applications for hunting permits shall be reviewed in accordance with instructions before receiving consensus from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 568 .- Conditions for Issuing or Not Issuing Permits

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources shall prepare application forms for wildlife hunting permits and provide wildlife hunting permits.

2- The issuance or non-issuance of wildlife hunting permits shall be conducted as follows:

a- Permit issuance shall have a quota.

b- Only one permit shall be issued per person.

c- Permits shall not be issued to persons with a history related to illegal wildlife hunting.

d- Permits shall not allow persons to transfer the permit or sell hunting rights to another person.

3- Hunting permits are valid for no more than 6 (six) months. Hunters may apply for a renewal of the wildlife hunting permit by attaching the expired permit.

4- In case of loss of the permit, the permit holder shall immediately notify the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 569 .- Suspension and Confiscation of Wildlife Hunting Permits

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources may suspend or confiscate wildlife hunting permits from persons who have received wildlife hunting permits and have acted contrary to the permit.

2- Sub-national administrations may suspend wildlife hunting permits from persons who have received wildlife hunting permits and have acted contrary to the permit. In this case, the sub-national administration shall notify the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

#### Article 570 .- Wildlife Database System

1- The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations shall establish a wildlife database system, hunting permits, and hunters for the purpose of monitoring, following, auditing, and regulating wildlife hunting activities.

2- Information in the database system may be permitted for the public to read in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

### Section 6 Wildlife Farming and Wildlife Commerce

#### Article 571 .- Wildlife Farming

1- Every person may farm wildlife for commercial purposes with permission from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations. The list of wildlife species that can be farmed with permits and the procedures for establishing wildlife farms, transportation, and slaughter of farmed wildlife shall be determined by a proclamation of the Minister in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

2- Every person may farm wildlife for entertainment purposes without the need for permission from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources, competent ministries, or sub-national administrations. The list of wildlife species that can be farmed for entertainment purposes without the need for permission shall be determined by sub-decree.

#### Article 572 .- Domestic Commerce

1- Persons may conduct domestic commerce in wildlife species, organs, or wildlife products of all kinds originating from wildlife farms as follows:

a- Purchasing or taking ownership, possession, cooking, or selling wildlife meat.

b- Displaying for sale or advertising to sell goods or products originating from wildlife.

c- Producing artificial wildlife using wildlife organs.

d- Producing products originating from wildlife.

2- Domestic commerce in wildlife, transportation, and wildlife products of all kinds shall have permission from the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 573 .- International Commerce

1- Persons may conduct activities related to international commerce in wildlife species, organs, or processed specimens or others as follows:

a- Possession.

b- Transportation.

c- Exhibition.

d- Stockpiling.

e- Exchange and gifts.

f- Scientific research.

g- Activities held in the Kingdom of Cambodia related to international commerce in wildlife organs or specimens.

2- Procedures for issuing permits for international commerce or international activities related to wildlife species, wildlife specimens, and processed specimens or others shall be determined by a proclamation of the Minister in charge of Environment and Natural Resources.

3- Procedures and forms for international commerce shall follow laws and legal instruments of the Ministry or institution in charge of commerce.

#### Article 574 .- Prohibited Activities

1- Trading, transportation, import, export, re-export, possession, sale, purchase, taking, transfer, stockpiling, giving as gift, consumption, farming, and using for commercial purposes of wildlife organs and wildlife products of all kinds without permission is prohibited.

2- Providing advertising information or advertising for the sale of wildlife, ornamental objects, wildlife organs, wildlife meat, or wildlife products of all kinds without permission is prohibited.

3- Producing counterfeit products advertised as wildlife products or wildlife organs shall be considered an offense the same as an offense involving real wildlife and wildlife organs.

#### Article 575 .- Non-responsibility of the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations shall not be responsible in the following cases:

a- Any property damage or impact caused by farmed wildlife or by escape from confinement in wildlife farms.

b- Any loss or death of any farmed wildlife, whether through escape or death due to disease, even if the Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources or sub-national administrations may require the culling of any farmed wildlife that is threatened by disease or other situations.

### Section 7 Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora (CITES)

#### Article 576 .- Implementation on Wildlife and Wild Plant Species

1- Based on laws, legal instruments, and the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora as stated in this Code, it may be applied to wildlife and wild plant species in:

a- Appendix 1 of the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora.

b- Appendix 2 of the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora.

c- Appendix 3 of the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora.

2- The Kingdom of Cambodia shall cooperate with various countries that are parties to the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora to promote effective implementation.

#### Article 577 .- Prohibitions

The following activities are prohibited:

a- Importing or exporting wildlife species into the Kingdom of Cambodia from outside the Kingdom of Cambodia or from within the Kingdom of Cambodia to outside the Kingdom of Cambodia as stated in the Appendices of the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora without permission from the CITES Management Authority of Cambodia.

b- Re-exporting, transferring, or transporting through the Kingdom of Cambodia toward other countries wildlife species as stated in the Appendices of the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora without permission from the CITES Management Authority of Cambodia.

### Section 8 International Cooperation

#### Article 578 .- International Cooperation in Suppressing Wildlife Offenses

The Ministry in charge of Environment and Natural Resources and competent ministries and institutions shall cooperate in providing information to law enforcement authorities of relevant states or countries to prevent, identify, and suppress wildlife offenses under the jurisdiction of the relevant states or countries, in accordance with the Code of Criminal Procedure and other effective laws.

#### Article 579 .- Investigation and Procedures

1- The Royal Government of the Kingdom of Cambodia may cooperate with international law enforcement authorities and international organizations and/or non-governmental organizations for the following tasks:

a- Providing documentary evidence, testimonies, witnesses, various substances, and detailed presentations for analysis and investigation.

b- Providing or exchanging personnel, technical officials, and other functional officials.

c- Conducting separate investigations or joint investigations.

d- Initiating procedures.

e- Providing other assistance upon request for each case.

2- Providing assistance as stated in paragraph 1 of this Article shall be implemented in accordance with the Law on Mutual Legal Assistance in Criminal Matters and other effective legal provisions.

# Section 9: Wildlife Species Restoration and Management Plan

#### Article 580.- Wildlife Species Restoration and Management Plan

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations shall prepare a restoration and management plan for all classes of wildlife species, particularly species in classes 1 to 6 that originate within natural protected areas. This plan shall have a duration of at least 10 (ten) years.

2- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations shall coordinate with relevant ministries and institutions in preparing the restoration and management plan for wildlife species after conducting public consultations.

#### Article 581.- Conditions for Wildlife Species Restoration and Management Plan

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations shall determine the conditions for:

a- Protection and conservation activities within the wildlife species restoration and management plan.

b- Key habitats for the protection and conservation of wildlife species, including management measures based on the type of habitat and area for the purpose of protection and conservation.

c- Management activities of habitats and landscapes in the restoration and management plan of natural protected areas.

d- Measurable status that can lead to continuous improvement of the target species. The wildlife species restoration and management plan shall determine the timeline and financial resources for implementing various activities and measures.

e- Relocation, ex-situ conservation measures, breeding for conservation, and restoration for conservation purposes.

#### Article 582.- Permit

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations may issue permits for wildlife restoration projects or research activities, data collection, photography, wildlife sound recording, use of camera traps or drones, or production of films or videos related to the identification of wildlife species in classes 1 to 6.

2- Before issuing a permit, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations shall review the wildlife species restoration and management plan.

#### Article 583.- Monitoring of Wildlife Species Status

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations shall cooperate with relevant parties to monitor the status of wildlife species in classes 1 to 6 within a period of 10 (ten) years after the status of wildlife species classes has changed, in accordance with legal instruments or regulations of this Code.

# Section 10: Management of Conservation Breeding Sites, Genetic Resources, Wildlife Rescue Centers, and Wildlife Parks

#### Article 584.- Registration

The establishment of conservation breeding sites, genetic resources conservation, wildlife rescue centers, and wildlife parks for the purpose of keeping all types of wildlife species shall require a permit and registration with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 585.- Inspection

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations shall conduct inspections of conservation breeding sites, genetic resources conservation, wildlife rescue centers, and wildlife parks.

#### Article 586.- Revocation of Permit or Temporary Suspension of Operations

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations has the right to revoke permits or temporarily suspend the operations of conservation breeding sites, genetic resources conservation, wildlife rescue centers, and wildlife parks, as well as confiscate wildlife in cases where operations are carried out in contradiction with the terms of the permit regarding wildlife keeping and collection.

#### Article 587.- Monitoring and Record Keeping

Operators or owners of conservation breeding sites, genetic resources conservation, wildlife rescue centers, or wildlife parks have the duty to report on births, deaths, and transfers of wildlife to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 588.- Transfer or Change of Wildlife Location

1- Every transfer or change of wildlife location shall require a transportation permit and/or prior notification to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

2- The transfer or change of wildlife location from one place to another shall be clearly recorded, and notification and documents shall be provided to law enforcement officers of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 589.- Disposal of Meat, Specimens, or Wildlife Decorative Objects

1- The disposal of organs, meat, specimens, or wildlife decorative objects that are kept or raised shall be conducted in accordance with the legal instruments in force.

2- Keeping whole organs or parts of wildlife in conservation breeding sites, genetic resources conservation, wildlife rescue centers, or wildlife parks is prohibited, unless permitted by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 590.- Conditions for Wildlife Treatment and Location

Wildlife kept at conservation breeding sites, genetic resources conservation, wildlife rescue centers, wildlife parks, or wildlife sanctuaries shall receive humane care and shall comply with animal welfare protocols or legal instruments in force.

#### Article 591.- Natural Characteristics of Wildlife

1- Every location permitted for conservation breeding, genetic resources conservation, wildlife rescue centers, wildlife parks, or wildlife sanctuaries shall ensure the natural characteristics of the wildlife species, and cages shall meet the acceptable standards for animal welfare.

2- Locations permitted for conservation breeding and genetic resources conservation shall maintain record books for each wildlife species, specifying breeding programs or enrichment in accordance with legal instruments in force.

3- Breeding type, breeding events, and single breeds shall be accurately recorded regarding the number of parent animals and offspring born from the original parents. Offspring born from original parents shall be permanently marked to identify the identification [part number] and origin of the offspring.

#### Article 592.- Data Management System

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations shall establish a data management system for observing, monitoring, inspecting, and managing the wildlife to be kept and the animal keeping locations.

#### Article 593.- Wildlife Release

1- Locations suitable for release or animals born in keeping locations that can be released shall be released in accordance with the legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

2- Before release, a health check of the wildlife, identification, and preparation of the release site shall be conducted to suit natural characteristics, and observation and monitoring shall be carried out after release if necessary.

# Section 11: Management of Confiscated Wildlife and Wildlife Organs/Parts

#### Article 594.- Management Procedures

The management of confiscated wildlife and wildlife organs/parts shall be implemented as follows:

a- Live wildlife originating from the country shall be assessed for health and various suitability before being released into the natural forest. In cases where they are not suitable, they shall be sent to an authorized wildlife rescue center for treatment and care.

b- Dead wildlife, organs/parts, or specimens may be destroyed or kept in accordance with legal instruments determined by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

c- Wildlife not originating from a natural protected area may be sent to an authorized wildlife rescue center, but in case of necessity, may be returned to its place of origin or measures may be taken according to the actual situation.

#### Article 595.- Preparation of Reports according to the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, competent ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations that have carried out seizures of endangered wildlife species as a whole or as wildlife organs/parts as defined in the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora [CITES] shall report to the Management Authority of the country.

2- The Management Authority in charge of the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora of the country shall report to the Secretariat of the Convention on International Trade in Endangered Species of Wild Fauna and Flora [CITES] in accordance with the reporting obligations defined in this Convention.

3- In cases where wildlife species defined in this Convention are seized while in transit or being distributed within the Kingdom of Cambodia, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, competent ministries and institutions, or sub-national administrations may keep or take measures to finalize the confiscation procedures in accordance with Book 9 of this Code.

#### Article 596.- Cooperation for Enhancing International Law Enforcement

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or competent ministries and institutions may request the Royal Government to negotiate and determine various agreements with international law enforcement authorities, international organizations, or regional organizations for the purpose of enhancing cooperation in strengthening international law enforcement.

# Chapter 9: Fisheries Management
## Section 1: Responsible Institutions

#### Article 597.- Responsible Institutions

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations shall be responsible for the management, conservation, and development of fishery resources within natural protected areas.

2- The management, conservation, and development of fishery resources outside natural protected areas shall be the competence of the Ministry in charge of the fisheries sector.

#### Article 598.- Roles and Duties

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations have the roles and duties to manage the conservation and development of fishery resources in natural protected areas as follows:

a- Prepare draft management, conservation, and development policies for fishery resources, management plans, master plans, and data maps for fisheries management.

b- Determine fishing areas to permit fishing activities and areas for conservation of fishery resources.

c- Manage, conserve, and develop fishery resources to enhance food security and maintain the national environment for the high interest of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

d- Manage and determine aquaculture activities.

e- Conduct scientific research on fishery techniques, economy, society, and environment.

f- Cooperate and share data on the management, conservation, and development of fishery resources with relevant ministries and institutions.

g- Educate and disseminate information to enhance public awareness in participating in the protection of fishery resources.

h- Conduct inspections, monitoring, and evaluation of fisheries management.

i- Conduct research and investigation and take legal measures against fisheries offenses, build case files, and act as a plaintiff in competent courts.

2- The ministry or institution in charge of the fisheries sector has the power to perform its roles and duties in accordance with this Code or laws related to its sector.

3- The specific division of functions, roles, and duties to each level of sub-national administration shall be determined by Sub-Decree.

# Section 2: Sustainability of Fisheries Management

#### Article 599.- Principles of Fisheries Management

Fisheries management shall respect the following principles:

a- Manage, conserve, develop, and use fishery resources sustainably based on information and scientific data.

b- Use and distribute fishery resources equitably.

c- Enhance cooperation in managing fishery resources with transparency and inclusiveness.

d- Enhance the development of aquaculture in areas with potential.

e- Effectively respect international agreements and other relevant international and regional instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

f- Ensure decent working conditions in fisheries on fishing vessels or related to fishing to protect workers against human trafficking or forced labor.

g- Ensure food safety.

h- Improve the livelihoods of fishers.

# Section 3: Fisheries Protection and Conservation

#### Article 600.- Classification of Fisheries Management Areas

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall determine areas for supporting and conserving fishery resources within natural protected areas in accordance with the provisions of this Code and other relevant sectoral laws to enhance the effectiveness of managing, supporting, and conserving fishery resources.

#### Article 601.- Prohibited Activities in Fisheries Conservation Areas of the Conservation Zone and Core Zone within Natural Protected Areas

Activities prohibited in fisheries conservation areas are as follows:

a- All types of fishing, unless permitted by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations for experimental, technical research, or fisheries science purposes.

b- Navigation through the area with fishing equipment prepared in a manner indicating an intent to fish, or other activities that affect fishery resources.

c- Use of fishing gear or other activities prohibited by the Law on Fisheries.

#### Article 602.- Activities Requiring Permit in Fisheries Conservation Areas of the Conservation Zone and Core Zone within Natural Protected Areas

Activities that may be permitted in fisheries conservation areas of the conservation zone and core zone within natural protected areas are as follows:

a- Fishing in fisheries conservation areas of the conservation zone and core zone that does not affect the objectives of fishery resources conservation, where possible, with a permit from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations.

b- Digging or excavation of soil, sand, and minerals in fisheries conservation areas of the conservation zone and core zone, where possible, with prior consultation and joint decision between the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the ministry or institution in charge of the mineral resources sector.

#### Article 603.- Preparation of Fishery Eco-tourism

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the right to establish fishery tourism areas within the conservation zone and core zone of natural protected areas by consulting with the ministry or institution in charge of the fisheries sector and sub-national administrations.

# Section 4: Management of Fishing in Community Zones and Sustainable Use Zones

#### Article 604.- Rights of Citizens

Cambodian citizens or persons legally residing in the Kingdom of Cambodia have the right to fish and participate in activities related to fishing in the community zones and sustainable use zones of natural protected areas in accordance with this Code and the Law on Fisheries.

#### Article 605.- Types of Fishing

1- Fishing types shall be defined as follows:

a- Small-scale fishing.

b- Medium-scale fishing.

c- Large-scale fishing.

2- Fishing types shall be defined by the type of fishing gear and fishing vessels. Fishing gear types shall be defined by a Prakas of the Minister in charge of the fisheries sector.

#### Article 606.- Fishing

1- The ministry or institution in charge of the fisheries sector or sub-national administrations have the right to issue permits for fishing or fisheries business, fishery products, or aquaculture in accordance with the provisions of this Code and the Law on Fisheries in community zones and sustainable use zones, except where such permits affect the management objectives of these zones.

2- The procedures for issuing permits shall be defined by a Prakas of the Minister in charge of the fisheries sector.

# Chapter 10: Ecotourism

#### Article 607.- Responsible Ministry and Institution

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall coordinate and cooperate with the Ministry in charge of the tourism sector and sub-national administrations in managing ecotourism projects within natural protected areas.

#### Article 608.- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of the Environment and Natural Resources Sector

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft policies, legal instruments, strategic plans, management plans, action plans, and technical guidelines for managing ecotourism development investment projects and community-based ecotourism conservation projects.

b- Coordinate and cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions, sub-national administrations, and the private sector in managing ecotourism investment projects.

c- Study, coordinate, and manage ecotourism development investment projects and community ecotourism conservation projects by cooperating with relevant ministries and institutions.

d- Coordinate the establishment, inspection, monitoring, and evaluation of the implementation of ecotourism laws to attract national and international tourists to visit natural protected areas by cooperating with relevant ministries and institutions.

e- Coordinate studies to determine locations and demarcate land boundaries for ecotourism development investment projects within natural protected areas.

f- Prepare, monitor, and inspect the progress of the implementation of ecotourism investment projects and community ecotourism development projects.

g- Inspect and improve master plans and development programs for ecotourism investment projects.

h- Coordinate and resolve various conflicts and irregularities occurring in relation to the implementation of ecotourism development programs and projects.

i- Establish mechanisms to collect revenue from ecotourism services to contribute to the support of conservation activities in natural protected areas.

j- Mobilize financial and technical assistance from development partners and various private sectors to support effective ecotourism development.

k- Study and prepare the establishment of ecotourism products in natural protected areas and strengthen the capacity of sub-national administrations and local communities in ecotourism development by cooperating with relevant ministries and development partners.

#### Article 609.- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of the Tourism Sector

The Ministry in charge of the tourism sector has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft policies and development plans for ecotourism in natural protected areas by cooperating with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, sub-national administrations, and relevant parties.

b- Prepare national and regional ecotourism plans for submission to the Royal Government for review and decision and inclusion in the national development strategic plan.

c- Manage and inspect the ecotourism industry within natural protected areas together with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

d- Issue licenses and permits to the ecotourism industry within natural protected areas.

e- Cooperate with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and sub-national administrations in studying and determining potential areas for the implementation of ecotourism within natural protected areas.

f- Monitor, inspect, and conduct inspections on the implementation of licenses and permits of the ecotourism industry.

g- Perform other additional roles and duties in accordance with the provisions of the Law on Tourism.

#### Article 610.- Obligations of the Owners of Ecotourism Development Investment Projects

1- Owners of ecotourism development investment projects have the obligations to protect, maintain the environment, manage and conserve biodiversity and natural resources in accordance with their licenses and permits.

2- Owners of ecotourism development investment projects shall contribute to planting trees within the permitted project areas.

#### Article 611.- Evaluation of Resource Effectiveness

1- Every ecotourism development activity or project that causes negative impacts on the environment and natural resources beyond the permitted levels or does not comply with ecotourism standards shall undergo an evaluation of resource effectiveness to ensure the protection of the environment and conservation of natural resources.

2- The formalities and procedures for the evaluation of resource effectiveness shall be defined by an Inter-Ministerial Prakas of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the ministry or institution in charge of the tourism sector by consulting with sub-national administrations.

#### Article 612.- Preparation of Legal Instruments Related to the Sustainability of Ecotourism Development

1- The ministry or institution in charge of the tourism sector, in cooperation with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, relevant ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall prepare legal instruments on the formalities, procedures, and criteria for reviewing sustainability applicable to ecotourism projects and activities.

2- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with the ministry or institution in charge of the tourism sector, relevant ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations, shall prepare legal instruments on the development and management of ecotourism in natural protected areas in accordance with national and international ecotourism standards.

#### Article 613.- Entrance Fees for Community Ecotourism

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector may collect entrance fees for visiting community ecotourism located within natural protected areas for the purpose of protecting and conserving natural resources.

2- The service fees for visiting community ecotourism within natural protected areas shall be defined by an Inter-Ministerial Prakas of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the ministry or institution in charge of the economy and finance sector by cooperating with the ministry or institution in charge of the tourism sector.

#### Article 614.- Prohibited Activities

All activities of ecotourism development projects are prohibited as follows:

a- Destroying or clearing natural resources within the permitted project area.

b- Transforming or changing the original form of the area that causes serious impacts on the functions of the natural protected area.

c- Digging, clearing, and encroachment of trees within the project area, unless permitted by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

d- Digging or clearing land for the purpose of transforming such land into private ownership.

# Chapter 11: Mineral Resources Management

#### Article 615.- Responsible Ministry or Institution

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall coordinate and cooperate with the ministry or institution in charge of the mineral resources sector and sub-national administrations in managing mineral resource business projects in

within natural protected areas. The management of all types of mineral resource business projects is the competence of the ministry or institution in charge of the mineral resources sector.

#### Article 616.- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of the Environment and Natural Resources Sector

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the following roles and duties:

a- Coordinate and cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions in managing mineral resource investment projects that may cause serious impacts on the environment.

b- Coordinate the establishment, inspection, monitoring, and evaluation of the implementation of mineral business projects in natural protected areas in cooperation with relevant ministries and institutions.

c- Observe, monitor, and inspect the implementation of the environmental management plan and environmental protection contract of mineral business projects.

d- Issue permits and various approvals in accordance with the provisions on environmental impact assessment.

e- Study and submit proposals to the Royal Government in cases where mineral business projects cause serious impacts on the environment compared to the economic benefits derived from the projects.

f- Consult and joint decide before submitting proposals to the Royal Government for review and decision on any mineral business projects carried out in the conservation zone and core zone of natural protected areas.

#### Article 617.- Roles and Duties of the Ministry or Institution in charge of the Mineral Resources Sector

The ministry or institution in charge of the mineral resources sector has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft policies, legal instruments, strategic plans, management plans, action plans, and technical guidelines for managing mineral resource business investment projects.

b- Inspect, monitor, and evaluate the implementation of mineral resource business projects.

c- Issue mineral resource licenses or various permits in accordance with the Law on Management and Exploitation of Mineral Resources and relevant legal instruments.

d- Study and evaluate the economic benefits derived from mineral resource business projects.

e- Monitor, inspect, and strengthen law enforcement.

f- Conduct inspections on mineral resource business projects in accordance with various licenses and permits.

g- Consult with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, relevant ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations in cases where mineral resource business projects cause impacts on the environment and cultural heritage areas.

h- Consult and joint decide with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector before submitting proposals to the Royal Government for review and decision on mineral resource business projects located within the conservation zone and core zone of natural protected areas.

#### Article 618.- Obligation to Avoid Environmental Damage

1- Owners of mineral resource business projects have the obligation to avoid pollution or serious environmental damage resulting from the operation or mineral resource business project.

2- The use of modern environmentally friendly technologies in mineral resource business is encouraged.

3- Owners of mineral resource business projects shall be responsible for paying compensation for damage to citizens affected by the mineral resource business project.

#### Article 619.- Reporting Obligation

1- Owners of mineral resource business projects shall immediately report to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector and the ministry or institution in charge of the mineral resources sector in cases of leaks or spills of acid, dissolved substances, or hazardous waste that cause impacts on the environment and society.

2- The costs of environmental restoration as mentioned in paragraph 1 above shall be borne by the owners of the mineral resource business projects.

#### Article 620.- List of Modern Environmentally Friendly Technologies

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall cooperate with the ministry or institution in charge of the mineral resources sector to prepare legal instruments on the list of modern environmentally friendly technologies to be used in the operations of mineral business projects.

#### Article 621.- Prohibited Activities

The following activities are prohibited:

a- Constructing liquid waste storage ponds near rivers, seas, or water sources necessary for public use and which are shallow and not in accordance with environmental impact assessment provisions.

b- Spills or leaks of acid or dissolved substances from mineral resource business projects that cause impacts on the environment and society.

c- Conducting reconnaissance, exploration, and mineral resource business without a permit.

d- Discharging liquid, solid, or any types of hazardous waste from mineral resource business activities that cause impacts on the environment and society.

# Chapter 12: Energy Sector Management

#### Article 622.- Encouragement of Responsible Energy Use

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector encourages responsible energy use projects and renewable energy within natural protected areas to ensure energy sustainability and contribute to reducing the use of electricity that causes environmental impacts.

#### Article 623.- Power Transmission Lines through Conservation Zones or Core Zones

The ministry or institution in charge of the energy sector shall consult with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector before starting any projects to install power transmission lines through the conservation zone or core zone.

#### Article 624.- Mutual Application of Provisions

1- Unless otherwise provided in this Chapter, the provisions in Title 10 (Responsible Energy) of Book 2 of this Code shall be applied mutatis mutandis to the management of the energy sector within natural protected areas.

2- Responsible energy as mentioned in Title 10 (Responsible Energy) of Book 2 of this Code shall not be managed and used in cases where such management and use of responsible energy contradict the objectives of environmental protection and management of natural resources within natural protected areas.

# Title 3: Management of Coastal, Island, and Marine Resources

## Chapter 1: General Provisions

#### Article 625.- Objectives

This Title aims to manage resources in coastal, island, and marine areas through environmental protection, conservation, use of natural resources, and economic development by ensuring environmental balance and ecosystems of coastal, island, and marine areas, and enhancing the local community economy.

#### Article 626.- Scope

This Title applies to the management of coastal, island, and marine resources of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

## Chapter 2: Responsible Institutions

#### Article 627.- Responsible Institutions

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall be responsible for the management, protection, and conservation of natural resources in coastal, island, and marine areas within natural protected areas of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

2- The Royal Government may determine the specific roles, responsibilities, and cooperation for competent ministries and institutions.

#### Article 628.- Roles and Duties

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector acts as the staff of the Royal Government in managing the protection and conservation of coastal, island, and marine resources within natural protected areas of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

2- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft national policies, national strategic plans, action plans, various legal instruments, and technical guidelines for managing coastal, island, and marine resources.

b- Prepare draft policies, management plans, master plans, and geographic maps for managing the environment and resources of coastal, island, and marine areas.

c- Review and submit proposals to the Royal Government to participate in the implementation of relevant international instruments.

d- Study, evaluate, collect, compile, and maintain information and data on coastal, island, and marine resources.

e- Educate and disseminate information to enhance public awareness in participating in the maintenance and protection of coastal, island, and marine resources.

f- Develop capacity and provide technical support to sub-national administrations and local communities in managing coastal, island, and marine resources.

g- Conduct inspections, monitoring, and evaluation of the management of coastal, island, and marine resources.

#### Article 629.- Technical Guidelines

The formalities, procedures, and technical guidelines for the management of coastal, island, and marine resources within natural protected areas shall be defined by a Prakas of the Minister in charge of the environment and natural resources sector by consulting with relevant ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 630.- Establishment of Natural Protected Areas Related to Coastal, Island, and Marine Areas

The establishment of natural protected areas related to coastal, island, and marine areas shall be carried out as provided in Article 388 (Establishment of Natural Protected Areas) of this Code.

#### Article 631.- Monitoring and Evaluation

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, in cooperation with relevant ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations, shall observe, inspect, monitor, and evaluate the implementation of strategic plans for the management, conservation, and restoration of biodiversity and ecosystems in coastal, island, and marine areas within natural protected areas.

#### Article 632.- Conditions for Conservation Activities or Projects

The approval of conservation activities or projects in coastal, island, and marine areas shall be based on the following conditions:

a- Activities or projects proposed by persons are in accordance with the land use plan of coastal, island, and marine areas.

b- Activities or projects proposed by persons shall include measures for preparation and mitigation of impacts on coastal, island, and marine areas to the minimum level.

c- Climate change impacts and disaster risks are forecasted to prevent and mitigate the risk of flood and inundation in coastal, island, and marine areas by establishing procedures for addressing the needs of the population and infrastructure.

d- Impacts from solid waste and liquid waste discharge into coastal areas shall be reduced to the minimum or terminated through effective waste management.

e- Filling of land for water use shall be reduced to the minimum.

f- Activities or projects shall comply with environmental and social impact assessment measures.

g- Other conditions defined in the legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 633.- Prohibited Projects

1- Construction activities, infrastructure, mineral business, and investment projects that cause loss, pollution, or serious damage to the environment, natural resources, and ecosystems in coastal, island, and marine areas are prohibited as follows:

a- Marine animal habitats and rare species areas.

b- Seagrass and coral reef areas.

c- Sources of wildlife food and natural areas.

d- Mangrove forest areas.

e- Forest areas, grasslands, lakes, and freshwater sources in island areas.

f- Natural breeding areas and spawning grounds, and other fishery resource areas.

2- The activities or projects mentioned above may be implemented in cases where there is permission from the Royal Government.

# Title 4: Implementation of Sectoral Provisions

## Chapter 1: General Provisions

#### Article 634.- Objectives

This Title aims to establish mechanisms for coordination, cooperation, and consistency in the implementation of sectoral laws and legal instruments related to fisheries, forestry, wildlife, wild plants, tourism, mineral resources, water resources, energy, cultural heritage, watersheds, and other relevant sectors regarding the management of all natural protected areas and natural resources within natural protected areas, as mentioned from Title 1 (Management of Natural Protected Areas) to Title 3 (Management of Coastal, Island, and Marine Resources) of Book 4 of this Code.

#### Article 635.- Scope

This Title applies to the fulfillment of the roles and functions of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector, relevant ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations to which specific sectoral laws and this Code have assigned duties.

## Chapter 2: Implementation of Specific Sectoral Provisions

#### Article 636.- Implementation of Sectoral Laws and Legal Instruments

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the right to implement laws and legal instruments related to fisheries, forestry, wildlife, wild plants, tourism, mineral resources, water resources, energy, cultural heritage, watersheds, and other relevant sectors to supplement the implementation of roles and duties in managing, supporting, and conserving natural resources within natural protected areas as defined in this Code to maintain the sustainability of natural resources, biodiversity, and ecosystems for the supreme interest of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

2- Relevant ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations to which specific laws have assigned duties have the right to implement the provisions of this Code to supplement the implementation of their roles and duties in their respective sectors.

3- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall cooperate and coordinate with relevant ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations in implementing the provisions of this Code to enhance the effectiveness of management, maintenance, support, and conservation of natural protected areas and natural resources within natural protected areas for the supreme interest of the Kingdom of Cambodia, under its jurisdiction in accordance with the policies of the Royal Government of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

#### Article 637.- Determination of Rights for Natural Resource Extraction and Investment Projects

Relevant ministries and institutions to which specific sectoral laws have assigned powers related to the management of all natural resources in the Kingdom of Cambodia may carry out natural resource extraction and allow any investment projects in community zones and sustainable use zones of natural protected areas after prior consultation with the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector or sub-national administrations. Any extraction of natural resources and decision on investment projects in the conservation zone and core zone may be implemented only in accordance with the provisions from Title 1 (Management of Natural Protected Areas) to Title 3 (Management of Coastal, Island, and Marine Resources) of Book 4 of this Code.

# Book 5: Environmental Assessment
## Title 1: Strategic Environmental Assessment
### Chapter 1: General Provisions

#### Article 638.- Objectives

This Title aims to ensure the effectiveness of strategic environmental assessment for the policies, plans, and development programs of the Royal Government across various sectors with public participation.

#### Article 639.- Scope

This Title applies to strategic environmental assessment within the framework of policies, plans, and development programs of the Royal Government for the industrial, mineral, energy, agricultural, tourism, infrastructure, telecommunications, and other priority sectors.

## Chapter 2: Responsible Institutions

#### Article 640.- Responsible Institutions

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall be responsible for organizing strategic environmental assessment within the framework of policies, plans, and development programs of the Royal Government.

#### Article 641.- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of the Environment and Natural Resources Sector

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector has the following roles and duties:

a- Determine priority sectors that require strategic environmental assessment.

b- Review and decide on strategic environmental assessment reports.

c- Cooperate with relevant ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations to prepare legal instruments for strategic environmental assessment.

d- Define guidelines for preparing strategic environmental assessment reports.

#### Article 642.- Framework of Strategic Environmental Assessment

Every preparation of policies, plans, and development programs of the Royal Government must include strategic environmental assessment.

## Chapter 3: Strategic Environmental Assessment Procedures

#### Article 643.- Implementation of Strategic Environmental Assessment

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources sector shall prepare legal instruments to provide further details on the procedures for implementing strategic environmental assessment in this Title, including:

a- Detailed operations of strategic environmental assessment procedures.

b- Criteria for determining the potential for proposals that may have significant impacts.

c- Implementation of provisions on public participation and the right to environmental information as provided in this Code.

d- Content and format of strategic environmental assessment reports.

#### Article 644.- Procedures

1- Strategic environmental assessment procedures shall include the following stages:

a- Categorization of policies, plans, and programs.

b- Determining the scope of policies, plans, and programs.

- c- Preparation of Strategic Environmental Assessment reports.

- d- Review of Strategic Environmental Assessment reports.

- e- Decisions on Strategic Environmental Assessment reports.

- f- Monitoring the implementation of Strategic Environmental Assessments.

- 2- Procedures for Strategic Environmental Assessment shall be determined by Sub-decree.

#### Article 645 .- Public Participation in Strategic Environmental Assessment

1- Strategic Environmental Assessment procedures shall include public participation during the Strategic Environmental Assessment process.

2- Procedures for public participation shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 646 .- Responsibility for Preparing Strategic Environmental Assessment Reports

Other ministries, institutions, and/or sub-national administrations shall prepare Strategic Environmental Assessment reports and submit them to the Royal Government for approval after review and comments from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 647 .- Implementation of Strategic Environmental Assessment Reports

1- Ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations in charge of the environment and natural resources shall implement the Strategic Environmental Assessment reports agreed upon by the Royal Government.

2- Types of projects within the scope of the Strategic Environmental Assessment report shall continue to prepare Environmental Impact Assessment reports as stated in Content 2 (Environmental Impact Assessment) of Book 5 of this Code.

#### Article 648 .- Monitoring and Observation

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and sub-national administrations shall observe and monitor the environmental and social impacts as stated in the Strategic Environmental Assessment report.

2- Relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations shall submit Strategic Environmental Assessment monitoring reports to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources every 3 (three) years or 5 (five) years.

3- Procedures for Strategic Environmental Assessment monitoring and observation shall be determined by Sub-decree.

### CONTENT 2
## ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT ASSESSMENT

# CHAPTER 1
# GENERAL PROVISIONS

#### Article 149 .- Objective

This Content aims to ensure the effectiveness of Environmental Impact Assessments for both public and private investment projects, with public participation, aimed at preventing, avoiding, or mitigating negative environmental and social impacts caused by investment projects.

#### Article 150 .- Scope

1- This Content applies to the assessment of environmental impacts of all investment projects, both public and private, including proposed projects, existing projects, and ongoing projects that may cause impacts on the environment, health, economy, society, and culture.

2- In cases where a development project may cause transboundary environmental impacts on the environment, health, economy, society, and culture, the project owner shall conduct further studies on transboundary environmental impacts in accordance with laws and legal instruments related to transboundary environmental impact assessments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

#### Article 151 .- Exempted Projects

Environmental Impact Assessments shall not apply to state development projects or activities that have been decided or approved by the Royal Government or the National Assembly and are considered necessary and urgent regarding national security, territorial integrity, national sovereignty, or various disasters.

### CHAPTER 2
## RESPONSIBLE INSTITUTIONS

#### Article 652 .- Responsible Ministry and Institutions

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall be responsible for ensuring that environmental impact assessments are conducted for all public and private investment projects.

#### Article 653 .- Roles and Duties of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft policies, legal instruments, strategic plans, action plans, technical guidelines, and various procedures for environmental impact assessment.

b- Classify investment projects, determine terms of reference, determine the scope of studies, and prepare environmental impact assessment reports.

c- Review, comment, agree, and/or reject environmental impact assessment reports and issue various documents related to environmental impact assessments.

d- Promote public participation from relevant persons in accordance with the provisions stated in this Code.

e- Monitor, inspect, audit, investigate, guide, and take measures regarding the implementation of environmental impact assessment reports, environmental management plans, environmental protection contracts, and various legal instruments related to investment projects.

f- Prepare and issue legal instruments on public participation in the environmental impact assessment process.

g- Determine the conditions, qualifications, formats, and procedures for the registration of environmental impact assessment consultants who are natural or legal persons.

- h- Prepare the legal framework and various legal instruments for transboundary environmental impact assessment.

- i- Determine the composition of officials who have the status of judicial police officers.

### CHAPTER 3
## PRINCIPLES OF ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT ASSESSMENT

#### Article 654 .- Fundamental Principles of Environmental Impact Assessment

1- Every development project shall conduct an environmental impact assessment with prior agreement from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources before submitting it to the competent authorities or ministries, institutions, and/or the Royal Government for approval.

2- Every issuance of licenses, permits, or decisions on development projects by competent authorities or ministries, institutions, and/or the Royal Government shall be made in accordance with the conditions specified in the letter and certificate of agreement on the environmental impact assessment report or environmental protection contract.

3- All licenses, permits, or decisions that contradict the provisions of this Article shall be null and void.

#### Article 655 .- General Principles of Environmental Impact Assessment

The environmental impact assessment process is valid only if the environmental impact assessment process is conducted in accordance with the conditions and principles of public participation in the environmental impact assessment process as stated in Content 2 (Environmental Impact Assessment) of Book 5 of this Code.

### CHAPTER 4
## ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT ASSESSMENT PROCESS

#### Article 656 .- Classification of Development Projects

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall determine the classification of development projects to require project owners to prepare the following documents:

a- Full Environmental Impact Assessment Report.

b- Initial Environmental Impact Assessment Report.

c- Environmental Protection Contract.

2- Types of projects required to prepare a Full Environmental Impact Assessment Report, or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment Report, or Environmental Protection Contract shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 657 .- Requirements for Preparing Full Environmental Impact Assessment Reports, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment Reports, or Environmental Protection Contracts

1- Requirements for preparing a Full Environmental Impact Assessment Report shall be made for projects determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources or development projects that have serious impacts on the environment and society.

2- Requirements for preparing an Initial Environmental Impact Assessment Report shall be made for projects determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources or development projects that have medium impacts on the environment and society.

3- Requirements for preparing an Environmental Protection Contract shall be made for projects determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources or development projects that have minor impacts on the environment and society.

#### Article 658 .- Requirement for Consulting Firms for Preparing Full and Initial Environmental Impact Assessment Reports

1- When a proposed project is required to conduct a Full Environmental Impact Assessment, the project owner shall cooperate with a consulting firm registered and recognized by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources to prepare the Terms of Reference for the study and prepare the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report in accordance with the provisions and various legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, and shall request approval from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

2- When a proposed project is required to conduct an Initial Environmental Impact Assessment, the project owner shall prepare the environmental impact assessment report by themselves and/or through an environmental impact assessment consultant who is a legal or natural person, and shall request approval from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

3- When a proposed project is required to only provide an Environmental Protection Contract, the project owner shall enter into an environmental protection contract attached with an environmental management plan and relevant documents, and shall request approval from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 659 .- Environmental Impact Assessment Consultants as Legal or Natural Persons

1- Environmental impact assessment consultants who are legal or natural persons shall be registered and recognized by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources before practicing the profession of preparing environmental impact assessment reports.

2- Environmental impact assessment consultants shall practice their profession independently and be responsible for every Full Environmental Impact Assessment report and Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report.

3- Conditions and procedures for registration to obtain the status of an environmental impact assessment consultant as a legal or natural person shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 660 .- Review and Comments on Full Environmental Impact Assessment Reports

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall review and provide comments on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report within 90 (ninety) working days from the date of receipt of the report. This 90 (ninety) day period shall expire when the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has provided comments, whether those comments are a rejection, an agreement, or an order for correction or adjustment of the report.

2- The 90 (ninety) working day period for review and comments mentioned above shall restart when the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources receives a request for review along with the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report that the project owner has finally corrected in accordance with previous orders or instructions provided by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

3- In cases where the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources fails to provide comments within 90 (ninety) working days, the competent ministries, institutions and/or the Royal Government may determine that the project has met the requirements of this Code.

4- The project owner shall be responsible for all activities caused by faults resulting from delays or failure to make adjustments in accordance with the above orders or instructions.

#### Article 661 .- Expert Review Committee

1- Every development project required to conduct a Full Environmental Impact Assessment must have technical comments from the Expert Review Committee.

2- The Expert Review Committee consists of members from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, relevant ministries and institutions, sub-national administrations, and independent experts with appropriate qualifications and experience in reviewing Full Environmental Impact Assessment reports.

3- The roles and duties of this Expert Review Committee shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 662 .- Consultation Meetings of the Expert Review Committee

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall review and provide comments on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report after:

a- Hearing and considering the formal presentation and defense of the project owner and the consulting firm.

b- Considering the comments of persons directly or indirectly affected and the comments of the relevant public.

c- Considering the comments of the Expert Review Committee proposed only for Full Environmental Impact Assessment reports.

2- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall organize public participation processes in accordance with the provisions in Chapter 6 (Public Participation in the Environmental Impact Assessment Process) of Content 2 (Environmental Impact Assessment) of Book 5 of this Code.

#### Article 663 .- Review and Comments on Initial Environmental Impact Assessment Reports

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall review and provide comments on the Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report within 60 (sixty) working days from the date of receipt of the report. This 60 (sixty) day period shall expire when the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has provided comments, whether those comments are a rejection, an agreement, or an order for correction of the report.
