2- The 60 (sixty) working day period for review and comments mentioned above shall restart when the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources receives a request for review along with the Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report that the project owner has finally corrected in accordance with the orders or instructions provided by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

3- In cases where the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources fails to provide comments within 60 (sixty) working days, the competent ministries, institutions and/or the Royal Government may determine that the project has met the requirements of this Code.

4- The project owner shall be responsible for all activities caused by their faults resulting in delays or failure to make adjustments in accordance with the above orders or instructions.

#### Article 664 .- Review and Comments on Environmental Protection Contracts

1- Within 30 (thirty) working days, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall review, provide comments, agree, reject, or correct the Environmental Protection Contract and/or the environmental management plan of the project owner. This period shall be counted from the date the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources receives the request to review the Environmental Protection Contract, the environmental management plan, and relevant documents.

2- The 30 (thirty) working day period for reviewing and providing comments on the Environmental Protection Contract and/or the environmental management plan shall restart when the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources receives a request for review that the project owner has finally corrected in accordance with the orders or instructions provided by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

3- In cases where the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources fails to provide comments within 30 (thirty) working days, the competent ministries, institutions, and/or the Royal Government may determine that the project has met the requirements of this Code.

4- The project owner shall be responsible for all activities caused by faults resulting from delays or failure to make adjustments to the Environmental Protection Contract and/or the environmental management plan in accordance with the above orders or instructions.

#### Article 665 .- Agreement, Rejection, or Correction of Full or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment Reports

1- Providing comments of agreement, rejection, or other corrections on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report shall be made considering negative and positive impacts on the environment, economy, society, and culture, as well as economic benefits and the cost of environmental damage.

2- In cases where the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources agrees on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall issue a letter and a certificate of agreement on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report for that project attached with an environmental protection contract.

3- In cases where the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources rejects the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall state the reasons for its decision.

4- In cases where the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources provides comments to make corrections to the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall attach reasons and point out clearly the points to be corrected.

#### Article 666 .- Validity of Full Environmental Impact Assessment Reports, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment Reports, and Environmental Protection Contracts

1- A Full Environmental Impact Assessment report is valid only if that report is prepared by an environmental impact assessment consulting firm that has obtained competence from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources in accordance with procedures, conditions, provisions, and various legal instruments.

2- An Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report is valid only if that report is prepared by the project owner and/or through an environmental impact assessment consultant who is a legal or natural person in accordance with the procedures, conditions, provisions, and various legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

3- An Environmental Protection Contract is valid only if that contract complies with the procedures, conditions, provisions, and various legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 667 .- Validity of Letters and Certificates of Agreement on Environmental Impact Assessment Reports

1- Letters and certificates of agreement on Full Environmental Impact Assessment reports, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment reports, or Environmental Protection Contracts shall be valid according to the development project as determined in a Sub-decree.

2- In cases where the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources finds that there are changes to the master plan and/or the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report is not appropriate or not effective in implementing environmental and social impact mitigation measures, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has the right to require the project owner to re-prepare the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report and/or update the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report to obtain a new letter and certificate of agreement on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report.

#### Article 668 .- Prohibitions on Construction Activities or Project Operations

1- Project owners shall not start construction activities or project operations before receiving a letter or certificate of agreement on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Environmental Protection Contract. In cases where the development project has a letter and certificate...

agreement on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Environmental Protection Contract, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has the right to decide to suspend construction activities or project operations entirely.

2- Project owners shall display the signboard or certificate of agreement on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Environmental Protection Contract in front of the construction site.

3- All concession agreements provided by the Royal Government of Cambodia at both national and sub-national levels must have letters and certificates of agreement on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Environmental Protection Contract.

#### Article 669 .- Transfer or Change of Project Owner

1- In cases where there is a transfer or change of project owner, the letters of agreement on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report and/or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report and/or Environmental Protection Contract and all conditions shall be transferred to the new project owner automatically.

2- The transfer or change of project owner related to the environmental impact assessment is valid and applicable only if the transfer or change receives agreement from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

### CHAPTER 5
## ADDITIONAL ENVIRONMENTAL ASSESSMENT

#### Article 670 .- Purpose of Risk Assessment

Risk assessment is conducted to:

a- Identify and prioritize actions to be implemented.

b- Assist in resource allocation to minimize environmental damage.

c- Determine the degree of environmental restoration necessary to repair negative environmental impacts.

d- Determine the timeline within which environmental restoration can be completed and fully satisfied.

e- Ensure transparency or insurance for all risk management caused by activities or projects.

f- Inform the public.

g- Prevent and minimize negative impacts on the environment and society.

#### Article 671 .- Objectives of Risk Assessment

Risk assessment aims to identify and evaluate actual risks that may occur to the environment, public safety, and human health resulting from decision-making, agreement on activities or projects, in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 672 .- Scope of Risk Assessment

1- Risk assessment shall be conducted by the project owner and submitted to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, relevant ministries and institutions, or competent sub-national administrations before any decisions and agreements are issued, including the issuance of permits or agreements as stated in this Code for activities or projects that have negative impacts on the environment and society.

2- Risk assessment is a part of the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Environmental Protection Contract.

#### Article 673 .- Requirements for Accredited Consultants

All risk assessments must be prepared by an accredited consulting firm or one that has received agreement to perform this work from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and other competent ministries and institutions.

#### Article 674 .- Legal Instruments on Risk Assessment

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall prepare legal instruments on risk assessment including, but not limited to, guidelines on the content and format of risk assessment reports, responsibilities of relevant ministries and institutions, and qualifications required for accredited and agreed risk assessment consultants, using best practice formats, scientific principles, and proper risk assessment principles.

#### Article 675 .- Risk Assessment Reports

The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, other ministries, institutions, or sub-national administrations shall ensure that each risk assessment result report is prepared in accordance with the legal instruments on risk assessment. Such risk assessment reports shall be prepared and finalized by an accredited or agreed consultant to perform this work from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, relevant ministries, institutions, or sub-national administrations.

#### Article 676 .- Preparation of Risk Assessment Reports

The consultant preparing the risk assessment report shall be responsible for preparing the risk assessment report in accordance with the principles stated in this Code.

#### Article 677 .- Access to Information from Risk Assessment

The data management system for all risk assessment activities and finalized risk assessment reports shall be made available to the public for search by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources in accordance with the provisions on the right to access information of this Code. The data management system shall be maintained by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and relevant sub-national administrations.

#### Article 678 .- Sustainability Risk Assessment

All financial institutions and legal persons providing financial guarantees, financial insurance, or loans for activities or projects must conduct sustainability risk assessments to ensure that the proposed activities or projects are fully evaluated and considered regarding environmental impacts that may occur in design, environmental management, operations, and the closure of the proposed activities or projects.

#### Article 679 .- Assessment of Sustainability Risk Costs

Sustainability risk assessment shall ensure that feasibility studies for proposed activities or projects include the costs of environmental impact assessment, including costs for implementing environmental management plans, all responsibilities for remediation and compensation for environmental damage, all payments required for ecosystem services, and adequate financial guarantees for closure, environmental restoration, repair, or project termination as necessary to meet relevant legal requirements.

#### Article 680 .- Liability in Cases of Absence of Sustainability Risk Assessment

All financial institutions and legal entities providing financial guarantees, financial insurance, or loans for activities or projects that do not conduct sustainability risk assessments shall be liable for damages or compensation for damages for significant environmental impacts caused by those activities or projects.

#### Article 681 .- Environmental Impact Assessment and Climate Change

1- Full Environmental Impact Assessments must include the assessment of project impacts that cause climate change, such as calculating the amount of greenhouse gas emissions from the development project, including the release of greenhouse gases.

2- Project owners shall study alternative energy options with low greenhouse gas emissions and study the requirements for mitigating climate change and impacts caused by climate factors.

#### Article 682 .- Analysis and Assessment of Potential Impacts on Climate Change

Full Environmental Impact Assessments must analyze and evaluate the vulnerability of the project resulting from climate change and the project's activities on the environment, economy, society, and culture to achieve economic development with low greenhouse gas emissions.

#### Article 683 .- Health Impact Assessment

Environmental impact assessments shall include health impact assessments, which include:

a- Baseline health data in the project area and of affected people.

b- Predictions of project impacts that may occur due to construction, population waste, and changes to the environment.

- c- Impact mitigation plans to reduce or eliminate negative project impacts and improvement plans shall be implemented by the project owner to improve the health of local communities.

- d- Issues related to health monitoring and management of residual impacts in the short and long term for the project.

#### Article 684 .- Risk Analysis in Health Impact Assessment

In conducting a health impact assessment, the project owner shall:

a- Propose a safety and health management plan as part of the health impact assessment for the working environment, analyzing relevant risks and types of hazards at the project site, such as physical, chemical, biological, and radioactive hazards.

b- Identify and evaluate the close risks of impacts on the safety and health of the local community during project design, construction, implementation, and termination, and propose protection and impact management plans for these stages.

#### Article 685 .- Cumulative Environmental Impact Assessment

1- A Full Environmental Impact Assessment must analyze and evaluate the cumulative impacts caused by existing projects and future projects around those project sites that may create significant environmental, socio-economic, and cultural impacts.

2- In the impact assessment report, the project owner shall evaluate the capacity of physical resources, biological resources, and socio-economic resources to consider additional impacts based on timing, geographical location, and activities of other projects around the project site.

3- Project owners shall consider alternative mitigation measures for cumulative impacts to reduce or avoid those significant cumulative impacts.

#### Article 686 .- Transboundary Environmental Impact Assessment

1- Development projects that may have transboundary environmental impacts shall conduct a transboundary environmental impact assessment in accordance with laws and international legal instruments related to transboundary environmental impact assessments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party. In this case, the transboundary environmental impact assessment shall be included in the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report.

2- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall prepare legal instruments on the criteria for determining signs of transboundary environmental impacts and proceed to conduct transboundary environmental impact assessments.

3- Formats and procedures for implementing transboundary environmental impact assessments shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

### CHAPTER 6
## PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN THE ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT ASSESSMENT PROCESS

#### Article 687 .- Purpose of Public Participation

1- The main purpose of public participation is to ensure that all relevant parties participate in environmental protection, biodiversity conservation, sustainable use of natural resources, sustainable living, and the environmental impact assessment process.

2- Procedures for public participation shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 688 .- Rights of Public Participants to Take Action

Relevant parties who have the right to participate publicly may take the following actions:

a- Report to relevant authorities and disseminate information about any activities or decisions that may have impacts on the environment and natural resources.

b- Propose requests for clarification, protest, and participate in discussions about any activities or decisions that may cause impacts on the environment and natural resources.

c- File further complaints regarding decisions in accordance with the environmental and natural resources dispute resolution mechanism as stated in Content 1 (Procedures for Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution) of Book 9 of this Code.

#### Article 689 .- Obligations of Relevant Ministries, Institutions, Sub-national Administrations, and Project Owners

All relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and project owners, before making any decisions or agreements on any case related to this Code, shall:

a- Inform and provide various necessary information, as well as providing appropriate time for relevant parties for public participation.

b- Provide necessary information to relevant parties, and in providing such information, attention must be paid to understandable language and the culture of those relevant parties.

c- Consider technical and/or scientific knowledge as a basis for creating projects, action plans, and making decisions.

d- Pay attention to the strength and values of relevant parties in the public participation process.

e- Provide appropriate time to evaluate and respond to information related to proposed projects, activities, plans, or any decisions.

f- Consider the extent of public participation to be appropriate with the interests and damages of relevant parties.

g- Ensure that the public participation process is flexible and adjustable according to the expectations of relevant parties.

h- Consider all comments from the public during the decision-making process. The acceptance or rejection of the above comments must be justified with appropriate reasons.

#### Article 690 .- Public Comments in the Environmental Impact Assessment Process

Public participation in the environmental impact assessment process shall:

a- Record the comments of the participating public during the planning and implementation stages of the environmental impact assessment.

b- Focus on issues raised by affected parties that may arise from the proposed project.

c- Include details of the project's impacts on the public and the acceptance or rejection of public proposals.

d- Provide clear reasons for rejection in cases where the project owner rejects public proposals.

#### Article 691 .- Prior Consultation

1- The public participation process shall ensure prior consultation with those affected by the project regarding proposed impact mitigation measures based on consultation and after receiving prior information.

2- In this environmental impact mitigation plan, the project owner shall:

a- Determine measures to improve livelihoods and assist those affected by the project.

b- Ensure that affected persons participate in preparing resettlement plans to minimize relocation impacts and ensure that compensation for property loss is resolved appropriately, correctly, and fairly first, and that environmental impact mitigation measures are proper.

3- In cases where the affected local community does not agree with the mitigation measures proposed by the project owner above, the development project continues, but the project owner must determine other mitigation measures or resolve those impacts for the local community.

4- Procedures for resolving compensation for affected local communities and procedures for resettlement shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

5- Formats and procedures for paying compensation for damages to affected local communities shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and the Ministry in charge of economy and finance.

#### Article 692 .- Public Participation in Mitigation Plans

1- The public participation process shall seek consent from persons affected by the project regarding the proposed impact mitigation measures. Project owners shall provide an environmental management plan to be implemented with public participation in the monitoring and compliance process with conditions stated in the environmental management plan.

2- Environmental Impact Assessment reports and environmental management plans shall consider comments from affected persons regarding the proposed impact mitigation measures.

#### Article 693 .- Public Dissemination

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall ensure that information related to the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report and relevant documents is disseminated publicly, and that relevant parties and local communities affected by the project can access sufficient and clear information.

2- Project owners shall disseminate publicly, through their website, at least the documents or executive summaries of the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report and adjustment plans, as well as various proposed project impact mitigation measures.

3- Procedures for public participation and the right to access information shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

### CHAPTER 7
## ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT PLAN AND PROJECT MONITORING

#### Article 694 .- Inspection and Monitoring of Environmental Management Plan Implementation

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources is the authority to inspect and monitor the implementation of the project owner's environmental management plan, in cooperation with relevant ministries, institutions, sub-national administrations, and relevant parties.

2- Project owners shall implement the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Environmental Protection Contract, and/or the environmental management plan within 6 (six) months from the date the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources agreed on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Environmental Protection Contract, and/or environmental management plan.

#### Article 695 .- Preparation of Environmental Management Plan

1- Environmental management plans shall be prepared by the project owner.

2- Environmental management plans shall include requirements for environmental and social impact mitigation measures determined in the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Environmental Protection Contract.

3- Environmental management plans shall be updated regularly in accordance with the actual evolution of environmental standards, actual practices resulting in general results in each sector, or various changes in the project status.

#### Article 696 .- Internal Environmental Management System

1- All development projects and project operators shall establish a working group in charge of inspecting the implementation of the environmental management plan and establish an internal environmental management system that ensures regular self-monitoring procedures and methods as stated in the environmental management plan.

2- In cases where environmental impacts are larger than the impacts estimated in the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report or environmental management plan, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall take measures to remediate the impacts immediately or adjust the environmental management plan.

3- The adjusted environmental management plan and monitoring program must receive agreement from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources. Conditions, formats, and procedures for adjustment or re-adjustment shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

4- Project owners shall prepare environmental monitoring reports every quarter or semester and submit them to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources for review and evaluation. The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has the right to conduct on-site inspections and verify the monitoring results of the project owner.

#### Article 697 .- Right to Report Environmental Issues

1- Persons affected by the project and relevant parties have the right to report issues and environmental and social concerns to the project owner and file complaints to competent ministries and institutions.

2- Competent ministries and institutions shall respond to complaints or protests and resolve relevant environmental and social issues within an appropriate timeframe and provide information to relevant parties according to the actual situation.

3- Formats and procedures for resolving complaints or protests shall be determined based on the procedures for environmental and natural resources dispute resolution as stated in this Code.

#### Article 698 .- Preparation of Environmental Monitoring Reports

1- Each project owner shall prepare an environmental monitoring report for the project to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

2- Conditions, formats, and procedures for preparing environmental monitoring reports shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 699 .- Submission of Quarterly or Semi-annual Reports

1- Project owners who have letters and certificates of agreement on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Environmental Protection Contract shall submit quarterly or semi-annual reports to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources regarding their environmental monitoring and management.

2- Project owners shall review all deficiencies in the quarterly or semi-annual reports that the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has reviewed and provided comments on, and shall include these deficiencies in the next quarterly or semi-annual report and resubmit to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources for re-review and evaluation. Project owners shall report immediately about negative environmental impacts to relevant ministries and institutions and the public to avoid negative impacts on the environment and society.

3- Project owners shall provide information related to the project's environmental management to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources in accordance with the instructions of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

### CHAPTER 8
## ENVIRONMENTAL AUDIT

#### Article 700 .- Environmental Audit

1- The implementation of any project or activity that has received agreement on a Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, Environmental Protection Contract, or permit shall be subject to an independent environmental audit and shall be compiled as documentation.

2- Conducting an environmental audit includes:

a- Reviewing and verifying any project or activity that complies with the requirements of this Code and relevant legal instruments.

b- Identifying and demonstrating the environmental impacts of any project or activity.

c- Identifying and demonstrating any applicable measures to make corrections in accordance with the requirements.

d- Reducing negative environmental impacts to the minimum level.

#### Article 701 .- Scope of Projects or Activities Subject to Environmental Audit

Types of projects or activities that are subjects of environmental audits, procedures, and specific durations for conducting environmental audits shall be determined by relevant legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 702 .- Projects Required to Conduct External Environmental Audits

Projects determined in separate legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources regarding the list of projects required to have external environmental audits must be audited by an independent environmental auditor who has qualifications as stated in the legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources regarding external environmental audits.

#### Article 703 .- Appointment and Qualifications of Environmental Auditors

1- Persons who have obtained competence as environmental auditors may perform environmental audits with permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

2- Procedures and guidelines for accrediting environmental auditors shall be determined by legal instruments of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 704 .- Roles and Duties of Independent Environmental Auditors

Independent environmental auditors have the following roles and duties:

a- Assist in making accurate and complete environmental records.

b- Provide professional audit opinions, specifically environmental reports showing a complete and appropriate view of the project's environmental management or project operations.

- c- Review the adequacy of the implementation of internal management and environmental monitoring procedures, and provide recommendations for improvement.

- d- Provide information about irregularities and gaps in their internal environmental management.

- e- Provide one original copy of the audit report to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 705 .- Costs of Environmental Audits

All costs related to environmental audits shall be the responsibility of the operator or project owner.

#### Article 706 .- Failure to Comply with Orders or Requirements

1- In cases where the project owner fails to comply with orders or requirements in conducting environmental audits, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall instruct the project owner to select an environmental auditor approved by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources to prepare the environmental audit report for that project or activity.

2- All costs related to the environmental audit as stated in paragraph 1 above shall be the responsibility of the project owner.

#### Article 707 .- Monitoring and Report Preparation

Project owners who have received agreement on the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, Environmental Protection Contract, or permit shall conduct monitoring and prepare environmental audit reports in accordance with the requirements stated in this Code and other relevant legal instruments.

#### Article 708 .- Impact Resolution

In cases where the environmental audit shows that the impacts of the project and/or activity are larger than what was estimated in the Full Environmental Impact Assessment report, Initial Environmental Impact Assessment report, or Environmental Protection Contract, the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall take immediate measures to resolve those impacts and order corrections to the environmental management plan appropriately to avoid future impacts.

#### Article 709 .- Access to Information Related to Audit Reports

The information related to environmental audit reports shall be made available to the public after the report is submitted to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

### CHAPTER 9
## RIGHT TO ACCESS INFORMATION AND NATIONAL ENVIRONMENTAL LISTS

### PART 1
## RIGHT TO ACCESS ENVIRONMENTAL AND NATURAL RESOURCES INFORMATION

#### Article 710 .- Making Information Available for Public Search

1- The Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations shall ensure that information related to environmental and natural resources sectors can be displayed for public search.

2- The format and procedure for information disclosure, as stated in paragraph 1 above, shall be determined by the legal instruments of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, relevant ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 711 ._ Right to Receive Information from Public Ministries and Institutions

1- Individuals have the right to request information related to the environment and natural resources from the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, relevant ministries and institutions, and sub-national administrations, except for confidential information.

2- The right to receive information as stated in paragraph 1 above shall be implemented in accordance with existing laws and legal instruments.

#### Article 712 ._ Right to Receive Information from Private Projects

Individuals have the right to request information related to the implementation of private projects from the project owner, except for confidential information.

#### Article 713 ._ Request for Designation of Confidential Information for Private Investment Projects

1- Private investment project owners have the right to request the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, relevant ministries and institutions, or sub-national administrations to designate information related to any project operations as confidential.

2- The format and procedure for designating confidential information for private investment projects shall be determined by the legal instruments of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, competent ministries and institutions, or sub-national administrations.

### Section 2 National Environmental Information List

#### Article 714 ._ Preparation of the National Environmental Information List

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall prepare the National Environmental Information List in cooperation with relevant ministries and institutions and sub-national administrations.

#### Article 715 ._ Public Access to the National Environmental Information List

The National Environmental Information List is information that the public can search for, except for information that is confidential for national defense or acts or cultural property determined by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

### Section 3 National Environmental Data Center

#### Article 716 ._ National Environmental Data Center

1- A National Environmental Data Center shall be established to compile and store all information related to the environment and natural resources.

2- The organization and functioning of the National Environmental Data Center shall be determined by a sub-decree upon the proposal of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 717 ._ Access to Information from the National Environmental Data Center

All data prepared by the National Environmental Data Center shall be made available for the public to search, except for data considered confidential for national defense and the protection of cultural property and natural heritage.

# Book 6 Responsibility for Environmental and Natural Resources Damage

## General Provisions on Restoration and Compensation for Environmental and Natural Resources Damage

### Chapter 1 General Provisions

#### Article 718 ._ Purpose

This Book aims to define the obligations of project owners and operators in repairing, restoring, and providing compensation for negative impacts or damage to the environment or natural resources.

#### Article 719 ._ Scope

This Book applies to all activities of project owners and operators that cause negative impacts or damage to the environment or natural resources in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### Chapter 2 Responsible Institutions

#### Article 720 ._ Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall be responsible for managing and taking measures against all activities of project owners or operators that cause impacts on the environment or natural resources.

#### Article 721 ._ Roles and Duties

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare legal instruments and technical guidelines to manage and determine the extent and type of damage to the environment or natural resources.

b- Take measures to stop, prevent, and mitigate damage to the environment or natural resources.

c- Prepare plans and procedures for the restoration of environmental or natural resources damage.

d- Take legal action or build cases for court by acting as the plaintiff in claiming compensation for environmental or natural resources damage.

e- Collect and compile data related to impacts on the environment or natural resources.

f- Inspect, monitor, and evaluate environmental or natural resources damage.

g- Conduct public dissemination in cases where environmental or natural resources pollution may affect public health.

h- Compile reports related to environmental or natural resources damage.

### Chapter 3 Responsibility of Project Owners or Operators Before Environmental and Natural Resources Damage Occurs

#### Article 722 ._ Obligation of Project Owners or Operators to Implement Immediate Mitigation Measures for Environmental or Natural Resources Damage

1- In case of an urgent risk that may cause negative impacts or damage to the environment or natural resources due to the implementation of their project activities, the project owner or operator must take immediate measures to prevent environmental or natural resources damage. In case the project owner or operator fails to take measures or the measures taken are insufficient, the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources may require the project owner or operator to take immediate measures to prevent such environmental or natural resources damage.

2- Immediate measures to prevent environmental or natural resources damage as stated in paragraph 1 above shall be appropriate to the extent and type of environmental or natural resources damage. All costs associated with measures to prevent environmental or natural resources damage shall be the responsibility of the project owner or operator.

3- After implementing measures to prevent environmental or natural resources damage, the project owner or operator must immediately notify the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources about the implementation of such measures.

### Chapter 4 Responsibility of Project Owners or Operators When Environmental and Natural Resources Damage Occurs

#### Article 723 ._ Obligation of Project Owners or Operators Causing Environmental or Natural Resources Damage

1- In case of environmental or natural resources damage, the project owner or operator who caused the environmental or natural resources damage has the obligation to be responsible for such damage.

2- Notwithstanding the provisions in paragraph 1 above, the project owner or operator may be exempted from the obligation to be responsible in any of the following cases:

a- Damage caused by war or force majeure.

b- Damage caused entirely by the acts or omissions of a third party other than the personnel, employees, or agents of the project owner or operator.

c- Damage caused by activities permitted by law.

d- Damage caused by specifically permitted activities as described with specificity regarding the type and extent of damage in the environmental and natural resources management plan

natural resources management plan, environmental impact assessment, and/or certificate and letter of decision on environmental impact assessment.

3. The exemption of responsibility as stated in paragraph 2 above shall not apply if the project owner or operator has committed a serious breach in the implementation of licensing procedures or permits, or a serious breach of the conditions set forth in the license or permit.

#### Article 724 ._ Obligation to Restore Environmental or Natural Resources Damage

In case environmental or natural resources damage is caused by project implementation that can be restored to a better condition, the project owner or operator must carry out restoration of the environmental or natural resources damage.

#### Article 725 ._ Obligation to Provide Compensation for Environmental or Natural Resources Damage

1- The project owner or operator must provide compensation for environmental or natural resources damage caused by their project implementation in any of the following cases:

a- Restoration to a better condition is not possible.

b- The project owner or operator fails to implement the restoration of environmental or natural resources damage as stated in Article 724 (Obligation to Restore Environmental or Natural Resources Damage) of this Code.

2- Compensation for environmental or natural resources damage as stated in paragraph 1 above shall be limited to the extent of the environmental or natural resources damage.

#### Article 726 ._ Obligation to Notify about Environmental or Natural Resources Damage

In case of environmental or natural resources damage, the project owner or operator must immediately notify the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources regarding the information of such environmental or natural resources damage.

#### Article 727 ._ Procedures for Restoration and Compensation for Environmental or Natural Resources Damage

The format and procedures for the restoration and compensation for environmental or natural resources damage as stated in Article 724 (Obligation to Restore Environmental or Natural Resources Damage) and Article 725 (Obligation to Provide Compensation for Environmental or Natural Resources Damage) of this Code shall be determined by a sub-decree.

#### Article 728 ._ Right to Claim Costs for Implementing Restoration Measures for Environmental or Natural Resources Damage

Individuals who did not cause environmental or natural resources damage but have spent on implementing restoration measures for environmental or natural resources damage may claim such costs from the project owner or operator who caused the environmental or natural resources damage in accordance with the Civil Code and other existing laws of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

### Chapter 5 Measures for Prevention and Restoration of Environmental or Natural Resources Damage

#### Article 729 ._ Implementation of Mitigation and Restoration Measures for Environmental or Natural Resources Damage

1- Notwithstanding the provisions of Article 722 (Obligation of Project Owners or Operators to Implement Immediate Mitigation Measures for Environmental or Natural Resources Damage) of this Code, in case of an urgent risk that causes environmental or natural resources damage, the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources may take necessary measures to prevent environmental or natural resources damage by itself.

2- Notwithstanding the provisions of Article 724 (Obligation to Restore Environmental or Natural Resources Damage) of this Code, in case of environmental or natural resources damage, the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources may take necessary measures to restore such environmental or natural resources damage by itself.

#### Article 730 ._ Claiming Costs for Implementing Mitigation and Restoration Measures

In case the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has implemented mitigation measures as stated in paragraph 1 of Article 729 (Implementation of Mitigation and Restoration Measures for Environmental or Natural Resources Damage) or restoration measures as stated in paragraph 2 of Article 729 (Implementation of Mitigation and Restoration Measures for Environmental or Natural Resources Damage) of this Code, the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources may claim the costs of implementing such measures from the project owner or operator.

# Book 7 Education, Research, Science, Technology, Innovation, and Environmental Awareness

## General Provisions on Education, Research, Science, Technology, Innovation, and Environmental Awareness Policies and Strategies

### Chapter 1 General Provisions

#### Article 731 ._ Purpose

This Book aims to promote education, scientific research, technological development, innovation capacity, and environmental awareness for sustainable development, harmony in a good, clean, and safe ecosystem, and appropriate use of natural resources through strengthening and expanding research capacity, education, cooperation, and participation of relevant institutions.

#### Article 732 ._ Scope

This Book applies to ministries, institutions, the private sector, educational establishments, associations and non-governmental organizations, unions, and other organizations that perform tasks related to scientific research, technological development, innovation capacity, education, vocational training, and providing public awareness about the environment.

### Chapter 2 Responsible Institutions

#### Article 733 ._ Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources is responsible for scientific research, technological development, innovation capacity, environmental data management, and leading and coordinating environmental education and information dissemination, as well as organizing major environmental events.

#### Article 734 ._ Roles and Duties of the Ministry in Charge of the Environment and Natural Resources

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has the following roles and duties:

a- Prepare draft policies, legal instruments, strategic plans, action plans, budget plans, guidelines, and programs related to scientific research, technological development, and innovation capacity related to the environmental sector, data management, education, and environmental information dissemination, as well as environmental performance evaluation.

b- Collect, analyze, compile, and manage environmental information and data, including building and managing an integrated environmental data system.

c- Organize and manage environmental information dissemination systems.

d- Prepare reports on the national environmental state in cooperation and coordination with relevant ministries, institutions, and sub-national administrations.

e- Promote environmental awareness among the public through the integration of environmental education into formal and non-formal systems, dissemination of environmental knowledge, and encouragement of eco-friendly practices.

f- Produce and disseminate through information systems activities, situations, and achievements related to environmental protection, natural resources management, and sustainable living.

g- Organize and manage public events related to environmental information dissemination activities.

h- Coordinate and cooperate with various units under the relevant ministries, institutions, development partners, and the private sector to strengthen cooperation and increase the efficiency of environmental data management and educational dissemination work through environmental information systems.

### Chapter 3 Policies, Strategies, and Coordination Mechanisms

#### Article 735 ._ National Environmental Education Policy

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall cooperate to prepare a draft national environmental education policy, which serves as the foundation for developing environmental educational strategies and action plans in formal systems at all levels of vocational training, scientific research, technological development, innovation capacity, and disseminating environmental knowledge through all means and forms. The formal scientific national environmental education, technological development, and environmental innovation capacity policy may be reviewed and revised as necessary and based on the actual evolution of economy, society, and environment.

#### Article 736 ._ Coordination Mechanism for the Implementation of the National Environmental Education Policy

The implementation of the National Environmental Education Policy shall be coordinated by an inter-ministerial mechanism in charge of national scientific educational research, technological development, and environmental innovation capacity policy, which shall be established by the Royal Government to encourage the integration of scientific research strategic plans and action plans, technological development, and environmental innovation capacity into national and sub-national development plans.

#### Article 737 ._ Environmental Education Strategic Plans and Action Plans

Ministries, institutions, and units related to vocational training, research, and public dissemination of the environment shall prepare environmental educational strategic plans and action plans, scientific research, technological development, and environmental innovation capacity within the context of their jurisdiction in accordance with the national scientific research environmental education, technological development, and environmental innovation capacity policy. Environmental educational strategic plans and action plans, scientific research, technological development, and environmental innovation capacity shall be updated as necessary and based on actual needs.

#### Article 738 ._ Implementation of Environmental Education Strategic Plans and Action Plans

Sub-national administrations at all levels, units, or establishments under the relevant ministries and institutions and related parties shall implement these environmental educational strategic plans and action plans, scientific research, technological development, and environmental innovation capacity appropriately according to the actual situation of their locality, aiming to ensure environmental quality, ecosystem balance, and sustainable living.

### Chapter 4 Environmental Education and Research

#### Section 1 Environmental Education and Training in the Formal Education System

#### Article 739 ._ Environmental Education and Training Programs

All educational and research establishments under the relevant ministries and institutions shall have a core curriculum on the environment and materials for education, scientific research, technological development, innovation capacity, and vocational training in their respective units. The preparation of the core environmental curriculum and teaching materials shall be in accordance with the national scientific research environmental education policy, technological development, environmental innovation capacity, and the evolution of economy, society, culture, technology, and science.

#### Article 740 ._ Teaching Resources and Professors

All educational and research establishments under the relevant ministries and institutions related to environmental education shall build and develop qualified teaching resources and professors to ensure the efficiency and effectiveness of the implementation of strategic plans and action plans of the respective ministries and institutions.

#### Article 741 ._ Levels of Education and Training

All educational and research establishments under the relevant ministries and institutions related to environmental education shall determine the minimum educational and training programs in accordance with the goals and requirements of the educational and training work of their respective units.

#### Section 2 Research, Science, Technology, and Innovation

#### Article 742 ._ National Environmental Resource Center

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall establish an environmental resource center as a hub for collecting environmental data, information, science, technology, and innovation for the benefit of students, researchers, policymakers, and the public.

#### Article 743 ._ Scientific Research, Technology, and Innovation

Ministries, institutions, scientific research establishments, and operators related to the environmental sector shall cooperate to organize scientific forums, programs, or joint research activities as a collaboration between policymakers, scientists, and practitioners to have a scientific database for strategic planning and decision-making in solving environmental protection challenges, biodiversity conservation, natural resources management, and sustainable development.

### Chapter 5 Environmental and Eco-friendly Awareness

#### Section 1 Public Dissemination of Environment

#### Article 744 ._ Environmental Knowledge Dissemination Programs

Relevant ministries and institutions, sub-national administrations at all levels, and related parties shall have action plans and programs for disseminating knowledge on environmental protection, natural resources management, biodiversity conservation, and sustainable living.

#### Article 745 ._ Environmental Knowledge Dissemination Activities

Sub-national administrations at all levels, competent professional units, public establishments, the private sector, and related parties have the obligation and duty to disseminate policies, strategies, legal instruments, and knowledge related to the environment and natural resources broadly through all means and forms.

#### Article 746 ._ Participation in Promoting Environmental Awareness Every citizen has the duty to participate in promoting public awareness of the necessity and benefits of environmental protection, natural resources management, biodiversity conservation, and sustainable living.

#### Section 2 Eco-friendliness

#### Article 747 ._ Recognition and Incentive for Eco-friendly Practices

Individuals who have participated in eco-friendly activities and used environmental support technologies shall receive recognition and incentives.

#### Article 748 ._ Authority to Grant Recognition and Incentives

The recognition and incentives for individuals who have participated in eco-friendly activities and used environmental support technologies shall be determined by legal instruments prepared by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 749 ._ Eco-friendly Competitions

Relevant ministries and institutions, sub-national administrations, and the private sector shall ensure the sustainability of organizing competition programs and incentives for individuals who have practiced eco-friendly activities and used environmental support technologies.

#### Section 3 Eco-friendly Schools

#### Article 750 ._ Eco-friendly Schools

1- The Eco-friendly School Award program shall be implemented and competed every 2 (two) years to encourage general educational establishments, particularly primary and secondary schools, to:

a- Establish school policies and administration for environmental work.

b- Create environmental lessons and environmental education activities in schools and surrounding areas as supplementary or integrated curriculum.

c- Provide environmental educational materials, infrastructure, and environmental practices in general educational establishments.

d- Cooperate with relevant parties and disseminate environmental education and practices in areas around general educational establishments.

2- The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and the ministry in charge of education, youth, and sports have the duty to lead, manage, evaluate, and decide on all matters related to this Eco-friendly School Award program.

#### Article 751 ._ Indicators for Eco-friendly School Award Candidates

Each educational establishment that is a candidate for the Eco-friendly School Award shall be evaluated on 15 (fifteen) indicators, including:

a- Vision and mission that reflect environmental promotion.

b- Environmental practice principles.

c- Leadership structure for implementing the Eco-friendly School program.

d- Budget plan for implementing environmental activities.

e- Reporting system for environmental and financial activities.

f- Core and supplementary or integrated curricula that include environmental education lessons.

g- Building teacher capacity on environment and natural resources.

h- Lessons and learning activities suitable for local and/or regional contexts.

i- Teaching materials related to the environment.

j- Action groups for disseminating environmental education.

k- Equipment used for eco-friendliness.

l- Creativity and innovation of eco-friendly activities.

m- Green spaces and green areas.

n- Teaching materials for direct out-of-classroom implementation.

o- Implementation of eco-friendly activities and students' behavior in participating in eco-friendly implementation.

p- Partnering and stakeholders.

q- Level of partnership and support.

r- Dissemination activities in the locality.

#### Article 752 ._ Eco-friendly School Activities

In addition to the indicators stated in Article 751 (Indicators for Eco-friendly School Award Candidates) of this Code, the evaluation of winning candidates for the Eco-friendly School Award shall also consider other activities, including:

a- Planting grass, trees, and flowers.

b- Reducing or stopping the use of fossil fuel energy and using clean energy through the use of renewable energy.

c- Water conservation.

d- Waste management by segregating waste types and reducing, reusing, and recycling waste.

e- Prohibiting the sale of food with artificial flavors, colors, and smells within the school premises.

f- Prohibiting the use of plastic bags for food and beverage packaging at stalls within the school premises.

g- Regular cleaning of classrooms and toilets to ensure good hygiene and health.

h- Disseminating Earth Day, Plastic Free Day, and the Royal Ploughing Ceremony Day, etc.

i- Providing awards to students with good environmental education works and practices.

j- Environmental art competitions such as fashion shows using recycled materials, photography competitions, and drawing competitions.

#### Article 753 ._ Eco-friendly School Award

1- The award is divided into three categories, including:

a- Gold award valid for 4 (four) years.

b- Silver award valid for 2 (two) years.

c- Bronze award valid for 2 (two) years.

2- Award winners are not allowed to participate in the Eco-friendly School Award competition again as long as their current award remains valid.

# Book 8 Economic and Financial Provisions, Fees, and Environmental Funds

## Content 1 Economic and Financial Provisions

### Chapter 1 General Provisions

#### Article 754 ._ Purpose

This Book aims to define the rules and procedures for the management of economic and financial provisions, fees, funds, and the management of environmental and social funds that support the environment, natural resources, society, and culture.

#### Article 755 ._ Scope

This Book applies to economic and financial provisions, fees, and environmental and social funds established in the Kingdom of Cambodia in accordance with the Royal Government's policies, laws, and legal instruments, including international instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party.

### Chapter 2 Responsible Institutions

#### Article 756 ._ Responsible Ministries and Institutions

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, in cooperation with the ministry in charge of the economy and finance, shall be responsible for organizing and defining economic measures, financial funds, and fees related to the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 757 ._ Roles and Duties of the Ministry in Charge of the Environment and Natural Resources

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources has the following roles and duties:

a- Coordinate financial and fund-related work related to the environment and natural resources with relevant institutions.

b- Prepare legal instruments related to financial principles and funds related to the environment and natural resources for approval from the Royal Government or the ministry in charge of the economy and finance.

c- Responsibility and coordination in preparing strategic budget plans and annual budget drafts in accordance with Royal Government policies and plans.

d- Monitor and evaluate the implementation of budget plan projects.

e- Prepare budget reports and economic measures to support the environment, natural resources, society, and culture.

f- Monitor, inspect, and encourage the implementation of collection and use of environmental and social funds.

g- Manage and use protected area funds and environmental vulnerability funds.

### Chapter 3 Economic Measures

#### Article 758 ._ Assessment of Opportunities and Incentive Mechanisms for Environmental and Natural Resources Investment

1- The ministry in charge of the economy and finance shall lead the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and relevant ministries and institutions to conduct annual assessments of opportunities and incentive mechanisms for investment in environmental protection, conservation, management, and restoration of natural resources.

2- The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources or relevant ministries and institutions shall prepare plans, reports, and proposals for economic measures in accordance with the priority policies of their respective units and the ministry in charge of the economy and finance beforehand for review, cooperation, and agreement in case it is necessary to submit to the head of the Royal Government for review and decision regarding these incentives and measures.

#### Article 759 ._ Economic Measures to Support Environmental and Natural Resources Policies

1- The ministry in charge of the economy and finance, in cooperation with the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and relevant competent ministries and institutions, shall prepare draft policies and legal instruments on economic measures to support the environment, natural resources, society, and culture in accordance with laws and specific legal instruments related to audits and contracts.

2- The preparation of draft policies and legal instruments as stated in paragraph 1 above shall be in accordance with international legal instruments to which the Kingdom of Cambodia is a party and shall provide for economic measures related to:

a- Integrating standards related to environmental and social performance and risk management into decision-making of legal entities in the economic and financial sectors.

b- Private sector investment related to the environment and natural resources.

c- Reducing forest and natural resources exploitation and promoting biodiversity and ecosystem conservation.

d- Production and circular economy.

e- Sustainable hunting and fisheries.

f- Fresh water conservation, including promoting efficient use of water resources and research for water quality protection and management and reducing wastewater and liquid waste discharge.

g- Community-based natural resources management, including exemption from value-added tax and other taxes on income, fees, funds, or other contributions received from the implementation of joint management.

h- Development and investment in responsible energy infrastructure, such as small-scale electricity.

i- Promoting the use of renewable energy systems, including solar, wind, hydro, and ocean wave energy, and other installation equipment.

j- Use of clean energy, resource-efficient production, low-carbon emissions, and reduction of environmental and public health pollution.

k- Use of the best environmental technologies that have been tested and verified.

l- Development of infrastructure and services for the purpose of environmental protection and natural resources management.

m- Equitable distribution of income from carbon mechanisms as determined by the National Council for Sustainable Development.

n- Payment for production or distribution of natural resource and ecosystem services to individuals and communities in cooperation with sub-national administrations.

o- Promoting the import and purchase of eco-friendly vehicles and preventing the import of vehicles with high impacts on the environment and public health.

p- Promoting responsible energy use, including support for the use of responsible energy sources in business, businesses that trade in installing or supplying technical parts or maintaining responsible energy equipment, businesses that import, produce, distribute, or sell responsible energy production equipment or increase energy efficiency with appropriate qualifications in accordance with health, safety, and environmental regulations, and the import of responsible energy production or energy efficiency equipment.

q- Conservation and protection of cultural heritage.

r- Energy conservation through increasing knowledge and efficiency of electricity use, preventing energy loss, recycling energy waste, and replacing with renewable energy and other means of energy conservation as determined by competent ministries and institutions.

s- Other relevant economic measures determined by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 760 ._ Proposals for Private or Public Companies

1- The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, in cooperation with competent ministries and institutions, may issue permits for proposals from private or public companies to organize environmental support infrastructure and sustainable development in accordance with existing laws and legal instruments.

2- The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources may require the company to include supporting evidence and documents in their proposal as stated in the paragraph above, confirming resources and financial status, market studies, use of sustainable sourcing plans, demonstration of the project's contribution to sustainable development that is resilient to climate change and greenhouse gas emissions, and the capacity to provide technical support throughout the process.

3- The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall implement the acquisition process as defined in relevant policy guidelines concerning private or public companies.

### Chapter 4 Financial Provisions

#### Article 761 ._ Payment of All Types of Revenue

All types of revenue payments, in accordance with the provisions of this Code, shall be paid into the treasury account or partner banks as authorized by the ministry in charge of the economy and finance, unless otherwise specifically agreed between the ministry in charge of the economy and finance and the competent ministry or institution.

#### Article 762 ._ Payment for Ecosystem Services

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall organize the format and mechanism for defining payments for ecosystem services to individuals who have contributed to the inspection, protection, or management of ecosystem services based on data and best interests found related to the state and value of the ecosystem services that have been evaluated.

#### Article 763 ._ Cooperation of Competent Ministries and Institutions

Competent ministries and institutions shall support and cooperate with the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources to organize, implement, and manage the mechanism as stated in Article 762 (Payment for Ecosystem Services) of this Code.

## Content 2 Fees, Funds, and Management of Environmental and Social Funds

### Chapter 1 Fees, Royalties, or Environmental Premiums

#### Article 764 ._ Responsibility for Paying Fees, Royalties, or Environmental Premiums

Individuals shall be responsible for paying fees, royalties, environmental premiums, or other charges in accordance with inter-ministerial proclamations of the ministry in charge of the economy and finance and the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, laws, and existing legal instruments.

#### Article 765 ._ Organization of Fees, Royalties, or Environmental Premiums

The ministry in charge of the economy and finance, in cooperation with the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources or competent ministries and institutions, shall prepare legal instruments to define the amount

amount, procedures for management, and exemptions from paying any fees, royalties, or environmental premiums related to this Code, including:

a- Environmental impact assessment.

b- Environmental management and sustainability mechanisms.

c- Sustainable natural resources management.

d- Conservation and management of cultural heritage in natural protected areas.

e- Waste and environmental pollution management.

f- Environmental education and awareness.

g- Environmental and natural resources dispute resolution.

h- Other relevant sectors determined by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 766 ._ Financial Guarantee for Paying Fees, Royalties, or Premiums

1- The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and competent ministries and institutions may require individuals who have the obligation to pay fees, royalties, premiums, or other charges to provide sufficient financial guarantees for these payments before obtaining the rights to officially implement activities or projects.

2- The ministry in charge of the economy and finance, in cooperation with the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and competent ministries and institutions, shall prepare legal instruments on criteria and procedures for guarantees for paying fees, royalties, and premiums.

### Chapter 2 Payment and Management of Environmental and Social Funds

#### Section 1 Payment of Environmental Funds

#### Article 767 ._ Payment of Environmental Vulnerability Fund

Individuals shall pay an annual environmental vulnerability fund in accordance with laws and existing relevant legal instruments. The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall manage this fund in accordance with the provisions on the environmental and social fund of this Code.

#### Article 768 ._ Payment of Natural Protected Area Fund

Individuals shall pay a natural protected area fund in accordance with laws and relevant legal instruments. The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall manage this fund in accordance with the provisions on the mechanism and use of the natural protected area fund and provisions on the environmental and social fund of this Code.

#### Article 769 ._ Payment of Environmental and Social Fund

Individuals shall pay an environmental and social fund in accordance with laws and relevant legal instruments. The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall manage this fund in accordance with the provisions on the environmental and social fund of this Code.

#### Section 2 Environmental and Social Fund

#### Article 770 ._ Sources of Revenue for Environmental and Social Fund

The environmental and social fund has the following sources of revenue:

a- Initial capital from the Royal Government.

b- Contributions from special environmental taxes as determined by the Royal Government.

c- Contributions from civil aviation service fees as determined by the Royal Government.

d- Premiums from public resource development project owners as stated in the environmental management plan of the environmental impact assessment report and/or environmental protection contracts.

e- Revenue from environmental protection, ecosystem services, and biodiversity conservation.

f- Contributions from the environmental vulnerability fund.

g- Contributions from the natural protected area fund.

h- Contributions from ticket sales for visiting natural protected areas.

i- Service fees from leasing locations for antenna poles and other infrastructure under the jurisdiction of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

j- Contributions from service fees for leasing locations for electrical networks and water pipe networks under the jurisdiction of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

k- Contributions from leasing locations for agro-industrial investment projects with a size of less than 10 (ten) hectares under the jurisdiction of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

l- Contributions from carbon credit sales.

m- Money from dispute resolution in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

n- Donations or financial aid, materials, and equipment from philanthropists, development partners, and national or international private institutions.

o- Contributions from other environmental service fees.

p- Other sources.

#### Article 771 ._ Use of Environmental and Social Fund

The environmental and social fund shall be used for the following activities:

a- Environmental protection, natural resources management, biodiversity and ecosystem conservation, and sustainable living.

b- Restoration and improvement of biodiversity and ecosystem resources.

c- Research on environment, natural resources, and culture, biodiversity, and ecosystems.

d- Participation in infrastructure development and improvement of local community livelihoods within and near investment project areas or other areas.

e- Participation in supporting the establishment and development of local communities.

f- Participation in local community development projects, including providing small-scale loans as revolving funds.

g- Education and dissemination about environment, natural resources, culture, biodiversity, and ecosystems.

h- Human resource training for the environmental and natural resources sector.

i- Other activities related to the environment and natural resources sector.

#### Article 772 ._ Environmental and Social Fund Management Committee

1- The environmental and social fund shall be managed by an Environmental and Social Fund Management Committee established by sub-decree.

2- The roles, duties, and composition of the Environmental and Social Fund Management Committee shall be determined by a decision of the Royal Government.

3- The Environmental and Social Fund Management Committee shall prepare a strategy for long-term fund management. This strategy shall include considerations for procurement methods, fund management, and investment capitalization, administrative measures, legal measures, and other relevant policies. The Environmental and Social Fund Management Committee shall have a secretariat as its support unit. The organization and functioning of this secretariat shall be determined by a proclamation of the minister in charge of the environment and natural resources.

4- Procedures for revenue collection management and fund use management for the environmental and social fund shall be determined by inter-ministerial proclamations of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and the ministry in charge of the economy and finance.

5- Expenses related to performing the duties and roles of the Environmental and Social Fund Management Committee shall be determined by inter-ministerial proclamations of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and the ministry in charge of the economy and finance.

### Chapter 3 Establishment and Management of Environmental Trusts

#### Article 773 ._ Rights of Settlors

A settlor has the following rights:

a- Receive information related to trust management, including financial reports, audit reports, and other relevant documents.

b- Appoint and terminate trustees in accordance with the conditions defined in the trust instrument.

#### Article 774 ._ Obligations of Settlors

1- A settlor has the following obligations:

a- Transfer property or trust funds to the trustee to manage and dispose of on behalf of and for the benefit of beneficiaries.

b- Participate in coordinating the transfer of trust property to the trustee for management and disposal.

c- Provide written notice to the ministry in charge of the economy and finance regarding changes of the settlor or trust contributors or changes in the content of the trust instrument.

d- Comply with the conditions defined in the trust instrument and existing regulations.

2- In case the settlor does not have the capacity or permanent ability to perform their obligations, the ministry in charge of the economy and finance shall perform some functions as a substitute settlor.

#### Article 775 ._ Rights of Beneficiaries

1- A beneficiary has the following rights:

a- Receive benefits as stated in the trust instrument.

b- Demand the trustee to perform obligations as defined in the trust instrument and existing regulations.

c- Receive information related to the management and disposal of trust property.

d- Have other rights as stated in the trust instrument and according to existing regulations.

2- Beneficiaries may receive benefits from the trust from the effective date of the trust unless there are other relevant regulations.

3- In case there are multiple beneficiaries, they may receive the same or different benefits according to the manner or method stated in the trust instrument. In case there is no specified manner or method, it shall be assumed that the beneficiaries receive equal benefits.

#### Article 776 ._ Rights of Trustees

 A trustee has the following rights:

a- Receive remuneration or fees for managing the trust, which may vary according to their work experience and/or professional qualifications.

b- Seek other aid funds and additional investment opportunities for the benefit of the trust.

c- Represent the settlor or beneficiary before the law or competent authorities.

d- Comply with the conditions defined in the trust instrument or existing regulations.
