e- Manage and dispose of the trust property according to the purposes and conditions of the trust instrument.

f- Spend on social activities in accordance with the purposes and conditions of the trust instrument.

#### Article 777 ._ Obligations of Trustees

A trustee has the following obligations:

a- Apply for trust registration according to the forms and procedures of the ministry in charge of the economy and finance.

b- Report and provide information related to trust management to the settlor, beneficiary, and relevant regulators.

c- Keep minutes of meetings for annual account management.

d- Respond to relevant regulators.

e- Pay all types of taxes related to trust property.

f- Take appropriate measures to protect trust property and beneficiaries.

g- Enter into contracts that promote responsibility in the function as a trustee.

h- A trustee cannot transfer their function to another person.

i- Clearly segregate personal property from trust property.

j- Manage trust property with care and caution.

k- Maintain honesty and integrity in managing trust property.

l- Manage and dispose of trust property efficiently and effectively for the benefit of the beneficiary.

m- Register the inventory of trust property.

n- Provide regular financial reports to relevant regulators and to the ministry in charge of the economy and finance.

o- Cannot receive benefits from other parties in case of a conflict of interest.

p- Comply with conditions defined in the trust instrument or existing regulations.

#### Article 778 ._ Liability of Trustees

A trustee shall be liable for the following actions:

a- Causing loss and decrease of trust property without proper reasons.

b- Using trust property contrary to the trust purposes.

c- Mistakes committed intentionally in performing their work.

d- Any expenditure contrary to the trust purposes.

e- Avoiding direct and indirect conflicts of interest that lead to impact or damage to the beneficiary's interests.

f- Other liabilities according to the conditions of the trust instrument or existing regulations.

#### Article 779 ._ Establishment, Dissolution, and Purposes of Environmental Trusts

1- Environmental trusts are established in accordance with the procedures and forms determined by the ministry in charge of the economy and finance and shall be registered at the ministry in charge of the economy and finance. A settlor who intends to establish or operate an environmental trust shall prepare a trust instrument and register it at the ministry in charge of the economy and finance in accordance with relevant legal instruments on trusts.

2- A settlor may act alone or in cooperation with local or foreign associations to establish or operate a trust.

3- An environmental trust may be dissolved in accordance with the defined conditions.

4- Environmental trusts shall be used for activities determined as priorities in national and sub-national plans for the purposes of:

a- Protecting, conserving, and restoring the environment and natural resources.

b- Protecting cultural heritage and biodiversity.

c- Promoting sustainable natural resources management and ecosystem services.

d- Promoting environmental research and education.

e- Developing institutional capacity.

f- Support communities directly impacted by development.

g- For public benefit and environmental benefit as determined by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 780 ._ Environmental Trust Management

1- Environmental trusts include, but are not limited to, trusts for environmental protection, biodiversity conservation, sustainable and appropriate natural resources use, and sustainable living, and other forms of benefit-sharing in environmental, social, and economic aspects in the Kingdom of Cambodia.

2- Environmental trusts are managed by trustees through the transfer of property or funds from the settlor to the trustee for management for the benefit of beneficiaries in accordance with the settlor's instructions.

#### Article 781 ._ Legal Instruments on Environmental Trust Management and Inspection

The ministry in charge of the economy and finance, by coordinating with the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, shall prepare legal instruments on the rules and procedures for the establishment, management, and inspection of environmental trusts and these fund accounts.

#### Article 782 ._ Registration of Environmental Trusts

1- An environmental trust is valid only if it is registered at the ministry in charge of the economy and finance within no more than 3 (three) months after the date of establishment of the environmental trust. The ministry in charge of the economy and finance shall make a decision on the registration application within 90 (ninety) working days after receiving a proposal that meets sufficient conditions for registration.

2- The forms and procedures for environmental trust registration shall be determined by a legal instrument of the ministry in charge of the economy and finance.

#### Article 783 ._ Environmental Trust Property

1- Environmental trust property may be formed from:

a- National budget.

b- Official development assistance provided by national and international organizations.

c- Payments for ecosystem services.

d- Natural resources management services.

e- Direct donations from the private sector, philanthropists, and other sources.

2- All benefits arising from the environmental trust property shall be paid into and formed as part of the environmental trust property.

#### Article 784 ._ Conflict of Interest regarding Environmental Trusts

1- During the performance of their roles and duties, the trustee shall report conflicts of interest to the ministry in charge of the economy and finance.

2- In case the ministry in charge of the economy and finance has sufficient evidence and reasons to believe that the trustee is committing acts with conflicts of interest toward the environmental trust or acting contrary to the interests of the environment or the public, the ministry in charge of the economy and finance shall coordinate with the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources to initiate an investigation into this matter.

3- The ministry in charge of the economy and finance shall participate in the trustee's functions in case the investigation finds that the trustee has a conflict of interest with their trust management. In this case, the ministry in charge of the economy and finance shall appoint a temporary trustee in compliance with the requirements of this Code and in accordance with the trust instrument.

#### Article 785 ._ Annual Reports List for Public Access

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall maintain a list of annual reports of every environmental trust that the public can access and search.

#### Article 786 ._ Use of Environmental Trust Property

The trustee may use environmental trust property to:

a- Provide funds for project activities that fulfill the purposes of the environmental trust in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

b- Pay fees on environmental trust operations, such as trustee remuneration and various expenses in managing the environmental trust.

c- Spend on social activities in accordance with the purposes of the trust instrument.

d- Attest investment funds and securities that are environmentally and socially responsible.

#### Article 787 ._ Dissolution of Environmental Trusts

1- Environmental trusts shall be dissolved in any of the following cases:

a- The arrival of the time period defined in the trust instrument.

b- The fulfillment of the purposes of the environmental trust.

c- Determination by the settlor's discretion or absolute unanimous opinion.

d- A decision by a competent court.

e- Other cases as stated in the trust instrument.

2- The ministry in charge of the economy and finance shall be notified of every stage of the dissolution process of this environmental trust.

#### Article 788 ._ Appointment of Temporary Administrators

The settlor or substitute settlor has the right to appoint a temporary administrator to manage the trust in case of seeing risks to the state and conditions defined in the trust instrument.

#### Article 789 ._ Appointment of Liquidators

1- In case an environmental trust is dissolved, the settlor or substitute settlor shall appoint a professional liquidator to manage and evaluate the trust. A liquidator has the following duties:

a- Complete and finish the remaining tasks of the trustee.

b- Collect and pay remaining debts.

c- Evaluate remaining trust property.

2- Upon fulfilling these requirements, the liquidator shall provide final accounting and reports to the ministry in charge of the economy and finance and the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources. The remaining environmental trust property shall be distributed in accordance with the environmental trust instrument.

3- Remuneration for liquidators shall be calculated based on standard professional rates and paid from the environmental trust property.

#### Article 790 ._ Effect of Environmental Trust Dissolution

1- After the liquidation of the environmental trust, the trustee shall:

a- Transfer all remaining environmental trust property in accordance with the conditions of the environmental trust instrument.

b- Transfer all environmental trust property to all living beneficiaries in equal shares in case the environmental trust does not define the individuals to receive said property.

2- After the dissolution of the environmental trust, the trustee remains liable for losses and damages caused by intentional mistakes, negligence, misinterpretation, or dishonesty.

#### Article 791 ._ Disputes Related to Environmental Trust Operations

1- Disputes related to environmental trust operations shall be resolved by the Trust Dispute Resolution Council of the ministry in charge of the economy and finance, unless otherwise determined by relevant regulators.

2- Any party dissatisfied with the decision of the Trust Dispute Resolution Council of the ministry in charge of the economy and finance may file an administrative complaint or a lawsuit to a competent court within 30 (thirty) days from the date of the decision.

3- The results of all dispute resolution procedures shall be made available for search by the public.

#### Article 792 ._ Inappropriate Management or Use of Environmental Trusts

Any trustee who manages or uses an environmental trust inappropriately or contrary to the trust's purposes, causing damage to the environment, shall be liable for their actions in accordance with the provisions of this Code and other relevant legal instruments on trusts.

#### Article 793 ._ Environmental Trusts for Co-management

1- Environmental trusts for co-management shall be established to support co-management activities within each co-management area. This environmental trust shall be under the management of the Co-management Committee of the co-management area, with inspection, monitoring, and evaluation by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, ensuring transparency in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

2- The Co-management Committee may receive revenue from sources including, but not limited to:

a- Environmental and Social Fund.

b- Various private and public sources.

c- Various services, rental fees, usage fees, recreation fees, or entrance fees to the co-managed community area.

d- Contributions, donations, and aid.

#### Article 794 ._ Use of Revenue, Expenses, or Various Contributions

1- The use of revenue, expenses, or various contributions obtained through co-management implementation shall be used only for the benefit of the local community within the co-management area to support co-management implementation and for conservation within the co-management area and adjacent conservation areas. Clear methods for distributing revenue, expenses, or various contributions shall be defined in the co-management agreement.

2- Revenue, expenses, or various contributions obtained from co-management work shall receive priority for tax exemptions and other charges as defined by laws and relevant legal instruments.

### Chapter 4 Financing for Environmental Management Plans

#### Article 795 ._ Financial Resources for Environmental Management Plans

1- Applicants of projects required to have an environmental management plan in accordance with the provisions of Content 2 (Environmental Impact Assessment) of Book 5 of this Code shall provide a guarantee of sufficient financial resources for the implementation of the project's environmental management plan before the issuance of the project's approval letter or certificate.

2- The types and amounts of financial guarantees, criteria, resolution procedures, and facilitation of implementation for existing project owners and projects receiving approval from the Royal Government shall be determined by inter-ministerial proclamations of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and the ministry in charge of the economy and finance.

#### Article 796 ._ Budget for Environmental Management Plans

1- Detailed budget estimated for implementing the project owner's environmental management plan shall be included in the environmental management plan, including:

a- Budget for measures to mitigate environmental, natural resource, and social impacts.

b- Budget for monitoring.

c- Budget for the functioning of dispute mechanisms and project operations.

d- Budget for resettlement responsibility and compensation for individuals affected by the project.

e- Payments for any ecosystem services as stated in this Code.

f- Budget for site closure, rehabilitation, remediation, or project activity termination.

g- Other reserve budgets related to project environmental management plan implementation.

2- The project owner is responsible for implementing the environmental management plan using their own budget, which is not an environmental and social fund contribution.

#### Article 797 ._ Financial Guarantee Procedures

The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall prepare legal instruments on criteria and procedures for determining sufficient financial guarantees in implementing the environmental management plan of a requested project, including:

a- Scope of financial guarantee for plan implementation.

b- Acceptable forms of financial guarantees.

c- Amount of money to be provided in the financial guarantee based on size, scale, location, and potential impacts.

d- Timeframe for exchange regarding financial resources placed as guarantee; if no exchange occurs, these financial resources shall be made searchable.

#### Article 798 ._ Auditing and Financial Reporting

1- Project owners required to provide financial guarantees for environmental management plan implementation shall comply with requirements for auditing and report preparation in accordance with relevant legal instruments on auditing and reporting.

2- Project owners shall perform annual reviews of various cost estimates related to the environmental management plan and submit these estimates to the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources. This estimation shall be performed by an independent auditor.

3- In case the annual audit report shows non-compliance with the requirements for the financial guarantee, the project owner shall notify the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and other competent ministries and institutions.

4- Project owners shall comply with updated financial guarantee requirements within 30 (thirty) days after notification.

5- The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources may require additional reports and documents when necessary.

6- In case the annual audit report shows a financial guarantee that exceeds the requirements, the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources may submit a proposal to the ministry in charge of the economy and finance to allow the withdrawal of the excess funds as requested by the project owner.

# Book 9 Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution

## Content 1 Mechanisms and Procedures for Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution

### Chapter 1 General Provisions

#### Article 799 ._ Purpose

This Book determines the mechanisms and procedures for resolving environmental and natural resources disputes.

#### Article 800 ._ Scope

This Book has the scope of application on resolving disputes related to the environment and natural resources for the following complaints or demands:

a- Demand for compensation for environmental or natural resource damage.

b- Demand for restoration of environmental or natural resource damage.

c- Demand for determining measures to ensure that no new environmental or natural resource pollution or damage is caused.

d- Demand for determining measures to prevent negative impacts occurring to the environment or natural resources.

#### Article 801 ._ General Principles of Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution

1- Regarding environmental and natural resources dispute resolution, any party to the dispute may bring the dispute to the environmental and natural resources dispute resolution mechanism for mediation before filing a lawsuit to court by implementing in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

2- However, the provisions of paragraph 1 above do not prevent the use of the right to file a lawsuit directly to court. In necessary cases, a court receiving a complaint related to an environmental and natural resources dispute may send the case to the environmental and natural resources dispute resolution mechanism for mediation first. In case there is no resolution through mediation before the environmental and natural resources dispute resolution mechanism, parties have the right to request the court to continue its procedures in accordance with existing laws and procedures.

3- Mediation for environmental and natural resources dispute resolution as stated in paragraph 1 above shall not be applied to criminal offenses punishable according to this Code.

4- Mediation for environmental and natural resources dispute resolution does not prevent the enforcement of other criminal provisions if the facts that are the subject of the mediation constitute an offense punishable according to this Code or other existing criminal laws.

#### Article 802 ._ Parties to Environmental and Natural Resources Disputes

Parties who have the right to file environmental and natural resources complaints include:

a- Affected parties who may take legal action to demand compensation for damage or mental suffering.

b- The ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources or sub-national administrations who have the right to take legal action to demand compensation for damage to the environment or natural resources, or to restore the environment, or to prevent any activity that causes environmental impact or pollution and damage to natural resources.

c- Associations and organizations registered in accordance with laws and recognized by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, which may take legal action to stop or prevent any activity, demand compensation for damage to the environment or natural resources, or demand compensation for damage or mental suffering on behalf of victims or communities. However, regarding the demand for compensation for damage or mental suffering for victims or communities, there must be a valid letter of power of attorney.

### Chapter 2 Mechanisms for Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution

#### Article 803 ._ Structure of Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committees

1- Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committees have the following structure:

a- National Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committee.

b- Capital and Provincial Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committees.

2- The organization and functioning of the National Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committee and the Capital and Provincial Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committees shall be determined by sub-decree upon the proposal of the minister in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 804 ._ Jurisdiction of Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committees

1- The National Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committee has the jurisdiction to mediate environmental and natural resources disputes when any party to the dispute files a complaint dissatisfied with the mediation of the Capital or Provincial Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committee, or in cases where it receives a complaint directly from any party to the dispute.

2- Capital and Provincial Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committees have the jurisdiction to mediate environmental and natural resources disputes within their respective capital or provincial jurisdictions.

### Chapter 3 Procedures for Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution

#### Article 805 ._ Mediation of Environmental and Natural Resources Disputes

Mediation of environmental and natural resources disputes shall be conducted based on existing laws and regulations.

2- Forms and procedures for environmental and natural resources dispute mediation by the National Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committee and the Capital and Provincial Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committees shall be determined by sub-decree upon the proposal of the minister in charge of the environment and natural resources and the minister of justice.

#### Article 806 ._ Settlement Agreement

1- A settlement agreement shall be made before the National or Capital/Provincial Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committee and shall be in writing, signed or thumbprinted by all parties to the dispute, and signed/thumbprinted as witnesses by all members of the committee.

2- A settlement agreement as stated in paragraph 1 above shall have the same executive force as an enforcement letter, unless the agreement has a defect.

3- This settlement agreement is not an obstacle for third parties in exercising their legal rights to protect their interests.

#### Article 807 ._ Further Appeals in Case of Unsuccessful Settlement

In case mediation is unsuccessful at the capital or provincial level, parties to the dispute have the right to appeal to the National Environmental and Natural Resources Dispute Resolution Committee within 90 (ninety) days at the latest after receiving the record of unsuccessful settlement. In case mediation is unsuccessful at the national level, parties have the right to file a lawsuit to a competent court in accordance with existing laws and procedures.

## Content 2 Natural Protected Area Management Officers and Environmental Inspection Officers

### Chapter 1 General Provisions

#### Article 808 ._ Purpose

This Content aims to determine the forms and procedures for performing the duties of natural protected area management officers and environmental inspection officers.

#### Article 809 ._ Scope

This Content has the scope of application on the work activities of natural protected area management officers and environmental inspection officers.

### Chapter 2 Responsible Institutions

#### Section 1 Natural Protected Area Management Officers

#### Article 810 ._ Appointment and Performance of Duties of Natural Protected Area Management Officers and Granting of Judicial Police Status

1- The minister in charge of the environment and natural resources shall appoint national-level natural protected area management officers. Sub-national administrations shall appoint...

sub-national levels within their respective jurisdictions. The appointment of natural protected area management officers shall be based on qualifications determined by a proclamation of the minister in charge of the environment and natural resources.

2- Natural protected area management officers have the duties to monitor, research, inspect, and enforce laws within natural protected areas.

3- National and sub-national natural protected area management officers shall be granted judicial police status to investigate natural resource offenses within their jurisdictions, and they have the power to search, seize evidence, summon relevant individuals, and perform other procedures by implementing the Code of Criminal Procedure of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

4- The granting of judicial police status and jurisdiction for natural protected area management officers as judicial police shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation of the ministry of justice and the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources. The forms and procedures for granting judicial police status to natural protected area management officers shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation of the ministry of justice and the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

5- The format of natural resource offense records shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation of the ministry of justice and the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 811 ._ Cooperation and Investigation of Offenses by Judicial Police

Natural protected area management officers have the right to request assistance from sub-national administrations, armed forces, and other relevant institutions to participate in inspecting and suppressing natural resource offenses or searching for and temporarily seizing evidence of offenses.

#### Article 812 ._ Uniforms during Missions

1- During law enforcement operations, natural protected area management officers shall wear uniforms, identification tags, and rank insignia, and shall have mission order letters.

2- Uniforms, identification tags, and rank insignia of natural protected area management officers shall be determined by sub-decree.

#### Article 813 ._ Use of Weapons

1- Natural protected area management officers who have been granted judicial police status have the right to use weapons in accordance with laws and other existing legal instruments.

2- Weapons shall be equipped and managed by the ministry of interior.

#### Section 2 Environmental and Natural Resources Inspection Officers

#### Article 814 ._ Appointment of Environmental Inspection Officers

1- Inspections related to environmental protection and environmental conservation shall be implemented by inspection officers of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, inspection officers of competent ministries and institutions, or inspection officers of sub-national administrations within their respective jurisdictions in accordance with this Code, laws, and existing legal instruments.

2- Forms and procedures for inspections related to environmental protection and environmental conservation shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and other relevant competent ministries and institutions.

#### Article 815 ._ Roles and Duties of Inspection Officers of the Ministry in Charge of the Environment and Natural Resources

Inspection officers of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources have the following roles and duties:

a- Conduct inspections, monitoring, research, and inspection, and promote the enforcement of the provisions of this Code related to environmental protection and environmental conservation under the jurisdiction of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

b- Prepare reports on inspection results for leadership review and decision.

c- Take other measures within the framework of Code enforcement.

d- Perform other roles and duties as assigned by the minister in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 816 ._ Appointment and Performance of Duties of Environmental Inspection Officers and Granting of Judicial Police Status

1- Environmental inspection officers of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, environmental inspection officers of competent ministries and institutions, or environmental inspection officers of sub-national administrations shall be granted judicial police status to investigate environmental offenses within their jurisdictions, and they have the power to search, seize evidence, summon relevant individuals, and perform other procedures by implementing the Code of Criminal Procedure of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

2- Granting judicial police status and jurisdiction to environmental inspection officers as stated in paragraph 1 above shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation of the ministry of justice and the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources. Forms and procedures for granting judicial police status to environmental inspection officers shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation of the ministry of justice and the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

3- The format of environmental offense records shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation of the ministry of justice and the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

#### Article 817 ._ Use of Uniforms by Environmental Inspection Officers

1- Environmental inspection officers of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, environmental inspection officers of competent ministries and institutions, or environmental inspection officers of sub-national administrations shall have mission order letters and wear uniforms, identification tags, and rank insignia during the performance of their roles and duties.

2- Uniforms, identification tags, and rank insignia of environmental inspection officers of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, inspection officers of competent ministries and institutions, or sub-national administration inspection officers shall be determined by sub-decree.

#### Section 3 Seized Evidence of Environmental and Natural Resources Offenses

#### Article 818 ._ Types of Seized Evidence of Environmental and Natural Resources Offenses

1- Seized evidence of offenses stated in this Code include, but are not limited to:

a- Natural resource products and by-products, hazardous substances, or products containing hazardous substances that are seized evidence related to the offense.

b- Materials, tools, equipment, and means used in committing the offense.

c- Assets obtained as proceeds of the offense.

2- For seized natural resource products and by-products that are perishable, natural protected area management officers have the right to release, destroy, or keep them for public benefit with a proper record and approval from the prosecutor.

#### Article 819 ._ Expenses for Seized Hazardous Substances or Products Containing Hazardous Substances

Expenses for transportation, storage, and destruction of seized hazardous substances or products containing hazardous substances shall be the responsibility of the owner of the seized evidence.

#### Article 820 ._ Preservation and Management of Seized Plants and Geological Pieces

Regarding preservation and management of seized plants and rare geological pieces, or rare geological outcome products:

a- In case of living specimens: These wild plants shall be replanted in their original soil or similar soil, or handed over to any institution for the purpose of breeding, propagation, or to educational institutions, research institutions, conservation organizations, wild plant specimen storage sites, or botanical gardens determined by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

b- In case of dead wild specimens or geological pieces that cannot be planted: They shall be kept at a location determined by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources after approval from the prosecutor.

#### Article 821 ._ Preservation and Management of Seized Wildlife, Pieces, or Wildlife Specimens

1- Preservation and management of seized dead wildlife, organ pieces, or wildlife specimens shall be kept in a safe place or at a location determined by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources after approval from the prosecutor.

2- In case of living seized wildlife, they shall receive care, emergency rescue, and first aid, and shall be handed over to a wildlife rescue center determined by the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources. Implementation of this method shall have a clear record and photos or videos as documentation.

#### Article 822 ._ Confiscation of Seized Evidence of Environmental and Natural Resources Offenses as State Property

Seized evidence of environmental and natural resources offenses stated in this Code shall be confiscated as state property by court decision, unless otherwise determined by relevant regulations.

# Book 10 Penalty Provisions

## Content 1 General Provisions

### Core Chapter Common Provisions

#### Article 823 ._ Types of Sanctions

Sanctions in this Code include administrative sanctions and criminal sanctions.

#### Article 824 ._ Administrative Sanctions

1- Administrative sanctions include, but are not limited to:

a- Warning.

b- Activity suspension, temporary suspension or revocation of licenses, permits, certificates, contracts, agreements, or projects.

c- Cancellation or revocation of licenses, permits, certificates, contracts, or agreements.

2- Warning, activity suspension, temporary suspension, revocation, or cancellation of permits, certificates, contracts, agreements, or projects, and license revocation are under the jurisdiction of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources, or other competent ministries and institutions, or sub-national administrations within their jurisdictions in accordance with laws and existing legal instruments.

3- Offenses subject to the above administrative sanctions shall be determined by a proclamation of the minister in charge of the environment and natural resources or a proclamation of relevant competent ministers.

4- Any individual dissatisfied with the decision on administrative sanctions stated in points 'b' and 'c' of paragraph 1 above has the right to file an appeal to the minister in charge of the environment and natural resources or the competent minister within 30 (thirty) days from the date of receiving the decision. The minister in charge of the environment and natural resources or the competent minister shall decide on the appeal within 30 (thirty) days from the date of receiving the appeal. Any individual dissatisfied with the decision of the minister in charge of the environment and natural resources or the competent minister on the appeal has the right to file a lawsuit to a competent court in accordance with procedures within 30 (thirty) days from the date of receiving the decision. Filing an appeal by the individual above does not have the effect of suspending the implementation of the decision on administrative sanctions.

#### Article 825 ._ Criminal Sanctions

1- Criminal sanctions include, but are not limited to:

a- Transactional fines.

b- Fines (monetary penalty).

c- Imprisonment.

2- Transactional fines for environmental and natural resources offenses are under the jurisdiction of officers granted judicial police status to investigate environmental and natural resources offenses in accordance with this Code and other relevant laws.

3- Payment of transactional fines results in the termination of the public action (criminal prosecution). Payment of transactional fines is not an obstacle to implementing administrative sanctions and criminal sanctions in accordance with the provisions of this Code and other existing criminal laws.

4- Forms and procedures for transactional investigations, payment of transactional fines, management of payment receipts, and management of revenue from transactional fines for offenses stated in this Code shall be determined by an inter-ministerial proclamation of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources or competent ministries, the ministry of justice, and the ministry of economy and finance. The Royal Government may decide to provide incentives to officers who participated in suppressing offenses stated in this Code.

#### Article 826 ._ Offenses Subject to Transactional Fines

1- Violations of Article 156 (Compliance with Emission Standards), Article 161 (Duty to Avoid Nuisance from Noise, Vibration, or Odor), Article 173 (Application and Types of Waste Permits), Article 189 (Hazardous Substance Registration), Article 191 (Registration of Production or Use of Materials and Toys Containing Hazardous Substances), Article 206 (Prohibition from Causing Hazardous Substance Spillage), Article 234 (Solid Waste Business Licenses for Hotels), Article 235 (Solid Waste Resource Recovery and Recycling Service Licenses), Article 236 (Solid Waste or Raw Material Output from Recycling), Article 287 (Responsibility of Specific Source Owners), Article 298 (Prohibition of Discharge), Article 299 (Vessel Owner Responsibility), Article 317 (Taking Measures and Notifying Air Pollution Spillage), Article 659 (Environmental Impact Assessment Consultants as Legal or Natural Persons), and Article 668 (Prohibitions on Construction or Project Operations) of this Code shall be determined as offenses subject to transactional fines, the amounts of which shall be determined by sub-decree.

2- Besides the offenses subject to transactional fines stated in paragraph 1 above, other offenses in the environmental and natural resources sector subject to transactional fines shall be determined by sub-decree.

## Content 2 Environmental and Natural Resources Offenses

### Chapter 1 Offenses Related to Environmental Management

#### Article 827 ._ Activities or Operations Related to Mining Excavation and Processing

1- Any individual who conducts activities or operations related to mining excavation and processing and causes impacts or damage to the environment, society, economy, biodiversity, human health, or property shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) year to 5 (five) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 500,000,000 (five hundred million) Riels.

2- Any individual who causes impacts or damage to the environment, society, economy, biodiversity, human health, or property as stated in paragraph 1 above and fails to fulfill their obligations as stated in Article 46 (Obligations of the Person Causing Impact or Damage) of this Code shall be punishable by double the penalty.

#### Article 828 ._ Violation of Obligation to Stop Environmental Damage

Any individual who fails to use available technology and best practices to stop causing pollution or serious damage to the environment as stated in Article 147 (Termination of Environmental Damage) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels.

### Chapter 2 Offenses Related to Environmental Protection

#### Article 829 ._ Waste or Pollutant Discharge Contrary to Emission Standards

Any individual who discharges waste or pollutants into the environment without complying with emission standards as stated in paragraph 1 of Article 156 (Compliance with Emission Standards) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 830 ._ Violation of Obligation to Avoid Nuisance from Noise, Vibration, or Odor

Any individual who conducts activities discharging noise, vibration, or odor that causes nuisance to living or impacts human health or public or private interests as stated in Article 161 (Duty to Avoid Nuisance from Noise, Vibration, or Odor) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 831 ._ Waste Discharge or Business Operation without Permit

Any individual who discharges waste or conducts a waste business without a permit as stated in paragraph 1 of Article 173 (Application and Types of Waste Permits) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 832 ._ Failure to Register Hazardous Substances

Any individual who fails to register the production, import, or distribution of hazardous substances or products containing hazardous substances under the jurisdiction of the ministry in charge of the environment and...

natural resources as stated in Article 189 (Hazardous Substance Registration) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 833 ._ Production, Import, or Distribution of Hazardous Substances or Chemical Products

1- Any individual who produces, imports, or distributes hazardous substances or chemical products containing hazardous substances under the jurisdiction of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources without permission in accordance with existing laws and regulations shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) year to 3 (three) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 300,000,000 (three hundred million) Riels.

2- Any individual who produces, imports, or distributes hazardous substances or chemical products containing hazardous substances under the jurisdiction of the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources without permission in accordance with existing laws and regulations and causes impacts to the environment, biodiversity, or public health shall be punishable by imprisonment from 2 (two) years to 5 (five) years and a fine from 200,000,000 (two hundred million) Riels to 500,000,000 (five hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 834 ._ Failure to Register Products or Equipment and Toys Containing Hazardous Substances

Any individual who fails to register the production, import, or distribution of products or equipment and toys containing hazardous substances as stated in Article 191 (Registration of Production or Use of Materials and Toys Containing Hazardous Substances) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 835 ._ Causing Exposure of Hazardous Substances

1- Any individual who conducts activities causing the exposure of hazardous substances into the environment as stated in Article 206 (Prohibition from Causing Hazardous Substance Spillage) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

2- Any individual who conducts activities causing the exposure of hazardous substances into the environment and causes impacts to the environment, biodiversity, or public health shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) year to 5 (five) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 500,000,000 (five hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 836 ._ Provision of Industrial Solid Waste Collection, Transportation, Treatment, and Disposal Site Services without Permission

Any individual who provides collection, transportation, treatment, and final disposal site services for industrial solid waste without a permit as stated in Article 234 (Industrial Solid Waste Business Licenses) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 837 ._ Provision of Solid Waste Recovery and Resource Recovery Services without Permission

Any individual who provides recovery or resource recovery services, or waste treatment services without a permit as stated in Article 235 (Solid Waste Resource Recovery and Recycling Service Licenses) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 838 ._ Export of Solid Waste or Raw Materials from Solid Waste Recovery without Permission

Any individual who exports solid waste or raw materials from solid waste recovery without a permit as stated in Article 236 (Export of Solid Waste or Raw Materials from Recovery) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 839 ._ Import of Solid Waste

Any individual who imports solid waste from abroad into the Kingdom of Cambodia in violation of the provisions of Article 237 (Solid Waste Import) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) year to 5 (five) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 500,000,000 (five hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 840 ._ Failure to Fulfill Obligations of Hazardous Waste Owners

Any individual who fails to comply with paragraph 1 of Article 242 (Hazardous Waste Owner's Obligations) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) year to 3 (three) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 300,000,000 (three hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 841 ._ Hazardous Waste Discharge without Permission

Any individual who fails to comply with Article 243 (Hazardous Waste Discharge Permits) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 2 (two) years to 5 (five) years and a fine from 200,000,000 (two hundred million) Riels to 500,000,000 (five hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 842 ._ Import of Hazardous Waste

Any individual who imports hazardous waste from abroad into the Kingdom of Cambodia in violation of the provisions of Article 264 (Prohibition of Hazardous Waste Import) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) year to 5 (five) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 500,000,000 (five hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 843 ._ Failure to Fulfill Obligations of Owners of Production, Business, and Service Sites Generating Hazardous Waste and Hazardous Waste Operators

Any individual who fails to fulfill their obligations as stated in Article 268 (Obligations of Owners of Production, Business, and Service Sites Generating Hazardous Waste and Hazardous Waste Operators in Production, Business, or Service Activities) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 844 ._ Activities Causing Environmental Impact

Any individual who stores, discharges, burns, buries, or discards hazardous waste and causes impacts to the environment, public health, or causes property damage as stated in Article 272 (Impact-Causing Activities) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) year to 5 (five) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 500,000,000 (five hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 845 ._ Failure to Implement Obligations in Liquid Waste and Wastewater Management

Any individual who fails to implement the management of their liquid waste and wastewater before discharge into public water areas according to the conditions stated in Article 287 (Responsibility of Specific Source Owners) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 846 ._ Waste Discharge without Prior Treatment or Permission

1- Any individual who discharges liquid waste, wastewater, oil waste, or chemical waste from land, islands, oil pumping sites, or fishing tools into the sea without prior treatment or permission as stated in Article 298 (Prohibition of Waste Discharge) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

2- Any individual who discharges liquid waste, wastewater, oil waste, or chemical waste from land, islands, oil pumping sites, or fishing tools into the sea without prior treatment or permission and causes impacts to the environment, biodiversity, or public health shall be punishable by imprisonment...

from 1 (one) year to 5 (five) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 500,000,000 (five hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 847 ._ Irresponsibility of Vessel Owners

Any individual who fails to comply with paragraph 3 of Article 299 (Vessel Owner Responsibility) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 848 ._ Failure to Take Measures and Notify Air Pollution Spillage

Any individual who fails to comply with the provisions stated in Article 317 (Taking Measures and Notifying Air Pollution Spillage) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

### Chapter 3 Offenses Related to Natural Protected Area Management

#### Article 849 ._ First Degree Natural Resource Offenses in Natural Protected Areas

Public action shall be taken with imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels against any individual who commits any of the following acts within a natural protected area:

a- Exporting or importing plant seeds, crossbreeding animal or rare plant species or rare alien plant species and all types of natural genetic resources without permission.

b- Negligence, carelessness, recklessness, or failure to fulfill obligations causing forest fires or peatland fires.

c- Using various tools that cause nuisance from noise, vibration, waves, and smoke that could cause damage to natural resources and environmental protection within core zones and conservation zones.

d- Mining without a permit.

#### Article 850 ._ Second Degree Natural Resource Offenses in Natural Protected Areas

Public action shall be taken with imprisonment from 1 (one) year to 5 (five) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 500,000,000 (five hundred million) Riels against any individual who commits any of the following acts within a natural protected area:

a- Establishing any type of kiln or establishing processing bases for timber products and by-products, wild plants, and all types of natural resources within core zones and conservation zones.

b- Establishing a base for processing agarwood, Mountain basil/Cinnamosma, or other plants, or for serving other prohibited plants within core zones and conservation zones.

c- Felling, knocking down, girdling, tying, poisoning, damaging, uprooting, clearing, or destroying forests and wild plants categorized in classifications 5 and 6 of wild plant species without permission.

d- Catching, chasing, trapping, snaring, using nets or electrical tools that cause damage, poisoning, killing, or collecting eggs and young of all wildlife types categorized in classifications 5 and 6 of wildlife species without permission.

e- Transporting, collecting, stockpiling, or gathering natural resource products and by-products without permission.

f- Using explosive weapons, chemicals, poisons, or electric shock tools that cause damage or pollution to natural resources.

g- Trading wildlife or all types of wildlife specimens or raising wildlife for commercial purposes without permission.

h- Harassing or destroying egg-laying sites, young, or breeding sites of wildlife and fish.

i- Moving, removing, or destroying boundary marks or boundary markers of a natural protected area.

j- Destroying public infrastructure.

k- Filling or clearing water areas, rainwater surface areas, waterways, natural lakes, primeval land, low-lying areas, tidal areas, underground water, or water basins without permission.

l- Obstructing the flow of sea water, rivers, streams, canals, or creeks without permission.

m- Putting up fence posts or placing markers on state land within natural protected areas for the purpose of illegal possession to take ownership.

n- Fishing in violation of the provisions of this Code which causes damage to natural resources in both sea and freshwater areas, flooded forests, mangroves, coral reefs, and seagrass within sustainable use zones and local community zones.

o- Taking stones, gravel, sand, or minerals, or excavating, clearing, or digging land without permission.

#### Article 851 ._ Third Degree Natural Resource Offenses in Natural Protected Areas

Public action shall be taken with imprisonment from 5 (five) years to 10 (ten) years against any individual who commits any of the following acts within a natural protected area:

a- Importing or exporting wildlife species or all types of plant seeds which are wildlife or plants categorized in classifications 2 to 4 of wild plant and wildlife species without permission.

b- Felling, knocking down, girdling, tying, poisoning, damaging, uprooting, clearing, or destroying forests and wild plants categorized in classifications 2 to 4 of wild plant species.

c- Intentionally causing forest fires or peatland fires.

d- Fishing in violation of the provisions of this Code which causes damage to natural resources in both sea and freshwater areas, flooded forests, mangroves, coral reefs, and seagrass within core zones and conservation zones.

e- Catching, chasing, trapping, snaring, using nets or electrical tools that cause damage, poisoning, killing, or collecting eggs and young of all wildlife types in any form categorized in classifications 2 to 4 of wildlife species without permission.

f- Felling, excavating, or clearing forest land, or plowing or bulldozing forest land for the purpose of illegal possession to take ownership.

#### Article 852 ._ Intentional Damage, Destruction, or Recovery of Underwater Cultural Heritage

Any individual who intentionally causes damage, destruction, or recovers underwater cultural heritage as stated in Article 427 (Prohibitions against Damage, Recovery, or Destruction of Underwater Cultural Heritage) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) year to 5 (five) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 500,000,000 (five hundred million) Riels.

### Chapter 4 Offenses Related to Environmental Assessment

#### Article 853 ._ Professional Practice of Environmental Impact Assessment Consulting Companies without Registration

Any individual who fails to comply with paragraph 1 of Article 659 (Environmental Impact Assessment Consultants as Legal or Natural Persons) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

#### Article 854 ._ Misleading Conditions by Falsifying or Hiding Data

Any individual who submits or provides reports, data, or information on environmental impact assessment or risk assessment for the purpose of misleading or causing misunderstanding to the ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) year to 3 (three) years and a fine from 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels to 300,000,000 (three hundred million) Riels.

#### Article 855 ._ Project Construction or Operation Activities in Violation of Prohibitions

Any individual who conducts construction activities or project operations before receiving a letter or certificate of approval for a full environmental impact assessment report or an initial environmental impact assessment report or environmental protection contract as stated in Article 668 (Prohibitions on Construction or Project Operations) of this Code shall be punishable by imprisonment from 1 (one) month to 1 (one) year and a fine from 10,000,000 (ten million) Riels to 100,000,000 (one hundred million) Riels, in case the offender has previously received transactional fines twice and still continues the offense within 1 (one) year from the previous transactional fine.

### Chapter 5 Aggravating Circumstances, Additional Penalties for Natural Persons, Criminal Liability of Legal Entities, and Implementation of Other Criminal Laws

#### Article 856 ._ Aggravating Circumstances

Acts stated in Article 827 (Activities or Operations Related to Mining Excavation and Processing), paragraph 2 of Article 833 (Production, Import, or Distribution of Hazardous Substances or Chemical Products), paragraph 2 of Article 835 (Causing Exposure of Hazardous Substances), Article 839 (Import of Solid Waste), Article 841 (Hazardous Waste Discharge without Permission), Article 842 (Import of Hazardous Waste), Article 844 (Activities Causing Environmental Impact), and paragraph 2 of Article 846 (Waste Discharge without Prior Treatment or Permission) of this Code shall be punishable with aggravating circumstances in the following cases:

a- Imprisonment from 5 (five) years to 10 (ten) years in case any of the above acts causes serious damage to the environment, biodiversity, human health, or causes permanent disability to another person.

b- Imprisonment from 7 (seven) years to 15 (fifteen) years in case any of the above acts causes the death of another person without the intent to kill.

c- Imprisonment from 15 (fifteen) years to 30 (thirty) years in case any of the above acts causes the death of multiple persons or in case the act was committed with the intent to cause serious damage to the environment, biodiversity, health, or life of others, or was committed by an organized criminal group.

#### Article 857 ._ Additional Penalties for Natural Persons

1- For natural persons committing offenses stated from Article 827 (Activities or Operations Related to Mining Excavation and Processing) to Article 855 (Project Construction or Operation Activities in Violation of Prohibitions) of this Code, the court may declare one or more additional penalties as stated in Article 53 (Types of Additional Penalties) of the Criminal Code.

2- The content, forms, and procedures for implementing these additional penalties shall comply with the provisions of the Criminal Code.

#### Article 858 ._ Criminal Liability of Legal Entities

Legal entities may be declared criminally liable under the conditions stated in Article 42 (Criminal Liability of Legal Entities) of the Criminal Code for offenses stated from Article 827 (Activities or Operations Related to Mining Excavation and Processing) to Article 855 (Project Construction or Operation Activities in Violation of Prohibitions) of this Code. Legal entities shall be punishable by a fine from 1,000,000,000 (one billion) Riels to 2,000,000,000 (two billion) Riels and one or more additional penalties as stated in...

#### Article 859 ._ Implementation of Other Criminal Laws

The implementation of the provisions of Book 10 of this Code does not prevent the implementation of other criminal laws in case the act determined as an offense in this Code is also an offense provided in other criminal laws, without consideration of other compensation for damages.

# Book 11 Transitional Provisions

#### Article 860 ._ Transitional Measures for Projects with Applications Submitted Before the Effective Date of this Code

For projects with applications submitted before the effective date of this Code, they shall be considered as submitted and the remaining procedures shall continue in accordance with the provisions of this Code.

#### Article 861 ._ Transitional Measures for Projects Implemented Before the Effective Date of this Code

For projects implemented before the effective date of this Code, they shall continue to be valid after the effective date of this Code.

#### Article 862 ._ Transitional Measures for Provisions Related to Environmental Standards

Provisions related to environmental standards that are existing and effective in this paragraph shall remain in effect until there are new provisions related to environmental standards, unless otherwise provided in this Code.

# Book 12 Final Provisions

#### Article 863 ._ Effectiveness of Legal Provisions

All legal instruments under the laws in the environment and natural resources sector shall remain in effect until replaced by new instruments, unless any provision is contrary to this Code.

#### Article 864 ._ Abrogation

1- The following previous laws and provisions related to the environment and natural resources sector shall lose their effect from the effective date of this Code:

a- Law on Environmental Protection and Natural Resources Management, promulgated by Royal Decree No. NS/RKM/1296/36, dated December 24, 1996.

b- Law on Natural Protected Areas, promulgated by Royal Decree No. NS/RKM/0208/007, dated February 15, 2008.

c- Law on Biosafety, promulgated by Royal Decree No. NS/RKM/0208/006, dated February 15, 2008.

2- Other legal provisions that are effective when this Code is implemented and are contrary to the provisions of this Code shall be considered ineffective to the extent they are contrary to this Code from the date this Code is implemented.

3- However, provisions determining environmental and natural resources offenses in paragraphs 1 and 2 above shall continue to be effective after the implementation of this Code for offenses committed before this Code was implemented.

4- Every provision of this Code shall be applied when there are no specific rules stated by special laws.

#### Article 865 ._ Implementation of the Code

This Code shall be implemented within 1 (one) year after its entry into force.

Done at the Royal Palace, June 29, 2023

**Norodom Sihamoni**

Having submitted to the King for Royal Signature, Prime Minister

**Samdech Akka Moha Sena Padei Techo Hun Sen**
