## Glossary

**Annex to the Environment and Natural Resources Code**

**Glossary**

### K

**Capacity Building**

Refers to activities implemented to strengthen the strengths, qualifications, and resources available to individuals, communities, institutions, or society to respond to changes.

**Nuclear Safeguard**

Refers to a system of inspections to verify that nuclear materials, including technology, activities, equipment, materials, and related facilities, are used for peaceful purposes only.

**Greenhouse Gas Mitigation**

Refers to human intervention to reduce the sources of greenhouse gas emissions or to enhance greenhouse gas sinks.

**Disaster Risk Reduction**

Refers to the formulation and presentation of policy goals and objectives, strategic measures, and various instruments used to assess future disaster risks by reducing hazards, exposure, or existing vulnerabilities and by increasing resilience to those risks.

**Resources Mobilization**

Refers to the collection of financial and non-financial resources to achieve the goals and objectives of institutions/entities. Financial resources include money, while non-financial resources include physical materials, human resources, and provided time.

**Genetic Modification**

Refers to techniques for changing the natural characteristics of animals, plants, or microorganisms by inserting any part of a gene (DNA) from one organism into another.

**Recycling**

Refers to activities, separation processes, or transformation of waste and solid waste into raw materials for reproduction.

**Environmental Degradation**

Refers to negative impacts on the environment, including deterioration, destruction, loss, or loss of balance of environmental components such as natural resources, cultural heritage, etc., and negative impacts on human health and well-being, such as loss of income, profit, taxes, fees, and revenue from various national enterprises, etc.

**Severe Environmental Degradation**

Refers to the extent of deterioration, destruction, loss, or decrease in the quality or function of the environment, such as water, land, air, natural resources, ecosystems, and cultural heritage, as well as impacts...

...negative impacts on human health, economy, and society, such as loss of wages, income, profit, taxes, fees, and revenue from various national enterprises, etc.

**Financial Management**

Refers to the preparation of budget plans, monitoring and directing financial activities, such as monitoring accounting systems, procurement, loans, etc., and the management and decision-making on the use of financial resources of institutions/entities.

**Sustainably Forest Resources Management/Sustainable Forest Management**

Refers to the management of forest resources in accordance with goals, guidelines, and technical standards for economic, social, cultural, and environmental benefits, including biodiversity conservation.

**Forest Management**

Refers to a system of practices for the possession and use of forest land with the aim of fulfilling ecological functions (including biological diversity), economic, and social functions of forests sustainably.

**Biodiversity Resources Management**

Refers to activities for the maintenance and respect for the diversity of living organisms on land, in water, and in other ecosystems, which include genetic diversity, species diversity, and ecosystem diversity.

**Emission Quota**

Refers to the quantity or share of total greenhouse gas emissions assigned to a country or group of countries that can be emitted under a framework of maximum total greenhouse gas emissions and based on resource analysis and obligations.

**Environmental Education**

Refers to the application of methods to educate people on the ethical relationships between them and the environment, promoting awareness, skills, attitudes, and values of people in environmental protection activities, as well as encouraging them to participate in this sector.

**Formal Environmental Education**

Refers to environmental education that is structured according to the hierarchy of the education system, from primary school to higher education, including general specialization studies, vocational skills programs, and various specialized institutes providing full-time technical and vocational skills training.

**Informal Environmental Education**

Refers to environmental education activities organized outside the formal education system, whether graded or ungraded, in any specialized manner, to serve target groups studying in environmental education programs.

**Hydrological Period**

Refers to the determination of the duration of financial operations included in river basin water resource plans or water use right plans and legal instruments.

**Wildlife Farm/ Wildlife Raising/ Wildlife Breeding**

Refers to the keeping of wildlife in captivity, with care and feeding, for various purposes such as breeding, trade, etc.

**Customary Uses**

Refers to practices or methods of using natural resources for family subsistence or community interests according to traditions and customs.

**Sustainable Consumption/ Sustainable Uses**

Refers to the use of natural resources in a manner that maintains the quantity, type, and source of natural resources or biodiversity for the benefit of future generations.

**Contained Uses (Living Modified Organisms)**

Refers to any operation or activity involving living modified organisms conducted within a facility, installation, or equipment and under specific control measures that effectively prevent the contact of such organisms with the external environment.

**Afforestation**

Refers to the planting of trees to establish forest on land that has historically been non-forest (for at least 50 years).

**Wildlife Hunting/Wildlife Poaching**

Refers to any activity that tracks, captures, harasses, destroys, or kills wildlife using any means.

**Wildlife Conservation**

Refers to the planning, management, direction, monitoring, tracking, and research of wildlife within and outside areas for the purpose of preventing extinction by restoring animal populations, species, and wildlife breeding.

**Family Uses**

Refers to hunting, fishing, gathering wild plants, and other forest by-products, and traditional barter for the purpose of providing food for an individual or their family at a minimum level necessary for subsistence, and does not include the sale or commercialization of wildlife or wild plant specimens obtained through hunting, fishing, or gathering.

**Ecosystem Restoration**

Refers to the improvement of a suite of functions of a system combined of living organisms and the non-living environment surrounding those organisms, as well as their interactions (interactions between living organisms and the non-living environment and interactions among those living organisms).

**Ecosystem Degradation**

Refers to negative impacts on a suite of functions of a system combined of living organisms and the non-living environment surrounding those organisms, as well as their interactions (interactions between living organisms and the non-living environment and interactions among those living organisms).

**Sustainable Production and Consumption**

Refers to the use of services and related products that respond to basic needs and bring a better quality of life by reducing the use of natural resources and toxic substances, as well as waste and pollutant emissions to a minimum level throughout the life cycle of the service or product so as not to pose risks to the needs of future generations.

**Strategic Environmental Assessment**

Refers to an approach involving analysis and participation for the purpose of integrating economic, social, environmental, and cultural considerations into policies, plans, and various programs.

**Hazardous Waste Management**

Refers to any activities conducted to protect, reduce, sort, collect, transport, store, treat, recycle, recover for reuse, and dispose of hazardous waste.

**Public Participation**

Refers to information dissemination, consultation, cooperation, participation, and decision-making in the environmental impact assessment process to exchange ideas, comments, information acceptance from all parties directly and indirectly affected and to find measures to reduce negative impacts from development projects in the pre-project implementation phase (project design and construction), the project implementation phase, and the project completion phase.

**Waste Sorting**

Refers to activities to separate waste by type at the source of waste generation, waste disposal sites, or at recycling or resource recovery sites.

**Commercial Uses**

Refers to the use of biodiversity or natural resources for the purpose of obtaining profit, economic benefits, or commercial trade.

**Open Burning**

Refers to the burning of waste, debris, or other objects in open fields, public places, or within private property boundaries, including burning in incinerators not equipped with devices for smoke emission control or air pollutant reduction.

**Extracting Resources from Waste**

Refers to activities to select or use waste to become tools, materials, or products that have value for reuse in new production, with the aim of reducing the amount of waste generated or waste that must be disposed of or discharged into the environment.

**Waste Disposal / Waste Discharge**

Refers to activities or processes of draining, emitting, or transporting waste from a source or site of waste generation into the environment or to other locations for any purpose.

**Greenhouse Gas Emission**

Refers to the emission of greenhouse gases and/or precursors and aerosols into the atmosphere in a certain area and over a certain period of time.

**Climate Change**

Refers to changes in the state of the climate that can be identified (through statistical analysis) by changes in the mean and variability of the climate, and that persists for an extended period, typically decades or longer.

**Technology Transfer**

Refers to the exchange of hard and soft knowledge, physical objects, and goods among stakeholders leading to the diffusion of technology for the prevention or mitigation of greenhouse gases and/or the dissemination of technology and cooperation in technology both domestically and internationally.

**Verification**

Refers to periodic assessment by independent institutions before and after the determination by the entity in charge of implementing greenhouse gas emission reductions from human activities tracked for greenhouse gas sources resulting from activities, Clean Development Mechanism projects which are registered or program-based activities (excluding forestry projects).

**Reforestation**

Refers to the planting of trees on land that was formerly forest but has since been converted to other uses.

**Measurement, Reporting and Verification**

Refers to a technical procedure to confirm greenhouse gas emissions and greenhouse gas emission reductions as material or financial resources used to determine climate finance.

**Payment for Environmental Pollution**

Refers to a financial measure to help reduce environmental pollution by requiring payment into the state budget for the discharge or storage of pollutants from production, business, or service sites into the environment.

**Environmental Pollution**

Refers to the introduction of pollutants from human activities or natural sources that can cause impacts on biodiversity, human health, animals, or affect production, business, service processes, and usage, changing the physical, chemical, or biological characteristics of the environment from its original natural state.

**Severity of Environmental Pollution**

Refers to environmental pollution that poses high-level risks to health or the lives of people, animals, ecosystems (or habitat systems), biodiversity, or public interests, including causing serious diseases, chronic diseases, or even death, and causing damage to environmental resources by causing water, soil, and air to contain high levels of toxins, leading to loss of biodiversity, loss of use of water and soil resources or crops, etc.

**Marine Pollution**

Refers to the introduction or discharge of waste, pollutants, petroleum products, chemical substances, etc., into the sea that can affect the quality of seawater or cause danger to the environment, biodiversity, and animals.

**Sustainable Development**

Refers to development that meets the needs of the present without compromising the ability of future generations to meet their own needs.

**Green Growth**

Refers to development that uses resources sustainably, including green environmental work and natural resources, water and sanitation, economy and investment, telecommunications and transportation, industry and energy, green tourism, food security and food safety, and land use.

**Ex-situ Conservation**

Refers to the conservation of components of biological diversity outside their natural habitats, such as the establishment of zoos, tree nurseries, etc.

**Biodiversity Conservation**

Refers to the maintenance and protection of the diversity of living organisms on land, in water, and in other ecosystems, including genetic diversity, species diversity, and ecosystem diversity.

**Genetic Resources Conservation**

Refers to the maintenance and protection of genetic diversity capable of reproduction in plants, animals, microorganisms, etc., including their values which are known or have potential, to ensure diversity in the present and future.

**Project Screening**

Refers to the process of identifying impacts, types and scales of project proposals that require a Full Environmental and Social Impact Assessment (ESIA) or an Initial Environmental and Social Impact Assessment (IESIA), or an Environmental Protection Contract.

**Fishing**

Refers to the attempt, research, or performance of activities using various means to attract, trap, injure, suffocate, kill, and collect fisheries yield within fishing domains.

**Risk Assessment**

Refers to the study and analysis of development projects or investment projects to identify hazards and risk factors from project activities on the environment, public safety, and human health, including natural disasters or security resources for development or investment projects, before determining appropriate means for managing, reducing, protecting, and eliminating hazards or risks that may occur.

**Pollutant Release**

Refers to the discharge, emission, or introduction of pollutants into water, soil, or air through direct or indirect human activities, intentional or unintentional, on a regular or occasional basis.

**Initial Environmental Impact Assessment**

Refers to the preliminary study of physical, biological, socio-economic, and cultural resources, largely relying on existing data within and around the project area, to serve as a basis for identifying, predicting, and analyzing potential environmental and social impacts that may occur due to project activities, in order to find measures to reduce negative impacts to the minimum level and increase positive impacts to the maximum level.

**Full Environmental Impact Assessment**

Refers to the detailed study of physical, biological, socio-economic, and cultural resources, largely relying on primary data within and around the project area, to serve as a basis for identifying, predicting, and analyzing potential serious environmental and social impacts that may occur due to project activities, in order to find measures to reduce negative impacts to the minimum level and increase positive impacts to the maximum level.

**Environmental Protection**

Refers to techniques and measures for the maintenance of the natural environment and reduction of negative impacts resulting from human activities.

**Game Hunting**

Refers to the hunting of wildlife for recreation within protected areas subject to specific principles and authorization from the Ministry or institution in charge of the environment and natural resources.

**Competent Institution or Ministry**

Refers to a Ministry or institution in charge of the legal management, leadership, and direction of any sector and various legal instruments.

**Ministries and Intitution responsible for electricity production, distribution, and management of use of electricity services**

Refers to institutions responsible for ensuring the provision of electricity services, such as the Electricity Authority of Cambodia, among others.

**Payment for waste water treatment service**

Refers to service fees to be paid for cleaning wastewater.

**Payment for waste water discharge service**

Refers to service fees to be paid for the maintenance, repair, restoration, and expansion of sewer manholes, sewer networks, open canals, reservoirs, and the operation of wastewater pumping stations.

**Program for International Management and Forest Product Certification**

Refers to the certification regime ensuring buyers that forest products have been assessed and certified as originating from forests managed according to social, economic, and environmental standards.

**Shifting Cultivation/ Traditional Practice Shifting Cultivation**

Refers to traditional farming practices of indigenous ethnic minorities by cutting and burning forests for cultivation, followed by a fallow period for soil quality recovery through natural reforestation. Crop cultivation is conducted on a rotational basis on that land.

### K J D T

**Environmental and Social Fund Committee**

Refers to a committee established by the Royal Government upon the request of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources and the Ministry of Economy and Finance for the purpose of managing the long-term fund.

**Forest Cover**

Refers to the natural physical environment, including wetlands, submerged lands, or dry lands, covered by naturally occurring or planted vegetation with a height of at least 5 meters on an area exceeding 0.5 hectares and having a level...

...canopy cover of more than 10%; tree plantations such as rubber, oil palm, acacia, teak, eucalyptus, and other wood-yielding plant species with characteristics as described above are also considered forest cover.

**Biodiversity or Biological Resources**

Refers to the potential collection of diversity of types within a single or different species and living organisms of all levels and sources, including terrestrial, marine, and freshwater ecosystems and ecological connection networks therein.

**Modern Biotechnology**

Refers to the application of:
A. In vitro nucleic acid techniques, including recombinant deoxyribonucleic acid and direct injection of nucleic acid into cells or organelles.
B. Fusion of cells beyond family lineages, which overcome natural physiological reproductive or recombination barriers and which are not techniques used in traditional breeding and selection.

**Wildlife Sanctuary**

Refers to an area of land or water that requires active management intervention to ensure the maintenance of habitats and/or to meet the specific requirements of any species of plants or animals.

**Biosphere Reserve**

Refers to areas representing important and relatively undisturbed ecosystems, surrounded by sustainable use zones that allow human activities within defined limits.

### K J N

**Basin Catchment**

Refers to the geographical area of a river basin where a water system flows into the river basin, including surface water and groundwater.

**Local Community Area**

Refers to an area serving the socio-economic development of the local community, which has existing activities including housing, rice fields, and plantations. The issuance of letters or titles for land possession or authorization to use land in this area must have prior approval from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

**Protected Landscape Area**

Refers to an area of land or water where the interaction of people and nature has produced an area of distinct character with significant natural beauty or ecological or cultural value, and which is often characterized by high biological diversity. Safeguarding the integrity of this traditional interaction is vital to the protection, maintenance, and evolution of such an area.

**Protected Area**

Refers to areas of state public property, including land or water, coastal areas, and seas located within regions recognized by laws or newly established areas within the jurisdiction of the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources. These areas are significant both physically and biologically, requiring management according to laws for the purpose of protecting and maintaining biodiversity, natural resources, and cultural resources, and are managed sustainably for all generations for environmental, social, and economic benefits.

**Natural Heritage Site**

Refers to terrestrial and/or aquatic areas having unique natural or semi-natural characteristics and outstanding value compared to others, due to their rare quality representing various natural systems, aesthetic quality, or cultural significance.

**Wetland**

Refers to areas including marshes, fens, peatlands, or areas of stagnant vegetation, whether natural or artificial, permanent or temporary, with water that is static or flowing, fresh, brackish, or salt, including areas of marine water the depth of which at low tide does not exceed 6 (six) meters.

**Multiple-Use Area**

Refers to terrestrial or aquatic areas rich in natural systems that have not yet been destroyed and require management to ensure long-term protection and maintenance of biodiversity and ecosystems. Simultaneously, they can provide natural products and services for the needs of communities.

**Humus Soil Area**

Refers to wetlands having a humus layer thicker than 40 cm resulting from the decomposition of plant parts in waterlogged and oxygen-deficient conditions, rich in organic carbon (more than 18 percent of dry mass).

**Core Zone**

Refers to areas of high value for the conservation of rare, endangered, and threatened species according to this content, and fragile ecosystems.

Access to this zone is prohibited, except for authorized management and conservation officers. Researchers in natural sciences for the purpose of protecting and conserving natural resources, biodiversity, and the environment must have prior permission from the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources.

**Conservation Zone**

Refers to areas adjacent to the core zone having value for conservation of natural resources, ecosystems, watershed areas, and areas with natural landscape characteristics.

Access to this zone requires prior permission from the local management and conservation administration. Use of natural resource by-products for the livelihoods of local communities and which does not significantly impact biodiversity must be subject to the strictest supervision.

**Sustainable Use Zone**

1- Refers to areas of high national economic value, serving the management and conservation of protected areas directly and contributing to promoting the livelihoods of local community members.

2- Sustainable Use Zones include:
A- Natural-Cultural Conservation Areas and Heritage Sites.
B- Ecotourism Areas.
C- Wildlife Conservation Areas and Recreational Services.
D- Biodiversity Resource Restoration Areas.
E- Protected Area Communities.
F- Botanical Gardens.
G- Infrastructure Development Areas, including irrigation systems and reservoirs.
H- Hydroelectricity, electric current poles.
I- Geographical Areas.
J- Mineral resource mining business areas favorable to the environment and natural resources within and around protected areas.

**Coastal Zone**

Refers to terrestrial areas adjacent to the sea and subject to marine influence, located in Kampot, Koh Kong, Preah Sihanouk, and Kep provinces, including marine integrity which is significant to the boundaries of the Exclusive Economic Zone of the Kingdom of Cambodia.

**Ramsar Site**

Refers to wetlands considered to be of international importance for ecology or biodiversity.

**Watershed Area**

Refers to mountainous or plateau areas or high-altitude areas, being rainwater catchment surfaces with geographical features located around or being watercourses connecting to rivers, river basins, or the sea. A watershed is an area of land where all water from rain and snow flows down to a specific body of water, such as a stream, river, lake, or wetland. A watershed is also called a rainwater collection area because the land located on the water divide collects all water, which then flows down.

**Nuclear Energy**

Refers to energy released from the nucleus of an atom through a reaction of fission or fusion.

Fission is a type of reaction occurring in the nucleus of an atom where a heavy nucleus (such as Uranium, etc.) is split into two or more lighter nuclei, called fission products, and releases energy in the form of electromagnetic radiation (gamma rays or X-rays) or particles (alpha, beta, or neutrons) with sufficient force to cause ionization when passing through any medium.

Fusion is a type of reaction occurring in the nucleus of an atom where two light nuclei (such as Hydrogen, etc.) are combined to form a heavier nucleus, with the release of a massive amount of energy.

**Responsible Energy**

Refers to principles of energy production and supply characterized by 6 (six) basic traits: sufficiency, sustainability, stability, quality, affordability, and availability throughout the Kingdom of Cambodia.

**Royalty**

Refers to the specified monetary amount to be paid to the state by any person for forest produce, by-products, wild plants, and genetic material specimens and wildlife collected or exploited.

**Database**

Refers to all information collected, compiled, and analyzed for use as required.

**Habitat**

Refers to the geographical location or site where any plant species naturally occurs as a sanctuary, feeding ground, and breeding ground for such animals and plants.

**Genetic Resources Protection and Conservation Area**

Refers to locations containing plant, animal, and microorganism components or other origins hosting functional units of heredity that have value for research, protection, and conservation.

**Eco-Tourism**

Refers to tourism services that do not adversely impact the natural environment surrounding tourism sites and maintain a sustainable outlook supporting conservation activities.

### D N P

**Responsible Energy Business**

Refers to businesses that have established responsible energy systems for their own electricity supply while being responsible for principles beyond "responsibility" themselves; subsequently, should they wish to connect to the public grid, they must comply with technical-economic and safety regulations set by the Ministry in charge of the energy sector.

**Natural Resources**

Refers to components of the natural environment, notably land, water, air, wind, plants, ecosystems, minerals, energy, petroleum and gas, stones and sand, mountains, forests and forest by-products, wildlife, fish, and marine resources.

**Genetic Resources**

Refers to the genetic diversity of genetic material of plants, animals, microorganisms, etc.

**Sustainability**

Refers to not causing permanent or persistent degradation or depletion of the quality of living organisms or of other resources under any form of utilization, both for the present and for future needs.

**Genetic Resource Proof**

Refers to evidence that verifies the source of genetic resources.

**Rare Species**

Refers to any species with small populations that are not yet endangered. In general, these species are found in specific habitats or are very sparsely scattered over a vast area.

**Endangered Species**

Refers to animals or plants that may become extinct due to natural environmental changes or direct human exploitation, habitat destruction, threats from other species, changes in food networks, pollution, or a combination of these factors.

**Environment-Friendly Technology**

Refers to technology aimed at protecting, monitoring, supervising, or reducing negative impacts on environmental technology and resource utilization.

**Cumulative Impact**

Refers to any impacts arising from the combined activities of existing projects and future projects located around those project areas, focusing on physical resources, biological resources, and socio-economic resources.

**Pollutant Substance(s) Movement**

Refers to the transfer of waste, pollutants, or hazardous substances out of the enterprise's premises or generation site for the purposes of utilization, recycling, resource recovery, treatment, or disposal, including the discharge of wastewater or liquid waste into public wastewater treatment systems.

**Environmentally Friendly Standard**

Refers to technical standards for defining criteria and indicators to be used for evaluating environment-friendly performance activities.

**Environmental Trust**

Refers to the management and allocation of resources provided by a trustor for environmental benefits to a party, in compliance with the trust deed or prevailing regulations.

**Monitoring Equipment**

Refers to instruments or machinery equipped to measure and record data on pollutant concentrations or quantities of waste discharged into the environment.

**Transboundary Environment**

Refers to environmental management as international cooperation between countries where environmental pollution impacts affect economic and social development, such as air pollution, haze, water pollution, etc.

**Person**

Refers to a natural person or a legal entity.

**Premiums**

Refers to the amount of money that a licensee or permit holder must pay in addition to the royalty (for forest products and by-products).

**Eco-system**

Refers to the totality of diverse interactions within a specific land area among living organisms (plant communities, animals, microorganisms) and the non-living environment (climate, micro-climate, soil, water) that establish environmental conditions and constitute a functional unit on the Earth's crust. Humans and the planet's resources, along with economic, traditional, cultural, and environmental needs, are also a diverse part of forest environment systems.

**Waste Treatment**

Refers to activities or purification sequences to remove or destroy chemical pollutants, hazardous substances, or pathogens present in waste through physical, chemical, or biological means.

**Waste Operator**

Refers to a natural or legal person that:
- Provides services for collection, transportation, treatment, incineration, or final disposal of waste.
- Conducts business in purchasing, selling, recycling, or recovering resources from waste.

**Point Source(s)**

Refers to stationary locations, including factories, enterprises, production sites, business sites, or service sites that generate and discharge liquid waste, pollutants, or wastewater into public sewer networks or public water bodies.

**Air Pollution Sources**

Refers to locations, places, and means of transportation that generate and emit waste, pollutants, odors, smoke, heat, and disturbances from noise and vibration into the atmosphere.

**Mobile Air Pollution Sources**

Refers to pollution sources consisting of vehicles that emit smoke, gases, air pollutants, odors, heat, and disturbances from noise and vibration into the atmosphere.

**Constant Air Pollution Sources**

Refers to pollution sources with stationary locations, including factories, enterprises, handicrafts, farms, warehouses, construction sites, and waste incinerators that emit smoke, gases, air pollutants, odors, heat, and disturbances from noise and vibration into the atmosphere.

**Non-Point Source(s)**

Refers to pollution sources with widespread areas, including cities, rice fields, and plantations from which liquid waste, pollutants, or wastewater overflows during rainfall events.

**Threatened Species**

Refers to animal and plant species listed as categories of threatened species heading toward extinction, such as critically endangered, endangered, or vulnerable species, as listed in the Red List of threatened species.

**Endangered Species**

Refers to animal and/or plant species that are considered to be facing an extremely high risk of extinction in the wild based on the best available evidence.

**Critically Endangered Species**

Refers to any animal or plant species in nature whose habitat index has decreased rapidly over the last 10 (ten) years or within 3 (three) generations in nature, and species of scientific importance as well as high economic value. The names of wildlife and plant species in the critically endangered group are defined in the Red List of threatened species.

**Native Species**

Refers to any animal or plant species that originated, formerly lived, or continues to live in any part of its distribution or regular migration within the geography of Cambodia, and is not the result of a new introduction, whether intentional or unintentional.

**Alien Species**

Refers to any animal and/or plant species that did not originally originate in Cambodia, including wildlife living in sanctuaries as well as parts of wildlife and plants that have been collected.

**Least Concerned Species**

Refers to animal and plant species classified by the International Union for Conservation of Nature that have been evaluated as species not subject to conservation threats.

**Alien Invasive Species**

Refers to plants, animals, pathogens, and other microorganisms that did not originate in the Kingdom of Cambodia and can cause damage to the economy or environment or have negative impacts on human health. Furthermore, alien invasive species have negative impacts on biodiversity, including the extinction or complete elimination of native species or the transmission of pathogens and the disturbance of ecosystems and native ecosystem functions.

**Resource Efficiency**

Refers to the use of resources such as budget, materials, environmental resources, human resources, or other resources to perform functions efficiently through the use of fewer resources, waste reduction, and economic savings aimed at maintaining environmental sustainability.

### P P P

**Forest Products**

Refers to any plants obtained from forests for direct use or commercial use, including forest produce, forest by-products, animal feed, etc.

**Environmental Management Plan**

Refers to the plans specified in Environmental and Social Impact Assessment reports that define protection and mitigation measures, responsibilities, and schedules for implementation, monitoring, and evaluating environmental impacts from projects, including financial plans for environmental activities during the construction, operation, and project completion phases.

**Wildlife Rescue Center**

Refers to a site aimed at:
- Rehabilitation (treatment) of injured wildlife and releasing such wildlife back into the wild.
- Receiving confiscated wildlife and caring for them until they can be returned to their original habitat or released.
- Providing long-term care for wildlife that cannot be returned to the wild.
- Implementing captive breeding for conservation purposes.

**Ecosystem functioning**

Refers to the establishment of processes defined by various interactions among biological communities, both native and alien species, and biophysical processes such as water regulation and the regeneration of soil and nutrients.

**Environmental and Social Fund**

Refers to a fund received from environmental protection fees, development partners, charitable donors, and investment project owners who have applied to the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources to study and assess the environmental impacts of proposed projects and other values.

**Environmentally Friendly**

Refers to the implementation of laws, production, services, and technology that support environmental protection, biodiversity conservation, natural resource management, and sustainable living.

**Waste Owner**

Refers to a natural or legal person that generates waste from production, business, or service activities, including daily living activities.

**Sub-national Administration**

Refers to the administration of the capital, provinces, municipalities, districts, khans, communes, and sangkats in charge of managing any task legally according to the Law on Administrative Management of the Capital, Provinces, Municipalities, Districts, and Khans and the Law on Administrative Management of Communes and Sangkats.

**Wild flora**

Refers to plant species that are not planted, including their growth conditions, occurring within ecosystems and natural habitats with no or limited human influence on their occurrence and reproduction.

### L V S

**Internation Instrument**

Refers to treaties, conventions, protocols, regional or international agreements, or other international laws.

**Emission Permit**

Refers to the setting of greenhouse gas emission levels (non-transferable, yet tradable) by competent authorities such as government institutions, central or local institutions, to regional entities (international frameworks, countries, national level, sub-national level) or sectorial entities (companies) for greenhouse gas emissions in specific quantities.

**Environmental flow**

Refers to the quantity, timing, and quality of water flows needed to sustain ecosystems, livelihoods, and the well-being of living organisms that depend on those ecosystems.

**Procurement**

Refers to the process of bidding until the acquisition of goods, construction, services, or consultancy services.

**Certificate on Environmental Protection Contract and Social Impact Assessment Report**

Refers to the certificate issued by the Ministry or institution in charge of the environment and natural resources based on the environmental assessment of a project, as specified in the Environmental Management Plan or Initial Environmental and Social Impact Assessment report or Full Environmental and Social Impact Assessment report.

**Radioactive**

Refers to elements that emit high energy in the form of electromagnetic radiation (gamma rays or X-rays) or particles (alpha, beta, or neutrons) with sufficient energy to cause ionization when passing through any medium.

**Local Community**

Refers to communities of indigenous ethnic minorities or non-indigenous ethnic minorities living in specific locations who have established or organized, or have not yet organized, as protected area communities, forest communities, or other communities defined by law.

**Community Protected Area**

Refers to local communities with an elected administrative structure recognized by the management and conservation administration for the purpose of participating in the organization, management, and sustainable use of natural resources in specific areas of protected areas, aimed at promoting the livelihoods of local community members.

**Ecotourism Community**

Refers to a local community that is the owner and primary manager of ecotourism infrastructure and activities, where such tourism activities must operate based on direct benefit sharing with the community.

**Toxic Chemical(s)**

Refers to chemicals or groups of chemicals that can cause poisoning to humans and animals or cause danger to the environment and biodiversity when present in the environment.

**Hazardous Substance(s)**

Refers to chemicals or groups of chemicals that cause acute or chronic poisoning, cause danger, cause prenatal impacts, and are resistant to degradation in the environment, capable of entering the food cycle and bioaccumulating in human and animal organisms and biodiversity.

**Pollutant(s) / Pollutant Substance(s)**

Refers to chemicals, groups of chemicals, waste, smoke, gases, odors, dust, or powders that enter the environment through human activities such as discharge, leakage, or through natural activities, causing environmental quality degradation or potential danger to the environment, biodiversity, humans, and animals.

**Validation**

Refers to the process of independent evaluation of project activities under the Clean Development Mechanism or programmatic activities by an operational entity through verifying plans and requirements of the Clean Development Mechanism against requirements for project design document preparation or detailed program activity documents and specific project activity documents.

**Botanic Garden**

Refers to plant gardens planted for scientific research and public display, or gardens that collect, cultivate, conserve, and display worldwide plant species with labels showing their names and origins.

**Electricity Services**

Refers to the production, transmission, and distribution of electricity, as well as additional or auxiliary services connected to these activities.

**Hazardous Waste**

Refers to waste that is acutely or chronically hazardous to human health, animals, or the environment, or causes property damage, including flammability, explosivity, reactivity, corrosivity, and infectiousness, which requires management separate from normal waste.

**Environmentally Friendly Label(s)**

Refers to logos representing certificates of participation in environment-friendly practices issued by the Ministry in charge of the environment and natural resources for attachment onto various products.

**Self-Monitoring (Waste)**

Refers to the activities of monitoring discharges into the environment implemented by the owner or person in charge of a pollution source through the installation of automatic monitoring equipment or data management systems, among others.

### H A Kh

**Green Finance**

Refers to financial investment into development projects and/or sustainable initiatives for environmental products and policies that incentivize sustainable development.

**Environmental Impact**

Refers to significant negative and positive impacts on physical resources, biological resources, and socio-economic resources caused by project activities either directly or indirectly.

**Green Building**

Refers to structures or buildings that are sustainable in both structure and process implementation through environmental and resource responsibility, having efficiency throughout the building's life cycle from water and electricity use through savings, design planning, construction, maintenance, repair, and demolition.

**Natural Resource By-products**

Refers to products obtained from protected areas including forest by-products, such as vines, resin, tubers, leaves, flowers or plant fruits, medicinal plants, grasses, bamboo, rattan, and bamboo shoots for people's livelihoods.

**Competent Authority**

Refers to sub-national administrations having authority to manage land or lead, manage, and arrange tasks within specific sectors in compliance with prevailing laws and regulations.

**National Park**

Refers to natural areas of land and/or water defined for:
A- Protecting one or more ecosystems of the defined area for the benefit of all generations.
B- Restricting uses that may damage or destroy biodiversity resources, natural resources, cultural resources, and the functions of that area in alignment with the zoning objectives.
C- Providing a basis for recreation, visits, education, scientific research, and beliefs, provided that all these activities do not pose a threat to the local natural environment and culture.

**National Marine Park**

Refers to coastal areas influenced by sea tides mixed with water from mountain streams, including islands covered by forests, plants, wildlife, and all types of natural resources having historical significance and cultural value, which are recognized by law and managed effectively.

**Greenhouse Gas**

1- Refers to gases in the atmosphere, occurring naturally or by human activity, which absorb and emit radiation at specific wavelengths within the range of infrared radiation emitted by the Earth's surface, atmosphere, and clouds. These elements cause the greenhouse effect.

2- Water vapor, carbon dioxide, nitrous oxide, methane, and ozone are the primary greenhouse gases in the Earth's atmosphere. Furthermore, there are an additional number of greenhouse gases in the atmosphere created entirely by humans, such as halocarbons and other substances containing chlorine and bromine, which are under the control of the Montreal Protocol. Besides carbon dioxide, nitrous oxide, and methane, the Kyoto Protocol manages other greenhouse gases such as sulfur hexafluoride and perfluorocarbons.

**Extractive Industry**

Refers to oil, gas, mineral, and metal industries.

**C. Approval of the Draft Environment and Natural Resources Code**

- The draft Environment and Natural Resources Code was approved by the Council of Ministers under the high presidency of **Samdech Akka Moha Sena Padei Techo Hun Sen, Prime Minister of the Kingdom of Cambodia** on 28 April 2023.

- This draft Code was approved by the National Assembly on 30 May 2023 at the 9th Session of the 6th Legislature, and was reviewed and completely adopted in its entire form and legal substance without amendment on 13 June 2023 at the 10th Plenary Session of the 4th Legislature of the Senate.

- The Environment and Natural Resources Code was promulgated by Royal Decree No. NS/RKM/0623/007 dated 29 June 2023.
